# FILE: `companies/AVIENCE.md`

Source: `run/avience-2026-09-26`

# AVIENCE — Avience Biomedicals Ltd (company memory)

Memory to weigh, never anchored evidence. Every number a stage reports comes
from that run's own inputs, verified against the source PDFs.

## THESIS (one line)
Not yet formed. Step-1 intake working question: can a 72%-traded Mindray
channel partner move toward ~50% in-house IVD reagents on closed-loop placed
instruments once the YEIDA plant runs from Oct-2026, and does the ~Rs 47 Cr
Uttarakhand order convert to the guided >=60% FY27 growth without the cash
cycle (debtor 146 days, inventory 218 days) eating it?

## DECISION STATUS AND ENTRY ZONE
None. Step-1 intake only (2026-09-26). No valuation has run.

## SPEAR
Spear: OVERRIDE 2026-09-26 (operator standing ruling 2026-09-05: Step-1 intake replaces the web spear)

Load-bearing facts (first verification priority for every stage), from
runs/avience-2026-09-26/step1-business-brief.md:
1. GUIDANCE VS DELIVERY. FY27 >=+60% to >Rs 100 Cr on a ~Rs 47 Cr
   Uttarakhand order; FY28 Rs 160-165 Cr; YEIDA production from Oct-2026;
   manufacturing mix ~27% to ~50%. No Reg 30 order filing seen since listing.
2. FY26 FIGURES ON A MOVING BASE. FY26 consolidated results filed
   17-Jul-2026, revised consolidated results approved 25-Aug-2026; FY25
   revenue on three bases (RHP Rs 45.97 Cr, call Rs 45.24 Cr, screener Rs
   44.92 Cr); standalone vs subsidiary (DR Meditech) split.
3. CONCENTRATION AND RELATED PARTIES. Top supplier 63-73% of purchases
   (Mindray, regional channel partner); top 10 customers >50%;
   subsidiary sales Rs 4.89 Cr and related-party advance Rs 3.38 Cr (FY26 AR).
4. CASH CONVERSION AND GOVERNANCE. CFO/OP 58%, FCF negative three years,
   possible interest capitalisation on YEIDA CWIP; auditor Haribhakti &
   Co. LLP replaced by M.A.M and Associates at the 30-Sep-2026 AGM (term
   end); borrowing powers to Rs 100 Cr.

Brief: runs/avience-2026-09-26/step1-business-brief.md

## HALT 1 GATE
- Mental Model signed: NOT SIGNED
- Halt 1 decision (KILL/SHALLOW/PROCEED): pending (phase 1 dossier ready 2026-09-26: CORPUS GAPPED, fragility FRAGILE, gate PROCEED WITH FLAGS)

## OPERATOR RULINGS
- 2026-09-26: Step-1 intake run; peers auto-selected (QLINE, MOLBIO,
  TARSONS; Hemant Surgical rejected for want of transcripts); empty folders
  accepted as gaps.

## ACTIVE TRIPWIRES
None yet (no signed model).

## OPTIONALITY REGISTER (summary)

```yaml
  - {risk: "Manufacturing:trading mix shift stalls", first_signal: "EBITDA margin flattening or falling despite revenue growth"}
  - {risk: "YEIDA plant ramps slower than guided", first_signal: "depreciation/finance cost rising ahead of matching revenue"}
  - {risk: "Cash conversion stays weak", first_signal: "operating cash flow vs reported PAT (CFO/PAT ratio)"}
  - {risk: "Receivables ageing continues to deteriorate", first_signal: "trade receivables >6-month aged bucket"}
  - {risk: "Placed-instrument base outgrows verification/collection discipline", first_signal: "CARO qualification language in next audit report; aged-receivable provisioning coverage"}
  - {risk: "Auditor transition coincides with ramp-year restatement risk", first_signal: "any restated/revised results filing"}
mgmt_questions:
  - "Average reagent revenue per placed instrument, and how many instruments are currently placed under active rental contracts?"
  - "Is the ~Rs 47 Cr Uttarakhand order confirmed in writing, and why no Reg 30 order-win filing since listing?"
  - "Reagent-rental margin vs manufacturing margin, quarter by quarter, as manufacturing mix rises?"
  - "Exact rupee amount of the CARO clause (ii)(b) bank stock-statement discrepancy, and has it recurred in H1 FY27?"
  - "How much of FY26's finished-goods inventory build (+80.9%) has sold through in H1 FY27?"
  - "Is the Mindray exclusivity/OEM private-label discussion moving to a signed term sheet, and on what timeline?"
  - "Expected FY27 collection period on government-linked orders, and is the Rs 35-40 Cr working-capital requirement at Rs 100 Cr revenue already arranged?"
one_line_verdict: "A small, growing IVD hybrid whose margin story depends on a mix shift not yet proven, funded so far by working capital and capex, not cash."
analyst_note: "No CLAUDE.md archetype library entry cleanly names this business: it is a distribution/agency relationship (Avience as regional Mindray channel partner, non-exclusive) with an embedded razor-and-blade reagent-rental model (free-placed instruments, 5-year locked-in consumables) layered on top of a smaller CDSCO-licensed manufacturing operation. The library's 'Outsourcing partner' archetype describes client concentration from the service-provider side; here Avience depends on Mindray as its PRINCIPAL/supplier, the reverse relationship, so that archetype only partially fits. Flagging this as a genuine library gap for Role 2 rather than forcing a fit. Separately, the sector-cap row question B00 already raised (Pharma/CDMO 38x vs a Distribution/trading 20x SOTP slice for the 72% traded revenue) is the single highest-leverage unresolved question for Stage 11: it alone could swing the destination multiple by roughly half, and Amendment 27.4's 'pure buy-and-sell' boundary text sits in real tension with the traded slice's AMC/CMC service layer and reagent-rental placement structure. Both flags need an explicit operator ruling at /fttcp, not a stage-11 default."
```

```yaml
analyst_note: "Tarsons across its four transcripts is the single most load-bearing peer in this run: its explicit 15-20%/30-35% year-1/year-2 utilisation curve makes Avience's own YEIDA guidance look sector-plausible in shape, but its own commissioning date slipped twice in nine months on a plant that was already substantially built, which is direct, evidenced grounds to discount the reliability of Avience's Oct-2026 date independent of the eventual utilisation percentage. No peer touches the Uttarakhand order at all; it remains as unverified after this stage as it was entering it. The Part 5 hypothesis (peers disclose per-unit reagent/instrument economics as standard practice at this business-model stage; Avience discloses none) is a concrete, testable management question, not yet asked on any transcript in this corpus."
```

```yaml
    demand_link: "Government incentive directly tied to Avience's own incremental-manufacturing-sales strategy"
    likely_source: "Department of Pharmaceuticals (Ministry of Chemicals and Fertilizers) scheme disclosures"
    cadence: "event-driven"
    shared: false
demand_externally_verifiable: true
analyst_note: "Central finding: management's FY26-FY28 guided CAGR (75.9%) outruns the evidence-based aggressive-case SOM3yr CAGR (43.4%) over a longer window, most likely because the ~Rs 47 Cr Uttarakhand order is a lumpy, largely one-off government contract, not a repeatable share gain. Stage 11 should carry the SOM-implied CAGR (43.4% yr3 / 38.7% yr5) as the cross-check and treat guidance growth as order-driven pending a second comparable win. SAM rests on two named judgment calls (32% North-India geography proxy from a diagnostics-SERVICES market share, and a 50% channel/capability discount) rather than filed percentages; both are disclosed in-line rather than smoothed into the headline numbers."
```

<a id="runs-avience-2026-09-26-outputs-final-business-narrative-md"></a>

# FILE: `runs/avience-2026-09-26/outputs/final/business-narrative.md`

Source: `run/avience-2026-09-26`

# Business narrative: Avience Biomedicals (AVIENCE)

Phase 1 lite. Evidence stages only. This file carries no valuation, no entry zone, no decision and no destination multiple. Those resolve after Stage 11.

## Business understanding narrative

Avience sells the chemicals, test kits and machines that hospitals and labs use to test blood and other samples, a field called in vitro diagnostics. Most of what it sells is not its own. In FY26, 60% of turnover came from reagents and consumables bought from Mindray, a Chinese diagnostics maker, and resold as Mindray's regional channel partner for Delhi NCR and eastern UP. Another 13% came from Mindray instruments. Its own Noida factory makes reagents and consumables (15% of turnover), rapid test cards for pregnancy, dengue and HIV (9%), and a few analysers and oxygen concentrators (3%), under 88 CDSCO product licences. A lab cannot run a blood analyser without the matching reagents, and for 80 to 90% of the reagent business the machine takes only the reagents it was placed with. So Avience gives away 80 to 85% of its instruments free on five year contracts and earns the money back on the reagents each machine consumes.

The buyers are hospitals, private labs and diagnostic centres (45% of 10 month FY26 standalone sales), other businesses and distributors (45%), government (8%) and export (2%). Named accounts include Max Healthcare, Sarvodaya Hospital and Dr Lal PathLabs. The ten largest customers take more than half of revenue and are not named. A lab that signs a placement contract buys that machine's reagents at agreed prices for five years, so switching is costly inside the term and open again at renewal. Government buyers pay slowly, 90 to 120 days on large projects by management's own account.

Present demand comes from a rising load of tests for diabetes, TB, heart disease and cancer, wider health insurance, and free diagnostics under the National Health Mission. Two signals govern it for Avience. Mindray territory and exclusivity decisions for North India decide the traded 72% of sales. State government diagnostic reagent tender awards in UP, Uttarakhand and new states decide the order led growth, and the unfiled Rs 47 Cr Uttarakhand order is the current test.

Forward growth has four external signals. CDSCO product licence grants toward the 175 product FY27 target gate how many products the factory can sell, and they show on the CDSCO SUGAM portal. YEIDA plant commissioning and medical device park incentive notifications gate the new capacity from October 2026. The PLI Scheme for Pharmaceuticals covers IVD devices, but Avience is not among the five selected applicants. GST Council and CBIC notifications on diagnostic kit rates set a live price variable for the whole range. The conservative market sizing grows the Indian IVD market about 6.5% a year.

The advantage differs by line. The placed instrument base with its locked reagent stream carries a moderate switching cost, bounded by the five year contract term. The 88 licences are a moderate barrier to a new entrant but do not stop Roche, Abbott or Transasia, who clear the same process at far larger scale. The traded Mindray line has no moat of its own: the arrangement is non exclusive and Mindray can reallocate the territory. Rapid cards have no moat, since many licensed makers sell the same tests. The emerging moat scan finds no new moat forming, with a score of about 9 to 10 against a threshold of 12 for modest development. Only three categories reach moderate strength: backward integration, where China's share of purchases fell from 31% to 3%; the incremental reagent rental lock in from the new plant; and a regulatory tailwind that other medical device parks share equally. The Family I items scored zero.

## Mindray reseller bets on own reagents

Avience listed on NSE Emerge in June 2026 at Rs 208 a share and raised Rs 30 Cr in fresh money. FY26 revenue was Rs 52.5 Cr, up 16%. PAT was Rs 8.75 Cr, up 23%. EBITDA margin was about 28 to 29%. Promoters hold 64.6% with nothing pledged, and the fall from 87.9% before the IPO is dilution from new shares, not selling.

The company wants to become a maker, not a reseller. Management guides the manufactured share of turnover from 27% to about 50%, a new YEIDA plant in production from October 2026, FY27 revenue up at least 60% to above Rs 100 Cr, and Rs 160 to 165 Cr in FY28. A Rs 47 Cr Uttarakhand order "in hand" carries about half of the FY27 target. Three facts cut against the guidance. No exchange filing, customer name or tender number confirms the order in 18 filings since listing. The second half of FY26 grew revenue only 5% on the year and fell 4% against the first half, while second half PAT fell 1%. A 50% manufactured share at Rs 100 Cr needs about Rs 50 Cr of own product against Rs 14 Cr in FY26, from a plant that starts at 15 to 20% use, while the order is heavy on equipment and the high end machines are Mindray's. On that arithmetic the manufactured share more likely lands near 33 to 40% unless the order is for Avience's own goods, which is an inference, not a filed fact.

The quality screen scores Avience GOOD, 87 of 160. Returns are high: ROCE was 31.5% in FY26 and 34.1% in FY25. Revenue compounded 48% a year and PAT 101% a year from FY24 to FY26 on a small base. The cap comes from cash. The cash generation block scores 2 of 20, and that deal breaker alone holds the grade at GOOD. The screen also counts four moats, which the business model read finds thinner and bounded by contract term.

Profit has not turned into cash. Operating cash flow covered 48% of PAT in FY25 and 68% in FY26. Free cash flow was negative all three years, about minus Rs 3 Cr, minus Rs 6 Cr and minus Rs 7.5 Cr, as the YEIDA plant took Rs 12.9 Cr of work in progress with nothing yet capitalised. The working capital side is the harder part. Receivables older than six months rose from 10% to 39% of the standalone book in one year, while the provision against them fell from 15.8% to 8.3% of that aged book. Finished goods stock rose 81% while revenue rose 16 to 22%. Debt stands at Rs 28.9 Cr, 1.7 times EBITDA. The IPO plan assumed receivables would fall to 80 days in FY27; on the call management cited 90 to 120 days on government work and conceded the full working capital need is not yet funded. No credit rating exists, so no outside view of working capital is available.

Management grades C on credibility, the default for a company nine weeks past its first ever call. No guidance has reached a test date. The first one is the H1 FY27 result due by November 2026. One promise is in progress: the plant building slipped about three months against the IPO schedule, while the October 2026 production date holds. The FY27 export target of Rs 5 to 7 Cr sits on a FY26 base of Rs 0.53 Cr, after exports fell 90% from Rs 5.24 Cr, and nobody on the call raised it. The first annual report as a listed company carries three reporting slips: profit split labels swapped between parent and subsidiary, a Board's Report line saying the audit carries no qualification when the auditor's own table lists one, and consolidated results revised in August 2026 for an elimination error. The non audit fee exceeds the audit fee in both years. The promoter review lands at CAUTION: clean regulatory and criminal record, zero pledge, an outside finance chief and company secretary, but a group company in the same business outside the non compete, a new Rs 5 Cr guarantee for the subsidiary's bank line, and an incoming auditor with no findable track record.

Peers complicate the story. Tarsons guided a 15 to 20% first year and 30 to 35% second year ramp on its new plant, the same shape Avience guides, and then slipped its own commissioning date twice in nine months. Tarsons also reports that government buying on GeM at the lowest bid has eroded its government lab revenue over five to seven years. Q-Line, the nearest mirror, earns 15 to 20% gross margin on instruments it makes and 10 to 15% on instruments it trades, which undercuts the higher margin management claims for the equipment heavy order. Molbio collects in about 87 days and confirms the closed system reagent model. All three peers guide FY27 growth of 15 to 35%, the same direction as Avience but well short of 60%. No peer confirms or contradicts the Uttarakhand order.

The market sizing puts the Indian IVD market at about Rs 12,100 to 12,800 Cr and the part Avience can serve at about Rs 1,900 Cr. Avience holds 2.8% of that served market, so headroom is about 36 times current revenue and the runway grades STRONG. An evidence based three year path reaches Rs 155 Cr, about 43% growth a year, and Rs 270 Cr in five years. Management's guidance to FY28 implies 76% a year, well ahead of that path. The gap most likely reflects a lumpy, one off government order rather than repeatable share gain, so FY27 growth should be read as order driven until a second order of similar size appears. Plant capacity of Rs 250 to 265 Cr of revenue does not bind; the constraint is the mix shift and unfunded working capital.

---

<a id="runs-avience-2026-09-26-outputs-final-gate-recommendation-md"></a>

# FILE: `runs/avience-2026-09-26/outputs/final/gate-recommendation.md`

Source: `run/avience-2026-09-26`

# Gate recommendation: Avience Biomedicals (AVIENCE)

**PROCEED WITH FLAGS**

Phase 1 lite scope. This is the FTTCP go or no go on evidence alone. It carries no valuation. No BUY, WATCHLIST or AVOID call, no entry range, no margin of safety price, no destination PE, and no Hurdle verdict appear here. Those resolve after Stage 11.

## Verdict reasoning

Run date 2026-09-26. CMP Rs 342. Market value about Rs 188 Cr (manifest). NSE Emerge listing 25 Jun 2026.

Verdict rule applied: rule 3 (active FLAG-CASH), capped by the INDETERMINATE cash rule, then moved one level by the rule 4 confidence band.

The chain:
- REWORK (rule 1): not triggered. Verifier A holds zero CRITICAL and zero source fidelity findings. No acceptance rate on the counted basis sits below 60 (A 100, B 71, C 87.7, D 100). Overall 71 clears the 60 floor.
- INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE (rule 2): not triggered. Stages 2 and 3 ran on the FY26 annual report. Gate 0 ran on screener data and filed results. The gaps (no credit rating, one own transcript, no filing for the Uttarakhand order, H1 FY27 not yet published) are partial and named below. They leave the decision relevant record intact, so they cap at CAVEATS rather than halting the verdict.
- FRESHNESS: the freshness verdict is FRESHNESS PAIRS OK. No freshness cap applies.
- FLAG-PROMOTER: not active. The promoter verdict is CAUTION, below the CONCERN trigger. Its findings are carried as caveats below.
- FLAG-CASH: active, determination INDETERMINATE (block below). An active flag places the verdict at PROCEED WITH FLAGS by rule 3. INDETERMINATE cash caps that at PROCEED WITH CAVEATS instead.
- CONFIDENCE: overall 71 sits in the 60 to 74 band, which moves a PROCEED family verdict one level toward caution. It takes the cash cap of PROCEED WITH CAVEATS one step down to PROCEED WITH FLAGS.
- Net verdict: PROCEED WITH FLAGS.

Operator note for Halt 1: Verifier B's strict caught only rate is 28.6% (2 of 7 material items). The rule of record counts partial catches as caught and gives 71%. The strict rate is recorded here, not applied as REWORK.

## Transition posture

Not classifiable in phase 1. The Mental Model is not signed, so the ugliness reading (artifact of climb or structural feature) has no signed value. The recognition gap resolves at Stage 11. The proof gate reads NOT FIRED on present evidence: the YEIDA plant has not started production, the manufactured share is still 27%, and the order behind the FY27 guidance is unfiled. On the matrix, a not fired gate leads to RESEARCH / WATCH if the weak cash optic is an artifact and the gap is open, to PRICED NARRATIVE (TRAP) if it is an artifact and the gap is closed, and to VALUE-TRAP RISK if it is structural. The FLAG-CASH resolution below is the observation that decides which row applies.

## Confidence delta

| Component | Score | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Numerical acceptance (B12a) | 100 | 35 of 35 material figures clean; coverage is Gate 0 figures plus three B02/B03 spot checks |
| Red flag coverage (B12b) | 71 | 5 of 7 material flags had (2 caught plus 3 partial); strict caught only 28.6% |
| Framework adherence (B12c) | 87.7 | Phase 1 half only: 57 of 65 Gate 0 and EM rules passed; valuation half pending phase 3 |
| Peer utilisation (B12d) | 100 | 6 of 6 peer transcripts substantive and supported; anchor drift 2 MAJOR plus 6 MINOR |
| **Overall** | **71** | set by red flag coverage; band 60 to 74 |

Weakest component: red flag coverage at 71. The concall stage missed the H2 FY26 slowdown and did not test the mix arithmetic behind the FY27 guidance. Both gaps are carried into the narrative and the monitorables here.

Binding verifier corrections carried into this file: the new capacity embedded growth figure is 190%, not 90% (99.6 / 52.51), and must be corrected before phase 3 reads it. The emerging moat score is 9.3 to 10.0, not 11.4, classification NONE unchanged, and the "borderline" framing is dropped.

## Active flag blocks

### FLAG-CASH

Determination: **INDETERMINATE**.

Cited evidence:
- Consolidated CFO to PAT was 0.478x in FY25 and 0.675x in FY26. Free cash flow was minus Rs 590.42 lakh in FY25 and minus Rs 754.24 lakh in FY26 (B03 flags; AR cash flow statement p.120). Cumulative FY24 to FY26 CFO to PAT is 0.573 and FCF was negative all three years (B01 block_b_trend).
- Receivables older than six months rose from 10.2% to 38.9% of the standalone book (18.4% to 38.2% consolidated). The six month to one year bucket rose from Rs 35.88 lakh to Rs 320.54 lakh. Provision coverage on the aged book fell from 15.8% to 8.3%. Management's own ratio note blames "extended credit cycles for certain customers" (B02 flags and top_findings rank 1 and 6; AR Note 18/42 standalone p.97, p.106; Note 20 consolidated p.135).
- Receivables composition: Rs 51.77 lakh of related party trade receivables (Ayush Diagnostics, Imperative Healthcare, Biocorn Healthcare) sit only in the consolidated note; the standalone note denies any. No split of the aged book by government, private or related party customer is disclosed (B02 top_findings rank 9; AR Note 20.1/36 consolidated p.135, p.141 to 142).
- Finished goods inventory rose 80.9% against revenue growth of 16.1% consolidated and 22.2% standalone (B02 flags; AR Note 17 standalone p.96, Note 19 consolidated p.134). Part of FY26 PBT growth is an unsold inventory build, a P&L inventory change credit of Rs 167.87 lakh standalone (B03 flags; AR p.82).
- Capex commissioning timeline: YEIDA CWIP rose from Rs 461.91 lakh to Rs 1,285.28 lakh with nothing capitalised to PPE. The IPO schedule set production for Oct 2026; the building milestone slipped about three months to Sep 2026. SIDBI loan repayment has already begun and no interest line appears in finance cost, which points to full capitalisation of borrowing cost, amount not disclosed (B05 promise_delivery; B02 top_findings rank 7 and 12; AR Note 13 p.95, Note 28 p.100; RHP pdf p.130 to 131; transcript p.3).
- Working capital plan versus guidance: the RHP plan assumed FY27 receivable days of 80, down from 128. On the call management cited 90 to 120 days on large government projects and a Rs 35 to 40 Cr working capital need at Rs 100 Cr revenue that is "not available today" (B12b findings; RHP pdf p.125 to 126; transcript p.6).
- The Q4 FY26 bank stock statements did not agree with the books; the auditor lists CARO clause (ii)(b) against the holding company with no rupee figure (B02 top_findings rank 13; AR Note 47 p.108; Annexure 1 p.115 to 116).

Rating agency verbatim quote: NOT AVAILABLE. Avience is an unrated issuer. The RHP states no rating was required for the issue (RHP pdf p.76), and no rating on bank facilities was found (B00 input_gaps).

Why INDETERMINATE: two readings fit the record. The GROWTH-INDUCED reading says the free cash outflow is the YEIDA plant build, the reagent rental model ties up stock and placed instruments ahead of revenue, and CFO to PAT improved from 0.478x to 0.675x. The STRUCTURAL reading says the aged receivables quadrupled as a share of the book, provisions fell, and finished goods jumped 81%, all during a half year in which revenue grew only 5%. Receivables building against slowing sales are not growth funding. The record cannot separate the two because the aged book has no customer split and no rating agency working capital view exists.

The one observation that separates them: the H1 FY27 half yearly result (due by Nov 2026). GROWTH-INDUCED holds if consolidated CFO is positive for the half and the aged receivables share falls back below 30%. STRUCTURAL is confirmed if the aged share rises past 40% or CFO turns negative while revenue grows.

Missing evidence to resolve the flag:
- Receivables ageing split by customer class (government, private, related party) at 30 Sep 2026. Source: management, via the next analyst meet or H1 FY27 notes.
- H1 FY27 cash flow statement. Source: NSE half yearly results filing.
- Rupee amount of borrowing cost capitalised into YEIDA CWIP, and the CARO (ii)(b) stock statement difference. Source: management; FY27 AR notes.
- Any credit rating rationale if the company takes one for the new bank lines. Source: rating agency site, NSE filing.

Cap effect: INDETERMINATE cash caps the verdict at PROCEED WITH CAVEATS. The confidence band moves it one level to PROCEED WITH FLAGS.

### FLAG-PROMOTER

Not active. Verdict CAUTION (6 clean, 4 caution, 0 red, no deal breaker). Carried as caveats: Imperative Healthcare, a group company in the same business, sits outside the Nov 2024 non compete agreements (B08; RHP pdf p.272 to 274). A new Rs 500 lakh guarantee backs the subsidiary's ICICI cash credit and the subsidiary's advance to the parent rose from Rs 160.09 lakh to Rs 337.54 lakh (B08; AR Note 36/41 p.104 to 105). Incoming auditor M.A.M and Associates has no findable track record (B08 input_gaps).

## Caveats carried (not verdict flags)

- Unverified order. The Rs 47 Cr Uttarakhand order has no exchange filing, customer name or tender reference across 18 filings since listing.
- Export base. FY27 export guidance of Rs 5 to 7 Cr sits on a FY26 base of Rs 53.11 lakh, after a 90% fall from Rs 524.30 lakh (AR Note 39(b) standalone).
- Supplier concentration. Mindray supplied 63.0% of purchases in 10M FY26 (66.3% FY25, 72.7% FY24) under a non exclusive regional arrangement (RHP p.36; transcript p.4).
- Disclosure discipline. Profit split labels swapped in AR Note 40 (p.143 to 144); Board's Report p.50 denies an audit qualification that the auditor's Annexure 1 (p.115 to 116) lists; consolidated results revised 25 Aug 2026 for an elimination error of Rs 337.55 lakh. 72% of standalone net PPE sits at customer sites outside the company's own physical verification (AR Note 12(b); CARO (i)(b)).
- Sector cap row. The Pharma / CDMO row versus a distribution and trading slice for the 72% traded revenue needs an operator ruling at /fttcp before phase 3.
- Archetype. No library archetype fits the reagent rental model nested inside a distributorship; named as a library gap for Role 2.

## Contradicted claims (priority monitoring items)

The peer stage logged no formally contradicted claim. The run and its verifiers found these, each against a filed source:

- "Higher contribution margin" on the equipment heavy order (transcript p.7). Peer Q-Line earns 15 to 20% gross margin on instruments it makes and 10 to 15% on traded instruments, and places large instruments at cost (QLINE call 23 Jun 2026 p.12, p.17, p.19). Avience's own remark that 80 to 85% of instruments earn no direct revenue points the same way (transcript p.7).
- Order "in hand" (transcript p.3, p.5) against "once confirmed orders are in place, banks can support" (transcript p.6) and the Board's Report line "no material changes and commitments" (AR p.39 to 40).
- Uttarakhand as "a new territory" (transcript p.5) against the RHP state table showing Uttarakhand standalone revenue of Rs 110.22 lakh, 6.78% of FY24, and Rs 21.74 lakh in 10M FY26 (RHP pdf p.160).
- "Consistent improvement" (CFO, transcript p.4) against H2 FY26 revenue up 5.0% YoY and down 4.2% on H1, with H2 PAT down 1.3% (revised consolidated results p.11).
- Board's Report "no qualification, reservation, adverse remark" (AR p.50) against the auditor's CARO Annexure 1 listing clause (ii)(b) (AR p.115 to 116).
- RHP "WHO and US FDA approved plant" (RHP pdf p.210) narrowed on the call to a plant "designed with WHO prequalification requirements in mind"; US FDA not mentioned (transcript p.8).
- RHP working capital plan of 80 receivable days in FY27 (RHP pdf p.125 to 126) against 90 to 120 days cited for government projects (transcript p.6).

## Monitorables and triggers

1. Uttarakhand order. Watch NSE for a Reg 30 order filing, and the H1 FY27 result for a government or Uttarakhand revenue line. H1 FY27 growth under 30% YoY with no such line kills the FY27 guidance. Tests the single largest input behind the 60% growth claim.
2. H1 FY27 revenue against the slowing base. H2 FY26 revenue was Rs 2,568.65 lakh, up only 5% YoY. Watch whether H1 FY27 grows off H1 FY26's Rs 2,681.93 lakh at the guided pace. Source: NSE half yearly results. Tests whether FY26's second half slowdown was a pause or a trend.
3. Cash conversion. Watch consolidated CFO to PAT in the half yearly cash flow statement; 0.70x or better is the bar, and a third year below it confirms FLAG-CASH as structural. Tests whether profit growth is turning into cash.
4. Receivable quality. Watch the share of receivables older than six months (38.9% standalone in FY26) and the provision against them (8.3%). Reversal toward 10 to 18% clears the concern; a rise past 40% confirms it. Source: results and FY27 AR Note 18. Tests the collection risk behind government orders.
5. YEIDA plant. Watch for CWIP (Rs 1,285.28 lakh) moving to PPE and a production start by Oct 2026. Still uncapitalised at the Dec 2026 half is the kill signal. Source: results notes, Reg 30. Tests the only capacity behind the maker transition.
6. Manufactured share of turnover. Watch any mix disclosure from 27% toward 50%. Below about 35% by FY27 end means the transition has stalled and the business stays a reseller. Source: H1 FY27 deck and results.
7. Mindray. Watch the purchase share (63.0% in 10M FY26) and any signed exclusivity or OEM agreement via Reg 30. A rise back toward 72.7%, or any territory cut, hits 72% of revenue. Tests supplier dependence.
8. Reporting controls. Watch the first opinion of the new auditor, a rupee figure for the CARO (ii)(b) difference, and correction of the Note 40 labels in the FY27 AR. Any new revision or qualification tests whether the FY26 slips were one year events.

## Falsification line

The single most damaging next print: H1 FY27 results (due by Nov 2026) showing revenue growth under 30% YoY with no Uttarakhand or government linked revenue, printed alongside receivables older than six months above 40% of the book.

## Publish check

No publish candidate this analysis.

# STAGE 1 — GATE 0 SCORECARD (160 points)
Avience Biomedicals Ltd (AVIENCE) | Run date 2026-09-26 | Model: claude-sonnet-5

Data available: 3 years (FY24 to FY26). Scoring adapted to 3-year history.
Basis: CONSOLIDATED (screener main URL is consolidated, FY24-FY26; standalone
carries FY22-FY26 but the scorecard follows the consolidated basis to match
the framework's preferred all-entity view). Consolidation began with the
DR Meditech subsidiary acquisition 08-Aug-2023, so FY24 already carries a
part-year consolidated base.

UNITS: filings are in Rs lakh; screener and this report use Rs Cr. Every
number below states its source; where a filing figure in lakh is converted
to Cr for consistency with screener, the conversion is shown once (100 lakh
= 1 Cr, per B00 reporting_units).

LOAD-BEARING FACTS CHECKED AGAINST THIS SCORECARD (from B00):
- LBF2 (FY26 revised results, FY25 revenue on three bases): the REVISED
  25-Aug-2026 consolidated results (note 8) corrected an inter-company
  elimination error (Other Current Liabilities and Short-Term Loans and
  Advances each overstated by Rs 337.55 lakh). NO impact on profit or
  equity (results p.10, note 8; results p.11, board outcome). This
  scorecard's D-block ratios use the REVISED consolidated balance sheet
  (results p.12), which is the correct post-fix version. FY25 revenue is
  used at the SCREENER figure (Rs 44.92 Cr) throughout this scorecard for
  internal consistency with the 3-year screener P&L/BS/CF series; the
  REVISED RESULTS FILE shows FY25 consolidated revenue as Rs 45.24 Cr
  (results p.11) and the RHP restated shows Rs 45.97 Cr (RHP p.132) — a

## BLOCK A: RETURN ON CAPITAL (Max 20) — Score: 20/20

Formula: ROCE = EBIT ÷ (Total Assets − Current Liabilities). Screener does
not carry its own ROCE figure in this Data_Sheet export (raw P&L/BS/CF
rows only), so ROCE is COMPUTED per the fixed formula.

FY26: EBIT = PBT 12.47 + Interest 1.70 = 14.17 Cr (screener-Data_Sheet).
Capital employed = Total Assets 7,456.82 lakh − Current Liabilities
2,958.09 lakh = 4,498.73 lakh = 44.99 Cr (revised consolidated results
p.12, balance sheet as at 31-Mar-2026). ROCE FY26 = 14.17 ÷ 44.99 = 31.5%
(computed).

FY25: EBIT = PBT 9.77 + Interest 1.45 = 11.22 Cr (screener-Data_Sheet).
Capital employed = Total Assets 5,648.84 lakh − Current Liabilities
2,359.18 lakh (computed as the identity Equity 2,265.98 + Non-current
liabilities 1,023.68, since the printed subtotal in the OCR'd text read
2,159.18, an evident scan error; the component sum and the balance-sheet
identity both give 2,359.18 lakh) = 3,289.66 lakh = 32.90 Cr (revised
consolidated results p.12). ROCE FY25 = 11.22 ÷ 32.90 = 34.1% (computed).

FY24: EBIT = PBT 3.03 + Interest 0.94 = 3.97 Cr (screener-Data_Sheet). The
FY24 consolidated balance sheet's current/non-current liability split is
NOT available in text form (RHP restated consolidated statements, pdf
pp.279-362, are image-only; not rendered for this stage). APPROXIMATION:
by the identity Capital Employed = Equity + Non-current liabilities, and
using RHP's own FY24 consolidated Debt-Equity ratio of 2.44x (=Long-term
debt ÷ Net worth, RHP p.132) applied to screener's FY24 net worth (Equity
Share Capital 3.28 + Reserves 2.94 = 6.22 Cr), implied FY24 long-term debt
≈ 2.44 × 6.22 = 15.18 Cr, which is close to screener's FY24 TOTAL
borrowings of 15.13 Cr — implying FY24 short-term borrowings were near
zero, i.e. non-current liabilities ≈ total borrowings for that year.
Capital employed (FY24, approximated) ≈ Equity 6.22 + Non-current liab
≈15.13 = 21.35 Cr. ROCE FY24 ≈ 3.97 ÷ 21.35 = 18.6% (computed,
APPROXIMATED; flagged in data_notes — do not treat as precise as the
FY25/FY26 figures).

A1 Median ROCE (18.6%, 34.1%, 31.5%) = 31.5% → ≥25% → **Score 5**
A2 Minimum single-year ROCE = 18.6% (FY24, approximated) → ≥15% → **Score 5**
A3 Median ROE: ROE = PAT ÷ average Net Worth.
  FY24: opening net worth unavailable (screener has no FY23 row); closing
  net worth used per rule, stated so. NW(FY24 closing) = 3.28+2.94 =
  6.22 Cr (screener-Data_Sheet). PAT FY24 = 2.16 Cr. ROE = 2.16÷6.22 = 34.7%.
  FY25: avg NW = (6.22 + [4.03+18.63=22.66]) ÷ 2 = 14.44 Cr. PAT FY25 =
  7.10 Cr. ROE = 7.10÷14.44 = 49.2%.
  FY26: avg NW = (22.66 + [4.03+27.38=31.41]) ÷ 2 = 27.04 Cr. PAT FY26 =
  8.75 Cr. ROE = 8.75÷27.04 = 32.4%.
  Median = 34.7% → ≥20% → **Score 5**
A4 ROCE trend, latest (31.5%) vs earliest (18.6%): latest ≥ earliest →
  **Score 5**

BLOCK A TOTAL: 20/20

## BLOCK B: CASH GENERATION QUALITY (Max 20) — Score: 2/20

CFO (screener-Data_Sheet): FY24 = 1.02 Cr, FY25 = 3.39 Cr, FY26 = 5.91 Cr.
Cumulative CFO = 10.32 Cr. Cumulative PAT (2.16+7.10+8.75) = 18.01 Cr.

B1 Cumulative CFO ÷ Cumulative PAT = 10.32 ÷ 18.01 = 0.573 → falls in
  0.50-0.69 → **Score 1**

Capex (purchase of PPE, from cash flow statement, excl. acquisitions):
FY26 = 13.4486 Cr (revised results p.13, "Purchase of property, plant and
equipments (including capital advances)" 1,344.86 lakh); FY25 = 9.2985 Cr
(929.85 lakh, same source, comparative column). FY24 capex is NOT broken
out anywhere in the provided corpus in this granularity; screener's total
"Cash from Investing Activity" for FY24 (−3.90 Cr) is used as a capex
PROXY (the company had negligible other investing lines pre-YEIDA ramp;
flagged as a proxy in data_notes).

FCF = CFO − Capex:
  FY24 ≈ 1.02 − 3.90 = −2.88 Cr (proxy capex)
  FY25 = 3.39 − 9.2985 = −5.91 Cr
  FY26 = 5.91 − 13.4486 = −7.54 Cr
All three years negative — consistent with B00/LBF4 ("FCF negative
FY24-FY26").

B2 FCF-positive years as proportion = 0 of 3 = 0% → <50% → **Score 0**
B3 Cumulative FCF ÷ Cumulative PAT = (−2.88−5.91−7.54) ÷ 18.01 = −16.33 ÷
  18.01 = −0.906 → negative → **Score 0**
B4 Change in WC Days, latest vs earliest available:
  Receivable Days = Receivables ÷ Revenue × 365 (Revenue basis; COGS is
  not explicitly stated as a single line in the source, so Revenue basis
  applies throughout, per the formula rule).
    FY24: 8.09÷23.83×365 = 123.9 days
    FY25: 17.21÷44.92×365 = 139.8 days
    FY26: 21.02÷52.46×365 = 146.2 days (matches B00/LBF4's screener-quoted
    146 debtor days almost exactly — cross-validated)
  Inventory Days (Revenue basis, per the same rule):
    FY24: 12.40÷23.83×365 = 189.9 days
    FY25: 13.07÷44.92×365 = 106.1 days
    FY26: 17.18÷52.46×365 = 119.5 days
    (Note: B00/LBF4 cites screener's own "inventory days" of 218, which
    screener computes on a COGS basis, not the Revenue basis this
    scorecard's formula mandates; the two are not comparable. Flagged in
    data_notes, not reconciled.)
  Payable Days (Revenue basis): NOT AVAILABLE for FY24 — screener's
  simplified balance sheet has no separate Trade Payables line (bundled
  into "Other Liabilities"), and the FY24 consolidated balance sheet note
  detail is not in text form (see A2 note above). Available for FY25-FY26
  from the revised results balance sheet (p.12):
    FY25: Payables (186.83→ FY26 col; FY25 col = 101.86+624.97=726.83
    lakh = 7.2683 Cr) ÷ 44.92 × 365 = 59.1 days
    FY26: Payables (186.83+760.63=947.46 lakh = 9.4746 Cr) ÷ 52.46 × 365 =
    65.9 days
  WC Days = RD + InvD − PD:
    FY25 = 139.8 + 106.1 − 59.1 = 186.8 days
    FY26 = 146.2 + 119.5 − 65.9 = 199.8 days
  FY24 WC Days cannot be computed (payables missing) — "latest vs
  earliest" uses FY25 as the earliest AVAILABLE full-basis year, not FY24
  (flagged; FY24-to-FY26 change may differ). Change FY26 vs FY25 = +13.0
  days → increased 5-15 days → **Score 1**

BLOCK B TOTAL: 2/20 (data_notes: cash generation is genuinely weak on the
provided numbers, not an artifact of a missing metric — CFO/PAT, FCF and

## DATA CONFIDENCE AND CLASSIFICATION

Data confidence: 3 years (FY24-FY26) → per the ladder, "3-4 LIMITED,
downgrade classification one tier."

Classification matrix: Core score 69 (falls in 60-79) + moat STRONG →
matrix gives GOOD+. Applying the 3-4-year LIMITED downgrade (one tier):
GOOD+ → **GOOD**.

Deal-breaker overrides checked:
1. Block A <8 → max GOOD: Block A = 20, NOT triggered.
2. Block B <8 → max GOOD: Block B = 2 → **TRIGGERED**, caps at GOOD
   (drivers: B2=0 zero FCF-positive years, B3=0 negative cumulative
   FCF/PAT — both driven by FY24-FY26 capex ramp for the YEIDA plant,
   which the company itself frames as growth capex, not distress; the
   scorecard records the mechanical trigger, not a judgment on cause).
3. Median ROCE <10% → max AVERAGE: 31.5%, NOT triggered.
4. Cumulative CFO/PAT <0.50 → max AVERAGE: 0.573, NOT triggered.
5. Pledge >15% → max AVERAGE: 0%, NOT triggered.
6. ND/EBITDA >3x AND IC <3x → AVOID: ND/EBITDA 1.69x, NOT triggered.
7. Revenue declined in majority of years → max AVERAGE: 0 decline years,
   NOT triggered.
8. PAT negative in any of last 3 years → max AVERAGE: PAT positive all 3
   years, NOT triggered.
9. History <3 years → AVERAGE: exactly 3 years, NOT triggered.

Both paths (matrix + history downgrade, and deal-breaker #2) independently
land on **GOOD**.

---

## GRAND TOTAL: Core 69 + Moat 18 = 87/160

## STRONGEST / WEAKEST BLOCK
Strongest: Block A (Return on Capital), 20/20, and Block C (Growth), 20/20
— tied. High, fast-compounding returns and PAT growing faster than
revenue on a small base.
Weakest: Block B (Cash Generation Quality), 2/20 — cumulative CFO covers
only 57% of cumulative PAT, FCF has been negative all three years, and
working-capital days have widened (debtor days 124→146, WC days 187→200
FY25→FY26). This is the single number set that should carry into every
downstream stage's cash-conversion check (per LBF4).

## DECISION LINE
Classification: **GOOD** (Core 69/100 GOOD+-range + STRONG moat, downgraded
one tier for 3-year LIMITED history; independently capped at GOOD by the
Block B <8 deal-breaker). This is a mechanical Gate 0 score only — it does
not resolve LBF1 (guidance delivery), LBF2 (revenue-basis reconciliation),
or LBF3 (concentration/related-party exposure), all of which are named as
first verification priorities for later stages.

---

## DATA NOTES
1. FY25 revenue used at the SCREENER figure (44.92 Cr) throughout for
   internal consistency with the screener 3-year series; the revised
   consolidated results file shows 45.24 Cr and the RHP restated shows
   45.97 Cr for the same year (LBF2). Not reconciled at this stage.
2. FY24 ROCE (18.6%) is an APPROXIMATION: the FY24 consolidated balance
   sheet's current/non-current liability split is not available in text
   form (RHP restated consolidated statements are image-only, pdf
   pp.279-362). Capital employed was derived from the accounting identity
   Capital Employed = Equity + Non-current liabilities, using RHP's own
   FY24 consolidated Debt-Equity ratio (2.44x) to infer that FY24
   long-term debt approximated total borrowings (i.e., near-zero
   short-term debt that year). FY25 and FY26 ROCE are computed directly
   from the revised consolidated results' full balance sheet detail and
   are not subject to this approximation.
3. FY24 capex used the screener's total "Cash from Investing Activity"
   (−3.90 Cr) as a PROXY for capex (purchase of PPE), since the detailed
   FY24 cash flow breakdown by investing line is not in the provided
   corpus in text form. FY25/FY26 capex are exact figures from the
   revised consolidated results cash flow statement.
4. Inventory Days in this scorecard are computed on a REVENUE basis per
   the fixed formula (COGS is not cleanly a single disclosed line here).
   B00/LBF4 quotes screener's own "inventory days" of 218, which screener
   computes on a COGS basis internally — the two figures (119.5 here vs
   218 at LBF4) are NOT comparable and are not an error.
5. Payable Days, and therefore full WC Days, could not be computed for
   FY24 (no Trade Payables line on screener's simplified balance sheet,
   and FY24 consolidated note detail is image-only in the RHP). Block B4's
   "latest vs earliest" therefore compares FY26 to FY25, not FY24.
6. E2 (promoter holding change) reflects IPO primary-issue dilution
   (87.89% pre-IPO to 64.59% post-IPO), not promoter selling, and only one
   shareholding pattern exists since listing — there is no genuine 3-year
   trend for a company listed 25-Jun-2026. Scored mechanically at 0 per
   the formula; flagged so the number is not misread downstream.
7. M5 and M7 scored 0 with PEER DATA NEEDED: the three concall-selected
   peers (QLINE, MOLBIO, TARSONS) are not a validated count of "listed
   players in the segment"; no broader ranking is in the provided corpus.
8. Loss-to-profit swings: none. PAT was positive in all three years
   (FY24 2.16 Cr, FY25 7.10 Cr, FY26 8.75 Cr).

## ANALYST NOTE
Growth and returns are exceptional on the numbers (48% revenue CAGR, 101%
PAT CAGR, ROCE rising to 31%+), but Block B's near-zero score is the real
story: every rupee of that growth has been funded by working capital
build and YEIDA capex, not internally generated cash, and FCF has been
negative in all three years on record. The GOOD classification (not
GOOD+) reflects that gap directly — a deal-breaker override, not a
history-length artifact alone. FY24's ROCE and WC figures rest on
approximations (data_notes 2-3, 5) because the RHP's FY24 consolidated
statements are image-only; a downstream stage should render those pages
if FY24 precision matters to a specific test. The IPO-driven promoter
dilution (E2=0) should not be read as an alignment red flag on its own.

---

<a id="runs-avience-2026-09-26-outputs-reports-02-notes-pass1-md"></a>

# FILE: `runs/avience-2026-09-26/outputs/reports/02-notes-pass1.md`

Source: `run/avience-2026-09-26`

# STAGE 2 - NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS, PASS 1 (FULL EXTRACTION)
Company: AVIENCE (Avience Biomedicals Limited) | Run date: 2026-09-26
Source: FY26 Annual Report (144 pp), standalone notes 1-58 (PDF pp 84-108) and
consolidated notes 1-54 (PDF pp 121-144). Unit as filed: ₹ Lakh. All figures
below are stated in ₹ Lakh as printed on the face of the notes (per B00-inputs
reporting_units; conversion to ₹ Cr happens at stage 10).

NOTE: My mandate for this pass is the Notes to Financial Statements only.
AOC-2 (Annexure-2 to Director's Report, PDF p.58) and the Independent
Auditor's Report / CARO (PDF pp.109-120 consolidated, and the standalone
equivalent) sit outside the Notes and are named only where they cross-
reference a note; their own content is for another stage.

---

## 1. ACCOUNTING POLICIES & CHANGES

- Basis of preparation: Indian GAAP (AS framework), NOT Ind AS. The company
  is SME-listed and MCA's 16-Feb-2015 notification exempts SME-Exchange
  companies from mandatory Ind AS adoption (Note 2(a), standalone p.84 and
  consolidated p.121). 🟢 Clean but material: this is a small, less
  standardised framework than Ind AS (no Ind AS 116 ROU/lease liability
  balance sheet recognition - see leases below).
- Depreciation: useful lives per Schedule II EXCEPT Plant & Equipment
  (10 yrs vs Schedule II 15 yrs) and Electrical Fittings (5 yrs vs 10 yrs),
  both shortened based on "technical evaluation carried out by management"
  (Note 2(e), standalone p.85; consolidated Note 2(f) p.122). 🟡 Watch:
  shorter useful life = MORE conservative (higher depreciation charge), the
  opposite direction of an aggressive policy. Residual value 5%.
- Leases: policy text describes only OPERATING lease treatment (expensed
  straight-line), consistent with AS (no Ind AS 116 ROU asset/liability
  recognition) (Note 2(g) standalone p.85-86; consolidated 2(h) p.122).
  Note 34 (both standalone p.101 and consolidated p.139) shows leases are
  "cancellable" (standalone) / "cancellable and non-cancellable, 3-5 years"
  (consolidated) with future minimum lease payments of Nil in FY26 (was
  Rs.3.41 lakh FY25, "not later than one year"). 🟢 Clean, small.
- Borrowing costs: AS-16 capitalisation of borrowing costs directly
  attributable to qualifying assets (Note 2(p) standalone p.88; 2(q)
  consolidated p.125). This is the policy that lets interest on the YEIDA
  plant loan be added to CWIP rather than expensed - see CWIP section below;
  no separate note quantifies the capitalised interest amount in FY26 (NOT
  FOUND IN DOCUMENT - the notes do not break out capitalised borrowing cost
  as a distinct line). 🟡 Watch: screener flagged "possible interest
  capitalisation" (LBF4); the notes confirm the POLICY exists but do not
  disclose the RUPEE AMOUNT capitalised in FY26, which is needed to test the
  flag.
- No first-time standard adoption disclosed in FY26. No policy CHANGE with a
  quantified P&L impact is disclosed (Note 58 standalone / 54 consolidated:
  "Previous year figures have been regrouped or reclassified wherever
  necessary" - a regrouping statement, not a policy change).
- Revenue recognition: risk-and-reward transfer model, net of discounts/
  taxes (Note 2(c) standalone p.84; 2(d) consolidated p.122). No contract
  asset/liability disclosure, no disaggregation by performance obligation
  timing - see Revenue section. 🟡 Watch: no ECL matrix disclosed for trade
  receivables beyond ageing-based provisioning (see Receivables section);
  no formal ECL % matrix by bucket is shown, provisioning appears case-by-
  case against ageing buckets (Note 18/20).
- Impairment: standard value-in-use test language, no growth/discount rate
  assumptions disclosed because no impairment was recognised or tested with
  quantified assumptions in the notes (NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT: no impairment
  testing assumptions table for goodwill or PPE).

## 2. RELATED PARTY TRANSACTIONS (Note 36 standalone p.103-104; Note 36
consolidated p.141-142, AS-18)

Standalone (Company only):
| Party | Relationship | Nature | FY26 (Rs lakh) | FY25 (Rs lakh) | YoY |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DR Meditech Surgical and Diagnostics (India) Pvt Ltd | Subsidiary (99.99%) | Sale of products | 488.64 | 567.04 | -13.8% |
| DR Meditech | Subsidiary | Purchases of stock-in-trade | 17.01 | 128.87 | -86.8% |
| DR Meditech | Subsidiary | Purchases of raw material | 7.77 | 5.65 | +37.5% |
| Dharam Deo Choudhary (CMD) | KMP | Remuneration | 30.00 | 37.50 | -20.0% |
| Ram Nagina Choudhary (WTD) | KMP | Remuneration | 12.00 | 7.29 | +64.6% |
| Deepa Choudhary (Director) | KMP | Remuneration | 24.00 | 14.58 | +64.6% |
| Aditya Pal | KMP relative | Remuneration | 5.22 | 1.12 | +366% |
| Directors (Dharam Deo, Ram Nagina) | KMP | Loan taken/(repaid), net | (60.69) | (135.05) | net repayment both years |
| Deepa Choudhary | KMP | Legal/professional fees | 0 | 7.06 | discontinued |

Balances at year end (standalone, Note 36-III, p.104):
- Advances from customer, DR Meditech: Rs.337.54 lakh (FY26) vs Rs.160.08
  lakh (FY25), +110.9% (Note 10, p.93 cross-ref; Note 36, p.104). This is
  the LBF3 figure: confirmed as an ADVANCE the standalone company owes to
  (has received from) its own subsidiary, i.e., DR Meditech has prepaid
  Avience for goods not yet delivered. 🟡 Watch: doubled YoY, and it sits
  inside "Other current liabilities" (Note 10, standalone p.93), not
  separately captioned on the balance sheet face. 🔴 On the standalone-only
  numbers, an intra-group amount owed by the parent to a 99.99%-owned
  subsidiary that has consolidated ONLY 79% of net assets/24% of profit
  (holding) vs 21%/76% (subsidiary) (Note 40, consolidated p.143) deserves a
  closer look at WHY the subsidiary is prepaying the parent - possible cash
  parking or working-capital shuffle between the two entities.
- Director loans payable: Dharam Deo Choudhary Rs.207.55 lakh (FY26) vs
  Rs.264.23 lakh (FY25); Ram Nagina Choudhary Rs.15.00 lakh vs Rs.19.00
  lakh; Janardan Pal Rs.18.39 lakh flat. Unsecured, interest-free, repayable
  on demand (Note 8/36.2 standalone). 🟢 Clean direction (repayment, not
  build-up) but 🟡 Watch: interest-free related-party loans are a related-
  party financing subsidy not priced at arm's length by definition of being
  interest-free (though immaterial in size vs balance sheet, ~Rs.241 lakh).
- Corporate guarantee: Company gave a Rs.500.00 lakh guarantee (FY26: new;
  FY25: Nil) to ICICI Bank on behalf of subsidiary DR Meditech's cash credit
  facility (Note 36-IV and Note 41, standalone p.104-105). 🟡 Watch: new
  parent guarantee for subsidiary debt, contingent liability at 500 lakh.
- Personal guarantees: KMP (Dharam Deo Choudhary, Ram Nagina Choudhary,
  Janardan Pal, Deepa Choudhary) and relative Geeta Choudhary have given
  PERSONAL guarantees for the company's bank cash credit facilities (Note
  36.2 standalone p.104; Note 36.2 consolidated p.142). 🟢 Standard SME
  practice, but a governance dependency on promoter personal guarantees.

Consolidated (Group level, additional related parties not in standalone,
Note 36 p.141-142):
- New related-party category disclosed ONLY at consolidated level:
  "Enterprise where KMP has substantial influence" - Ayush Diagnostics and
  Surgicals (proprietorship of Ram Nagina Choudhary), Imperative Healthcare
  Private Limited (director/promoter Deepa Choudhary), Biocorn Healthcare
  (proprietorship of a relative of Janardan Pal). Sales to Imperative
  Healthcare Rs.0 (FY26) vs Rs.100.44 lakh (FY25); Sales to Biocorn
  Healthcare Rs.1.87 lakh (FY26) vs Nil (FY25) (Note 36-II(e), p.141).
  🟡 Watch, NEW THIS PASS: trade receivables outstanding from these three
  entities total Rs.51.77 lakh (FY26) / Rs.63.70 lakh (FY25) (Note 20.1,
  consolidated p.135, and Note 36-III, p.142) - Ayush Diagnostics Rs.12.56
  lakh, Imperative Healthcare Rs.38.64 lakh, Biocorn Healthcare Rs.0.57
  lakh. 🔴 Red flag candidate: these are firms CONTROLLED BY OR CLOSELY
  LINKED TO DIRECTORS acting as CUSTOMERS with outstanding receivables, only
  disclosed at the CONSOLIDATED level (subsidiary DR Meditech is presumably
  the seller), while the standalone company's own Note 18.1/20.1 state "No
  trade receivables are due from directors or other officers... or from
  firms... in which any director is a partner, director or a member" -
  this is a standalone-vs-consolidated presentation gap worth reconciling in
  a later pass (which entity actually books these sales/receivables).
- Note 36.3 (both standalone and consolidated): sitting fees of Rs.0.09 lakh
  payable to Mr. Manoj Kumar Agarwal NOT disclosed as related party because
  he was not KMP as at year-end - a disclosure-scope carve-out, immaterial
  amount, flagged for completeness only. 🟢 Clean, immaterial.
- Note relating party count is materially larger at consolidated level (adds
  Kapil Deo Choudhary, Sumitra Devi, Arti Pal, Ayush Pal as KMP relatives,
  and the three "substantial influence" entities) vs standalone (only
  Aditya Pal, Geeta Chaudhary as KMP relatives) - subsidiary DR Meditech
  brings in its own KMP-family web.
- RPTs as % of revenue: standalone sale of products to DR Meditech Rs.488.64
  lakh / standalone revenue Rs.3,531.47 lakh = 13.8% of standalone revenue
  (Note 22 vs Note 36, standalone). At consolidated level this sale
  eliminates entirely (intra-group), so it is 0% of consolidated revenue by
  construction; the number matters for judging whether the STANDALONE
  entity's revenue print is inflated by intra-group sale.

## 3. CONTINGENT LIABILITIES (Note 41 standalone p.104-105; Note 39
consolidated p.143)

Standalone:
- Corporate guarantee to ICICI Bank for subsidiary DR Meditech cash credit:

- Capital commitments (contracts on capital account, net of advances, not

Consolidated:

  39(a), p.143). 🟡 Watch: a legacy tax dispute inherited with the DR
  Meditech acquisition (FY18 period, acquired Aug-2023).
- No capital commitment at consolidated level either (Nil FY26 vs Rs.100.72

- Total contingent liabilities as % of net worth: consolidated net worth
  (Note 40, "Net Assets") Rs.3,141.24 lakh; GST contingent liability Rs.37.16

  corporate guarantee (standalone-only; eliminates in consolidation as
  intra-group) is 15.9% of standalone net worth (share capital Rs.403.13 +
  reserves Rs.2,310.75 = Rs.2,713.88 lakh) - material at standalone level,

  No single item >10% of CONSOLIDATED net worth.
- No tax litigation beyond the GST matter; no income-tax dispute disclosed
  in the notes.

## 4. TRADE RECEIVABLES (Note 18 standalone p.97; Note 20 consolidated
p.135)

Standalone, FY26 vs FY25 (Rs lakh):
| Bucket | FY26 | FY25 |

| < 6 months | 722.34 | 811.17 |
| 6mo-1yr | 320.54 | 35.88 |
| 1-2 yr | 95.53 | 30.64 |

  QUADRUPLED as a share of the book, from 10.2% to 38.9%, driven mostly by
  the 6mo-1yr bucket jumping from Rs.35.88 lakh to Rs.320.54 lakh (+793%).
- Receivable days trend: the company's own Ratio note (Note 42, standalone
  p.106) shows Trade Receivables Turnover Ratio falling from 5.11x (FY25)
  to 3.47x (FY26), a -32% variance, and MANAGEMENT'S OWN STATED REASON is
  "a proportionately higher closing trade receivables balance... timing of
  collections and extended credit cycles for certain customers, which is
  expected to normalize" (Note 42, row 6). 🔴 Red flag: management
  itself flags credit-cycle extension in the mandatory ratio-variance note.
  This corroborates LBF4 (screener debtor days 146 consolidated).
- Provision for doubtful receivables roughly 2.6x, from Rs.14.59 lakh to
  Rs.38.04 lakh (+160.8%), broadly tracking the ageing deterioration but the
  provision as % of the >6mo book is only 38.04/459.52 = 8.3% FY26 vs
  14.59/92.42 = 15.8% FY25 - i.e., PROVISIONING COVERAGE ON THE AGED BOOK
  FELL even as the aged book itself grew. 🔴 Red flag candidate for
  provisioning adequacy.
- Disputed trade receivables (considered good) Rs.54.35 lakh FY26 vs
  Rs.29.59 lakh FY25; disputed AND doubtful Rs.31.96 lakh FY26 vs Rs.14.26
  lakh FY25 - both roughly doubled.
- Single customer >10%: NOT DISCLOSED IN THE NOTES (no customer-level
  breakdown in Note 18; AS-18/AS Schedule III does not mandate top-customer
  disclosure the way SEBI LODR concentration tables do). Cross-reference to
  RHP for the >50% top-10-customer concentration fact (LBF3) - the AR notes
  themselves are silent (NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT at note level).
- No receivables from related parties disclosed in the STANDALONE trade
  receivables note (Note 18.1: "No trade receivables are due from directors
  ..."). At CONSOLIDATED level, receivables from KMP-linked enterprises
  (Ayush Diagnostics, Imperative Healthcare, Biocorn Healthcare) total
  Rs.51.77 lakh (see Related Party section above) - a genuine related-party
  trade receivable exposure exists at the GROUP level even though the
  standalone note denies any such exposure (technically both statements are
  literally true if the subsidiary, not the parent, holds these balances,
  but an investor reading only the standalone note would miss this).

Consolidated, FY26 vs FY25 (Rs lakh), Note 20 p.135:

|---|---|---|
| <6 months | 1,322.72 | 1,416.42 |

## 6. INVESTMENTS (Note 14 standalone p.95-96; Notes 15-16 consolidated
p.134)

- Subsidiary: DR Meditech Surgical and Diagnostics (India) Pvt Ltd, 99.99%

- No ICDs or loans given to any party (standalone Note 45 / consolidated
  Note 42: "the Company/Group has not granted any loans or advances to

## 7. BORROWINGS (Note 5 & 8 standalone p.90-92; Note 6 & 9 consolidated
p.127-131)

Standalone long-term borrowings (Note 5, p.90-91), FY26 vs FY25 (Rs lakh):
| Instrument | Lender | FY26 O/S | FY25 O/S | Rate FY26 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GECL working capital | Bank of India (orig. Canara) | 3.71 | 9.64 | 8.75% (RBLR+0.65%) |
| Term loan (P&M) | Bank of India | 33.85 | 45.61 | 8.80% (RBLR+0.70%) |
| Term loan (YEIDA plant construction) | SIDBI | 954.50 | 602.00 | not separately stated, sanctioned Rs.1,200 lakh, Rs.960 lakh drawn |

- SIDBI YEIDA term loan is the dominant and growing facility: Rs.954.50 lakh
  o/s FY26 vs Rs.602.00 lakh FY25 (+58.6%), against a Rs.1,200 lakh
  sanctioned limit (80% drawn as at year end) (Note 5(a)(iii), p.90-91).
  Repayable via a structured 78-instalment schedule (24@5.5L, 6@10L, 6@15L,
  25@20L, 10@22L, 1@24L, 6@29L lakh), first instalment commenced March 2026
  (i.e. repayment has just begun, before the plant is even fully
  operational per LBF1's Oct-2026 production start). 🟡 Watch: debt service
  begins before revenue ramp from the new capacity.
- Standalone short-term borrowings (Note 8, p.91-92): total Rs.994.50 lakh
  FY26 vs Rs.746.07 lakh FY25 (+33.3%), driven by a new SIDBI cash credit
  facility of Rs.672.09 lakh (limit Rs.810 lakh, 8.30% effective rate) that
  REPLACED the prior bank cash credit (Rs.396.18 lakh FY25, discontinued in
  FY26) and the WC loan (Rs.19.63 lakh FY25, fully repaid). Unsecured
  related-party (director) loans fell from Rs.301.62 lakh to Rs.240.94
  lakh.
- Security: land at YEIDA (leasehold) hypothecated against the SIDBI loan
  (Note 5(b), p.91); Noida Sector 11 plant & machinery hypothecated against
  bank term loans (Note 12(d) cross-ref); personal guarantees of all four
  KMP directors plus a relative (Geeta Choudhary), and an equitable mortgage
  of a director's residential flat in Dwarka as collateral (Note 8(iv),
  p.92).
- No covenant breach or waiver disclosed in either note set (NOT FOUND IN
  DOCUMENT - no covenant table at all is presented, only security and rate
  terms; Ind AS-116-style covenant disclosure is not part of the AS
  framework's mandatory note content).
- Consolidated borrowings (Note 6, p.127-129) add subsidiary DR Meditech's
  ICICI Bank facilities: a Rs.183.36 lakh building-secured term loan
  (2017, ending 2033), a GECL-origin Rs.72.00 lakh loan (twice taken over,
  now via ICICI), a Rs.240.00 lakh "Top-up Loan" (Jun-2024), two vehicle
  loans (Rs.12.88 lakh and a new Rs.30.00 lakh Maruti Invicto loan taken
  Nov-2025), and a further Rs.50.00 lakh Top-up Loan (Dec-2025) - the
  subsidiary is layering MULTIPLE unsecured/secured top-up loans in FY26
  (Rs.30 lakh vehicle + Rs.50 lakh unsecured top-up + Rs.58 lakh unsecured
  term loan at 15% p.a., Note 6(iii)(i) consolidated p.129) alongside its
  existing book. 🟡 Watch: rising, layered subsidiary borrowing at a HIGH
  rate (15% p.a. on the newest Rs.58 lakh unsecured loan) is a cost-of-
  capital signal worth tracking.
- Consolidated short-term borrowings total Rs.1,570.61 lakh FY26 vs
  Rs.1,230.87 lakh FY25 (+27.6%) (Note 9, p.129-130).
- No related-party (promoter) borrowing found flowing the OTHER direction
  (company lending to promoters) - confirmed explicitly Nil by Note 45/42.

## 8. TRADE PAYABLES (Note 9 standalone p.92-93; Note 10 consolidated p.139)

Standalone, FY26 vs FY25 (Rs lakh):
| | FY26 | FY25 |
|---|---|---|
| MSME dues | 43.34 | 10.46 |
| Other dues | 463.95 | 489.69 |
| Total | 507.29 | 500.15 |

- MSME dues >45 days effectively: ageing table shows MSME "less than 1

- Payable days trend: Trade Payables Turnover Ratio flat at 4.17x both

  Consolidated MSME growth (+83.4%) is even sharper than standalone,

  Interest paid under s.16 fell from Rs.6.06 lakh to Rs.0.66 lakh at group
  level (Note 33 consolidated) - same non-payment-of-interest pattern.

## 9. PROVISIONS (Note 7/11/35 standalone; Note 8/12/35 consolidated)

- Gratuity (defined benefit, unfunded, AS-15 style): standalone closing DBO
  Rs.6.10 lakh FY26 vs Rs.7.29 lakh FY25; consolidated closing DBO Rs.17.46
  lakh vs Rs.25.97 lakh (both fell, driven by a large actuarial GAIN:
  standalone Rs.(5.69) lakh, consolidated Rs.(14.98) lakh, Note 35.2). The
  gain flowed through as a P&L CREDIT/reversal: standalone gratuity expense
  is NEGATIVE Rs.(1.18) lakh FY26 (a net credit) vs a Rs.5.48 lakh expense
  FY25; consolidated similarly flips from a Rs.2.36 lakh expense to a
  Rs.(6.99) lakh credit. 🟡 Watch: a swing from expense to a P&L credit
  driven by actuarial assumption changes (discount rate moved from 6.54% to
  7.15% standalone in FY26 per the table at line 4201, and attrition
  assumptions were revised down materially: Year-1 withdrawal rate cut from
  40% to 18% standalone) is a judgment-driven earnings tailwind worth
  isolating quantitatively at Role 1 (adds Rs.1.18 lakh standalone /
  Rs.6.99 lakh consolidated of non-operating P&L benefit, small in absolute
  terms against overall PAT of Rs.673-875 lakh but a genuine one-off).
- No warranty provision, no decommissioning provision, no onerous-contract
  provision disclosed anywhere in the notes (NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT - the
  business does not appear to carry these provision types).
- Only litigation-adjacent item is the subsidiary's GST demand carried as a
  contingent liability, not a provision (Note 39, consolidated - the
  company has NOT provided for it, treating it as merely contingent,
  consistent with an ongoing appeal).
- CSR provision: Nil shortfall/excess both years (Note 43, standalone
  p.107); required Rs.7.70 lakh FY26 (Nil FY25, first year required per the
  three-year average profit test), spent in full on "promoting educational
  activities" - no related-party CSR spend (Note 43(h): Nil both years).

- Standalone net DTL: Rs.18.64 lakh FY26 vs Rs.14.68 lakh FY25 (small,
  growing). Driven by DTL on PPE WDV difference (Rs.30.28 lakh) partly

- No unrecognised DTA disclosed.

## 11. REVENUE DETAILS (Note 22 standalone p.99; Note 24 consolidated p.137)

Standalone (Note 22.1, p.99), FY26 vs FY25 (Rs lakh):
| | FY26 | FY25 | YoY |

| Manufactured - Viral Transport Solution | 6.98 | 19.88 | -64.9% |
| Manufactured - Rapid Cards | 466.58 | 229.94 | +102.9% |
| Manufactured - Reagents & Lab Consumables | 337.28 | 476.45 | -29.2% |
| Manufactured - Instruments | 166.02 | 63.01 | +163.5% |
| Manufactured total | 976.86 | 789.27 | +23.8% |
| Traded - Instruments | 638.18 | 755.24 | -15.5% |

| Traded total | 2,522.66 | 2,089.05 | +20.8% |
| Sale of products total | 3,499.52 | 2,878.32 | +21.6% |
| Sale of service (instrument maintenance) | 31.95 | 10.63 | +200.6% |
| Total revenue | 3,531.47 | 2,888.95 | +22.2% |

- Manufacturing:trading split by revenue (standalone, sale of products
  only): 976.86/3,499.52 = 27.9% manufactured vs 2,522.66/3,499.52 = 72.1%
  traded FY26; 789.27/2,878.32 = 27.4% manufactured vs 2,089.05/2,878.32 =
  72.6% traded FY25 - the mix is essentially UNCHANGED year on year
  (~28:72), which is directly testable against LBF1's claim of a ~27:72 to
  ~50:50 mix shift planned for FY27 onward (the AR shows FY26 as the
  BASELINE year, no shift has started yet in the reported figures).
- No customer-level concentration disclosure in the notes (top-10 >50% is
  an RHP-level fact, NOT FOUND IN the AR notes themselves).
- No contract asset/liability note; no unsatisfied performance obligation
  disclosure (consistent with AS framework, no Ind AS 115 requirement).
- Export value (Note 39(b) standalone, p.104-105 renumbered / consolidated
  drops this disclosure per the exemption note): Manufactured goods export
  FOB value fell sharply, Rs.53.11 lakh FY26 vs Rs.524.30 lakh FY25 (-89.9%)
  (Note 39(b), standalone p.104). 🔴 Red flag: exports nearly disappeared
  in FY26, directly relevant to LBF1's FY27 export target of Rs.5-7 Cr -
  the base the company is growing exports FROM in FY26 is unusually LOW
  (Rs.53 lakh = Rs.0.53 Cr), meaning the FY27 export target implies roughly
  10-13x growth off a depressed base, not off the more normal ~Rs.5.24 Cr
  (Rs.524.30 lakh) FY25 base. Also matching this: unhedged FX trade
  receivable in USD fell from Rs.307.98 lakh FY25 to Nil FY26 (Note 38,
  standalone p.101), consistent with the export drop.
- Consolidated revenue (Note 24, p.137): Sale of products Rs.5,217.72 lakh
  FY26 vs Rs.4,510.16 lakh FY25 (+15.7%); Sale of services Rs.32.86 lakh vs
  Rs.13.42 lakh (+144.9%); Total Rs.5,250.58 lakh vs Rs.4,523.59 lakh
  (+16.1%). No manufactured/traded split is presented at consolidated level
  in the note (that granularity exists only in the standalone Note 22.1);
  consolidated disaggregation by product type is NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT at
  group level.
- Note 40 (consolidated, p.143) "Additional Information" - profit/net
  asset split by entity - shows the SUBSIDIARY, DR Meditech, generated
  91% of consolidated profit in FY25 and 76% in FY26 despite holding only
  28% (FY25) / 21% (FY26) of consolidated net assets. Correspondingly the
  HOLDING company (the listed entity investors actually price) generated
  only 9% (FY25) and 24% (FY26) of group profit despite holding 72%/79% of
  net assets. 🔴 Red flag / key structural finding: the standalone listed
  entity is a MINORITY contributor to group profit; group profitability is
  disproportionately carried by the wholly-owned but operationally distinct
  subsidiary. This bears directly on how "the company's" earnings quality
  and growth trajectory should be read - the RHP/call narrative (LBF1) is
  framed around the parent's manufacturing ramp, but the profit engine in
  both FY25 and FY26 has been DR Meditech.

## 12. OTHER CRITICAL NOTES

- Exceptional items: none labelled as exceptional in either note set (NOT

- Prior period items (Note 32 standalone p.100-101; Note 32a consolidated

- CWIP and capital commitments (Note 13 standalone p.95; Note 14
  consolidated p.133-134): "Projects in progress - Biochemistry Analyser
  Plant at YEIDA" - CWIP grew from Rs.461.91 lakh (FY25) to Rs.1,285.28

  Nil"). Ageing schedule (Note 13.1/14.1): of the Rs.1,285.28 lakh FY26
  balance, Rs.823.37 lakh is <1 year old and Rs.459.11 lakh is 1-2 years
  old - consistent with a plant still under construction, matching LBF1's
  "production from Oct-2026" claim (i.e., the plant had NOT started
  commercial production as at the 31-Mar-2026 balance sheet date). The
  note explicitly states "The Company does not have any CWIP which is
  overdue or exceeded to its cost compared to its original plan" (Note
  13.2/14.2) - a clean, unqualified management assertion. Capital
  commitment (contracts on capital account, not yet executed) fell to NIL
  FY26 from Rs.100.72 lakh FY25 (Note 41(b) standalone / 39(b)
  consolidated), meaning ALL committed capex contracts for the plant have
  now been placed. 🟢 On disclosure quality (clean, consistent cross-
  references); 🟡 Watch on substance: no separately quantified interest
  capitalised into this CWIP balance is disclosed (see Accounting Policies
  section above) despite the AS-16 policy explicitly allowing it, and the
  SIDBI loan funding this project grew Rs.352.50 lakh in the year (Rs.602.00

- Foreign currency exposure: hedging NOT disclosed as used (Note 38
  standalone / 38(i) consolidated show only the raw unhedged USD exposure,

- Segment reporting: single business segment (Medical devices and related
  products) and single geographic segment (India) declared under AS-17 in

  no segment-level profitability disclosure exists BECAUSE the company
  declares itself single-segment, even though the manufactured/traded split
  (Note 22.1) and the parent/subsidiary profit split (Note 40 consolidated)
  show materially different economics within "one segment." 🟡 Watch:
  single-segment self-declaration limits disclosure exactly where the
  Second-Order analysis (mandatory elsewhere in this pipeline) would want
  segment profitability.
- EPS: Basic and diluted EPS identical both years (no dilutive instruments)

  (FY26) vs Rs.18.71 (FY25) (Note 37 both sets). No ESOP scheme disclosed
  anywhere in the notes (NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT - no ESOP dilution to model).
- Share capital changes: no new shares issued in FY26 (share count flat at
  40,31,256 both years - IPO settlement in Jun-2026 falls AFTER this
  31-Mar-2026 balance sheet date, so no IPO-related equity movement is in
  these financial statements) (Note 3, both sets).
- Events after balance sheet date: the ONLY subsequent event disclosed in
  the notes is the listing itself - "Subsequent to the year end, on June
  25, 2026, the equity shares of the Company have been listed on Nifty SME
  Emerge Index" (Note 1 "BACKGROUND", standalone p.84 AND consolidated
  p.121, identical wording). 🟡 Watch / gap: NO note discusses IPO proceeds
  received, planned utilisation, or the elimination-error revision of the
  FY26 results (25-Aug-2026, after the AR's own signing date of 16-Jul-
  2026 - so the AR predates the revision event and could not disclose it as
  a subsequent event even though it happened before the 08-Sep-2026 AR

- No direct debits/credits to reserves bypassing P&L found (Reserves and

- Minority interest: Rs.0.00 in both years (Note 5 consolidated, p.127) -

  controlling stake in DR Meditech rounds to nil, not a red flag, just a

- Related-entity typo found: Note 2(b)(v) consolidated (p.121) states the
  subsidiary's financial statements used in consolidation "are drawn upto
  the same reporting date as followed by the Company, i.e., March 31, 2025"
  - this should read March 31, 2026 (a clear drafting typo, the surrounding
  consolidated financials are dated FY26 throughout and the subsidiary
  clearly IS consolidated to 31-Mar-2026 based on every other note). 🟡
  Watch: minor drafting quality issue, not a substantive error, but worth
  naming as a proofreading/review-control observation.

## LBF-SPECIFIC FINDING: THE ~Rs.337.55 LAKH ELIMINATION ERROR (LBF2/priority a)

Checked directly against the AR consolidated notes:
- Consolidated Note 11 "Other current liabilities" (p.132): total Rs.105.43
  lakh FY26 (vs Rs.142.62 lakh FY25) - NO trace of an inflated ~Rs.337.55
  lakh figure.
- Consolidated Note 22 "Short term loans and advances" (p.136): total
  Rs.188.61 lakh FY26 (vs Rs.186.43 lakh FY25) - again no trace of the
  error.
- CONCLUSION: the FY26 Annual Report's consolidated balance sheet and notes
  carry the CORRECTED figures consistent with the 25-Aug-2026 REVISED
  results, not the original 16/17-Jul-2026 filing that had the inter-
  company elimination error. This is consistent with the AR being signed
  16-Jul-2026 as printed on the notes' signature block, BUT the AR itself
  was filed to NSE on 08-Sep-2026, i.e. AFTER the 25-Aug revision - so
  either the notes text as printed was updated between the 16-Jul signature
  date and the 08-Sep filing without a re-dated signature block (a
  documentation-control point), or the auditor's 16-Jul work already
  reflected the correct elimination and the ORIGINAL 17-Jul results filing
  (not the AR) carried the error that was separately corrected on 25-Aug.
  Either way, INVESTORS READING THE AR ITSELF SEE CLEAN, CORRECTED NUMBERS.
  🟡 Watch, not a red flag on the AR's numbers themselves, but a flag on
  DOCUMENT SEQUENCING/INTERNAL CONTROL: a signed audit opinion dated
  16-Jul-2026 sits ahead of a 25-Aug-2026 revision of the SAME period's
  results, and the AR's own signature block was not updated to reflect
  that the underlying financial statements it reports had, in substance,
  a correction applied after the audit report date. Recommend Role 5.5 /
  claude.ai live verification of the exact document trail (NSE filing
  timestamps for the AR vs the revised results) since Claude Code cannot
  access live NSE announcement metadata beyond what B00 already logged.

# PASS 1 SUMMARY: TOP 10 MOST SIGNIFICANT FINDINGS

1. Subsidiary DR Meditech generated 76% (FY26) and 91% (FY25) of
   CONSOLIDATED profit while holding only 21%/28% of consolidated net
   assets; the listed HOLDING company contributed just 24%/9% of group
   profit (Note 40, consolidated, p.143). 🔴 Red flag - the profit engine
   is not where the equity story is framed.
2. Trade receivables aged >6 months rose from 10.2% to 38.9% of the book
   standalone (18.4% to 38.2% consolidated), and management's own Ratio
   note attributes it to "extended credit cycles for certain customers"
   (Note 18/Note 42 standalone p.97, 106; Note 20 consolidated p.135).
   🔴 Red flag - corroborates LBF4 cash-conversion concern with hard note
   evidence.
3. Finished-goods inventory grew +80.9% (both standalone and consolidated)
   against revenue growth of only +22.2% (standalone) / +16.1%
   (consolidated) (Note 17/19, p.96/134). 🔴 Red flag - inventory build
   outpacing sales.
4. Export sales collapsed -89.9% (Rs.524.30 lakh to Rs.53.11 lakh FY26),
   and the unhedged USD receivable fell to Nil (Note 39(b)/38, standalone
   p.101, 104-105). 🔴 Red flag - the FY27 export target (LBF1) is being
   set against an unusually depressed FY26 base.
5. The Rs.337.54 lakh advance FROM subsidiary DR Meditech TO the parent
   more than doubled YoY (Rs.160.09 lakh to Rs.337.54 lakh) (Note 10/36
   standalone, p.93, 104). 🟡 Watch - intra-group cash-flow direction
   worth understanding.
6. NEW related-party category found only at consolidated level: three
   KMP-linked enterprises (Ayush Diagnostics, Imperative Healthcare,
   Biocorn Healthcare) carry Rs.51.77 lakh of trade receivables, while the
   standalone notes state flatly "no trade receivables due from
   directors..." (Note 20.1/36 consolidated p.135, 141-142 vs Note 18.1
   standalone p.97). 🟡 Watch - presentation gap between standalone and
   consolidated disclosure.
7. The AR's consolidated Other Current Liabilities (Rs.105.43 lakh) and
   Short Term Loans & Advances (Rs.188.61 lakh) match the CORRECTED,
   post-25-Aug-2026-revision figures, not the erroneous 16/17-Jul filing
   (Note 11/22 consolidated, p.132, 136) - the AR itself is clean on this
   point, but the signature date (16-Jul) predates the revision (25-Aug),
   a document-sequencing question for Role 5.5. 🟡 Watch.
8. MSME trade payables grew +314% standalone / +83.4% consolidated while
   the company continues NOT paying the MSMED-mandated interest on delayed
   payments (interest paid fell to Rs.0.00 lakh standalone from Rs.0.89
   lakh) (Note 9/33, standalone p.92-93, 100-101). 🟡 Watch - supplier
   financing stretch.
9. YEIDA plant CWIP grew to Rs.1,285.28 lakh (Rs.823.37 lakh added in
   FY26, zero capitalised to PPE yet), funded partly by a SIDBI term loan
   that grew Rs.352.50 lakh in the year, with NO disclosed rupee amount of
   capitalised interest despite an explicit AS-16 capitalisation policy
   (Note 13/14, standalone p.95, consolidated p.133-134). 🟡 Watch - tests
   the screener's "possible interest capitalisation" flag (LBF4) directly;
   amount not quantified in the notes.
10. Standalone company gave a NEW Rs.500.00 lakh corporate guarantee to
    ICICI Bank for subsidiary DR Meditech's cash credit facility (Nil in
    FY25), equal to 15.9% of standalone net worth (Note 36-IV/41, p.104-
    105). 🟡 Watch - parent balance sheet now contingently exposed to
    subsidiary bank debt.

# STAGE 2 - NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS, PASS 2 (WHAT WAS MISSED)
Company: AVIENCE (Avience Biomedicals Limited) | Run date: 2026-09-26
Method: full re-read of standalone notes 1-58 (PDF pp.84-108) and consolidated
notes 1-54 (PDF pp.121-144) against the Pass 1 output, note by note, plus one
cross-check into AOC-1 (Annexure-1 to Directors' Report, PDF p.56-57) because
it directly tests a Pass 1 finding (Note 40's profit split). Unit as filed:
₹ Lakh throughout, per B00-inputs reporting_units.

Pass 1 was thorough. This pass reports ONLY items not covered, or covered but
under-quantified, in Pass 1.
