## NEW FINDING 1 (HIGH PRIORITY): NOTE 40 PROFIT SPLIT MAY BE MISLABELLED -
DIRECTLY CONTRADICTS PASS 1 RED FLAG #1

Cross-checking Note 40 consolidated (Additional Information, Schedule III
format, p.143-144) against two OTHER numbers in the same Annual Report:

- Note 40 FY26 states: "A: Holding company" - 24% of consolidated profit,
  Rs.205.91 lakh; "B: Subsidiary - DR Meditech" - 76% of consolidated profit,
  Rs.669.38 lakh (p.144).
- The Holding Company's OWN standalone Statement of Profit and Loss (Note 4
  standalone, p.89) shows "Profit as per Statement of Profit and Loss" for
  FY26 = Rs.673.33 lakh - within Rs.3.95 lakh of the amount Note 40 LABELS
  "Subsidiary" (Rs.669.38 lakh), not the amount it labels "Holding company."
- AOC-1 (Annexure-1 to Directors' Report, Form AOC-1, PDF p.56) discloses the
  subsidiary DR Meditech's OWN "Profit/Loss for the year": Rs.20,399.66
  THOUSAND = Rs.203.9966 lakh (unit confirmed: AOC-1's investment figure of
  Rs.5,72,98.23 thousand = Rs.572.98 lakh matches Note 14 standalone exactly)
  - within Rs.1.9 lakh of the amount Note 40 LABELS "Holding company"
  (Rs.205.91 lakh), not the amount it labels "Subsidiary."

In other words: the number Note 40 calls the PARENT's profit share (Rs.205.91
lakh) is almost identical to the SUBSIDIARY's own audited standalone profit
(Rs.204.00 lakh, AOC-1), and the number Note 40 calls the SUBSIDIARY's profit
share (Rs.669.38 lakh) is almost identical to the PARENT's own standalone
profit (Rs.673.33 lakh, Note 4). This pattern is consistent with the two
labels in Note 40 having been SWAPPED at the drafting stage.

🔴 Red flag candidate, NOT a confirmed conclusion: the Net Assets side of the
same Note 40 table does not swap as cleanly (Note 40 shows subsidiary net
assets Rs.648.88 lakh / 21%, while AOC-1 shows subsidiary "Net worth
attributable to shareholding" of Rs.1,004.33 lakh - neither the labelled nor
the swapped reading matches this cleanly, which is expected since AOC-1's net
worth figure is pre-consolidation-adjustment (before elimination of the
investment cost against subsidiary equity) while Note 40's net assets figure
is post-adjustment; the two are not directly comparable the way the profit
figures are). FY25 does not resolve as cleanly either: Note 40 labels
"Holding company" profit at Rs.67.14 lakh (9%) and "Subsidiary" at Rs.642.76
lakh (91%), while standalone Note 4's own FY25 profit is Rs.500.21 lakh -
matching neither the labelled nor a clean swapped reading. No FY25 AOC-1 is
in this corpus (it would sit in the FY25 Annual Report, not held) to complete
the FY25 cross-check.

WHY THIS MATTERS: Pass 1's Red Flag #1 ("the profit engine is not where the
equity story is framed... subsidiary generated 76% of consolidated profit")
rests entirely on Note 40's FY26 labels. If those FY26 labels are swapped -
which the near-exact matches to Note 4 and AOC-1 suggest for FY26 specifically
- then the read INVERTS: the listed HOLDING company (whose YEIDA plant ramp
is the actual growth story, LBF1) would have generated 76% of FY26 group
profit, and the subsidiary only 24%. This is a materially different
narrative for the same underlying fact pattern and it is not resolvable from
the documents in this corpus alone (FY25's numbers do not cleanly confirm
either the printed or the swapped reading, and no restated/audited subsidiary
standalone P&L for FY25 sits in this corpus). FLAG for Role 5.5 / claude.ai:
verify directly with the company or against the subsidiary's own FY26
standalone financial statements (not sighted in this corpus - only the
AOC-1 summary line is available) which entity actually produced the Rs.669-
673 lakh profit figure.
(Note 40 consolidated, p.143-144; Note 4 standalone, p.89; AOC-1, p.56)

## NEW FINDING 2: FINANCE COST NOTES CARRY NO INTEREST LINE FOR THE SIDBI
YEIDA LOAN - INDIRECT CONFIRMATION THAT ITS INTEREST IS FULLY CAPITALISED

Pass 1 flagged that the rupee amount of interest capitalised into YEIDA CWIP
is NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT. This pass finds circumstantial but strong
corroborating evidence for full capitalisation: the Finance Cost note lists
every OTHER interest-bearing facility by name (cash credit from banks, term
loan from banks, working capital loan, MSME dues, delayed statutory dues,
loan processing charges) but has NO line item for the "term loan from
financial institution" (i.e., the SIDBI YEIDA loan, Note 5(a)(iii)) in EITHER
the standalone Finance Cost note (Note 28, p.100) or the consolidated Finance
Cost note (Note 30, p.138), despite that loan's balance growing Rs.352.50
lakh in the year (Rs.602.00 lakh to Rs.954.50 lakh, average balance roughly
Rs.778 lakh). 🟡 Watch, ANALYST INFERENCE not a disclosed figure: this
absence is consistent with 100% of the SIDBI loan's FY26 interest cost being
capitalised into CWIP rather than expensed. Illustrative arithmetic only (NOT
a company-disclosed number, do not anchor Role 1 to it): at a rate in the
8.3-9% range (comparable to the company's other institutional facility, the
SIDBI cash credit at 8.30% per Note 8(iii)), an average balance of ~Rs.778
lakh implies roughly Rs.60-70 lakh of capitalised interest for FY26 - a
reasonable illustrative range, not a filed fact. The actual rupee amount
remains NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT and should still be requested directly.
(Note 28 standalone p.100; Note 30 consolidated p.138; Note 5(a)(iii)
standalone p.90-91)

## NEW FINDING 3: MAJORITY OF NET PPE SITS WITH THIRD PARTIES AND IS
EXCLUDED FROM THE COMPANY'S OWN PHYSICAL VERIFICATION

Note 12(b) standalone (p.95) discloses Plant and Equipment with a Gross block
of Rs.996.43 lakh / Net block of Rs.828.14 lakh "held with third parties" as
at FY26 (vs Rs.693.85/600.79 lakh FY25, net block +37.9%). Note 13(b)
consolidated (p.134, cross-checked) shows the group figure: Gross Rs.1,145.50
lakh / Net Rs.920.27 lakh FY26 vs Rs.839.32/693.77 lakh FY25. At standalone
level this is 72% of total net block (Rs.828.14 / Rs.1,149.02 lakh); at
consolidated level a similarly dominant share. 🔴 Red flag candidate: the
Independent Auditor's Report / CARO annexure (cross-referenced, outside the
Notes proper but directly bearing on Note 12(b)'s figures) states plainly
that "all Property, Plant and Equipment... have been physically verified by
the management at reasonable intervals EXCEPT FOR THE ASSETS HELD WITH THIRD
PARTIES" (CARO clause (i)(b)). The majority of the company's fixed assets -
consistent with a placed-instrument / reagent-rental commercial model (the
same model behind B00's flagged Distribution/trading SOTP question) - are
therefore both growing fast and outside the company's own asset-verification
regime. This is a genuine asset-control and existence-risk data point that
Pass 1 did not surface as a standalone finding (it appeared only as a
one-line note in Pass 1's Investments/Other Critical Notes review, without
the CARO cross-reference or the % of total PPE this represents).
(Note 12(b) standalone, p.95; Note 13(b) consolidated, p.134; CARO clause
(i)(b), cross-referenced)

## NEW FINDING 4: A SECOND UNDELIVERED LAND PARCEL, DISTINCT FROM YEIDA,
WITH POSSESSION OUTSTANDING NEARLY SIX YEARS

Note 15 standalone (Long term loans and advances, p.95-96): Capital advance
of Rs.205.27 lakh (FY26) includes Rs.79.52 lakh (FY26) / Rs.68.18 lakh (FY25)
paid for an INDUSTRIAL LAND parcel allotted under the "Udyog Bandhu Nivesh
Mitra" scheme at Greater Noida, per an allotment letter dated October 16,
2020 - almost six years before this balance sheet date. The note states
plainly: "The possession of the land is yet to be received." The Rs.79.52
lakh already paid includes Rs.12.64 lakh of interest. 🟡 Watch, NEW THIS
PASS: this is a SEPARATE parcel from the YEIDA plant land (Note 12(a), which
was leased in FY23-24 and is where the Biochemistry Analyser Plant CWIP
sits). A nearly six-year-old government land allotment with possession still
undelivered, and capitalised interest already embedded in the advance, is a
land-acquisition execution risk not previously surfaced; it should be tested
against whatever the company's growth plan assumes this second parcel is
for (an expansion site, or a stalled/dead allocation still carried as an
asset).
(Note 15, standalone, p.95-96)

## NEW FINDING 5: PREPAID IPO COSTS SIT ON THE FY26 BALANCE SHEET AS AN ASSET

Note 20 standalone (Short term loans and advances, p.97-98) footnote:
Prepaid Expense of Rs.38.15 lakh (FY26) "includes expense incurred of Rs.36.23
lakhs (March 31, 2025: Rs.15.39 lakhs) incurred in relation to ensuing SME
IPO." 🟢 Clean, standard treatment (share-issue expenses commonly deferred
and later adjusted against securities premium on completion of the issue,
which occurred in June 2026, after this balance sheet date), but a genuinely
new data point Pass 1 did not surface: cumulative IPO-related costs carried
as a prepaid asset had already reached Rs.36.23 lakh (of a presumably larger
total IPO cost) as at 31-Mar-2026, before the June-2026 listing. Relevant
context for reconciling post-IPO securities premium movements and total IPO
cost when that lands in the FY27 accounts.
(Note 20, standalone, p.97-98)

## NEW FINDING 6: MANDATORY DISCLOSURE OF A Q4 BANK STOCK-STATEMENT
DISCREPANCY, UNQUANTIFIED

Note 47 standalone (p.108) / Note 44 consolidated (p.144), a routine-looking
Schedule III mandatory disclosure that Pass 1 did not extract: "Quarterly
returns or statements of current assets filed by the Company in respect of
borrowings from banks are in agreement with the books of account, EXCEPT FOR
THE QUARTERLY RETURNS OR STATEMENTS FILED FOR THE FOURTH QUARTER. However,
the differences were not material." 🟡 Watch: this is exactly the kind of
routine-looking note Pass 2 exists to catch. It discloses a real
book-to-bank-filing discrepancy specifically in Q4 (Jan-Mar 2026, the
quarter immediately preceding the June-2026 IPO and closest to the
inventory/receivables buildup flagged elsewhere in this run) and
self-certifies it as "not material" WITHOUT stating the rupee amount of the
difference anywhere in the notes. Given the same period shows the sharpest
deterioration in receivables ageing and inventory build (Pass 1 findings
1-3), the unquantified Q4 stock-statement mismatch is worth requesting in
absolute rupee terms rather than accepting the qualitative assurance at face
value. The identical disclosure appears at consolidated level, naming it as
specific to "the Holding Company" (i.e., the subsidiary's own bank filings
reconciled cleanly; only the parent's Q4 filing did not).
(Note 47 standalone, p.108; Note 44 consolidated, p.144)

## NEW FINDING 7: OTHER INCOME COLLAPSED -71.7%, AN EARNINGS-QUALITY
IMPROVEMENT SIGNAL POINTING THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION FROM MOST OTHER FLAGS

Note 23 standalone (Other Income, p.99) was not extracted in Pass 1 at all.
Total Other Income fell from Rs.64.81 lakh (FY25) to Rs.18.36 lakh (FY26),
-71.7%, driven by: Export Incentives Rs.11.38 lakh to Rs.1.03 lakh (-90.9%,
tracks the export collapse already flagged); "Other incentives and
discounts" Rs.39.35 lakh to Rs.4.98 lakh (-87.3%, the single largest
component of the fall, and the note gives NO description of what this
income line represents - NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT beyond the caption itself);
and a "Liability no longer required written back" of Rs.2.57 lakh (FY25)
that did not recur in FY26 (Nil). 🟢 On direction, this is a genuine
earnings-quality POSITIVE the run has not yet weighed: FY26's reported PAT
growth (Rs.673.33 lakh vs Rs.500.21 lakh, +34.6% standalone) was achieved
DESPITE carrying Rs.46.45 lakh LESS of non-operating other income than FY25 -
meaning the underlying operating improvement is somewhat BETTER than the
headline PAT growth suggests, a genuine offsetting fact against this run's
several receivables/inventory/export red flags. The unexplained Rs.34.37
lakh fall in the "other incentives and discounts" line is itself worth a
one-line management question, since no note describes its nature (a
subsidy? a vendor rebate? a one-off settlement?).
(Note 23, standalone, p.99)

## NEW FINDING 8 (MINOR): RISING "SPECIAL PURPOSE AUDIT" FEE

Note 31 standalone (Payments to Auditors, p.100): fee "For Special Purpose
Audit" paid to Haribhakti & Co. LLP rose from Rs.2.50 lakh (FY25) to Rs.4.50
lakh (FY26), +80%, alongside a flat statutory audit fee (Rs.3.00 lakh both
years). 🟢 Minor, likely IPO-related certification work (the note does not
specify the purpose - NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT). Named for completeness given
the outgoing auditor's imminent rotation (LBF4) and the general relevance of
non-statutory fee patterns to auditor-independence reads.
(Note 31, standalone, p.100)

## NEW FINDING 9 (MINOR): SIDBI CASH CREDIT RATE REBATE CONFIRMS A ZED GOLD
QUALITY CERTIFICATION

Note 8(iii) standalone (p.92): the SIDBI cash credit facility (Rs.672.09
lakh drawn, Rs.810 lakh limit) carries "a rebate of 0.25% applicable to MSME
ZED Gold Certificate holders." 🟢 Confirms, as a side fact not previously
surfaced, that the company holds a Zero Defect Zero Effect (ZED) Gold
manufacturing-quality certification under the government's MSME ZED scheme -
a neutral-to-positive operational data point, named for completeness.
(Note 8(iii), standalone, p.92)

## NEW FINDING 10 (MINOR): SUBSIDIARY'S THREE ICICI TERM LOANS NOW STACKED
PARI PASSU ON A SINGLE BUILDING

Note 6(ii)(A) consolidated (p.129): the subsidiary DR Meditech's three ICICI
Bank term loans (Rs.183.36 lakh original building loan, Rs.240.00 lakh
Top-up Loan, and the new Rs.50.00 lakh Top-up Loan taken Dec-2025) are all
secured, pari passu, against the SAME collateral: the subsidiary's building
at 8, Block B, Pocket 3, Sector 17, Dwarka. Rs.473.36 lakh of secured debt is
now layered on one property, plus the fund-based working capital facility
also ranks pari passu against it. 🟡 Watch, adds specificity to Pass 1's
already-flagged "layering" observation: collateral concentration risk at the
subsidiary is on a single asset, not merely aggregate debt growth.
(Note 6(ii)(A), consolidated, p.129)

# PASS 2 NEW FINDINGS SUMMARY

Ten new items surfaced on the re-read, none of which duplicate Pass 1. The
single most consequential is New Finding 1 (the Note 40 profit-split labels):
it directly tests, and potentially inverts, Pass 1's Red Flag #1 conclusion
about which entity is actually driving group profit, using two independent
cross-checks (the parent's own standalone P&L, and the subsidiary's own
AOC-1 disclosure) both already sitting inside this Annual Report. This is
flagged as a high-priority verification item for Role 5.5, not resolved
here, because the corroborating FY25 numbers do not confirm either reading
cleanly and the subsidiary's own full FY26 standalone financial statements
are not in this corpus (only the AOC-1 summary line is). New Finding 2
(missing SIDBI interest line) and New Finding 3 (unverified third-party PPE)
both add hard, anchored substance to flags Pass 1 had already raised in
softer form. New Findings 4-10 are genuinely new items Pass 1's checklist
pass did not reach.

# STAGE 2 - NOTES TO FINANCIAL STATEMENTS, PASS 3 (PATTERN PASS + CONSOLIDATION)
Company: AVIENCE (Avience Biomedicals Limited) | Run date: 2026-09-26
Method: pattern re-read of standalone notes 1-58 (PDF pp.84-108) and consolidated
notes 1-54 (PDF pp.121-144) against Pass 1 and Pass 2 findings, PLUS a
document cross-check into the RHP (RHP_Avience_Biomedicals_Jun2026.txt) and
the FY26 standalone/consolidated results filings to settle the priority open
item named in the task brief. Unit as filed: Rs Lakh throughout unless noted.

## PART A: PATTERN PASS

Looking across all notes for contradictions, mismatches to the main
statements, deliberately thin disclosure, restatements, subsequent events,
and going-concern language, on top of what Pass 1 and Pass 2 already
surfaced:

- No NEW contradiction pattern beyond the one already flagged (Note 40
  profit-split labels vs Note 4 standalone PAT and AOC-1 subsidiary PAT,
  Pass 2 New Finding 1) was found on this re-read. That item IS the pattern
  finding this pass exists to catch, and it is resolved below in Part B
  using external documents the two prior passes had marked as unavailable.
- No further prior-year restatement or reclassification beyond the two
  already logged (25-Aug-2026 consolidated elimination correction; the
  standard "regrouped or reclassified" boilerplate) was found.
- No going concern language anywhere in the Notes (confirms Pass 1 and

- No subsequent event beyond the listing itself (Note 1, both note sets) is

- One additional cross-check performed as PATTERN work: EPS arithmetic.

  p.11 revised CFS) / 40,31,256 shares = Rs.21.71 EPS, matching Note 37's
  printed EPS exactly - internally consistent. FY25 consolidated PAT
  Rs.709.90 lakh / 40,31,256 shares implies ~Rs.17.61, but Note 37 prints

  average-share effect from the Aug-2023 share-swap issuance (4,58,385
  shares issued mid-FY24, so FY25's own weighted average should already be
  the full 40,31,256 - the gap is therefore NOT fully explained by share

PASS 3 pattern re-read otherwise yields no material new findings beyond the

## PART B: PRIORITY OPEN ITEM RESOLVED - NOTE 40 PROFIT-SPLIT LABELS

**Question carried from Pass 2:** are the Note 40 consolidated "Holding
company" (24%, Rs.205.91 lakh) / "Subsidiary" (76%, Rs.669.38 lakh) FY26
labels swapped, given Rs.669.38 lakh sits within Rs.3.95 lakh of the
parent's own standalone PAT (Rs.673.33 lakh, Note 4) and Rs.205.91 lakh
sits within Rs.1.9 lakh of the subsidiary's own AOC-1 PAT (Rs.203.9966
lakh)?

**Test (i): FY26 standalone PAT in the audited standalone results vs
consolidated PAT.**
- Standalone (parent-only) audited results, year ended 31-Mar-2026: Profit
  for the year Rs.673.33 lakh (AVIENBIO audited standalone results filing,
  PDF p.9, line "Profit for the period/year," column FY26; matches AR Note
  4 standalone exactly).
- Consolidated audited results, same period: Profit for the year after
  minority interest Rs.875.28 lakh (AVIENBIO audited consolidated results
  filing, PDF p.17; identical Rs.875.28 lakh in the 25-Aug-2026 Revised CFS
  filing, PDF p.11 - the revision did not touch this P&L line, consistent
  with B00's note that the elimination error had no profit/equity impact).
- Holding company's share of the CONSOLIDATED total, computed directly:
  673.33 / 875.28 = 76.9%. Residual (= subsidiary's net contribution after
  intra-group elimination) = 875.28 - 673.33 = 201.95 lakh = 23.1%.
- This computed 76.9% / 23.1% split matches the Note 40 table's PRINTED
  PERCENTAGES (76% / 24%) almost exactly, but only if the LABELS are
  swapped: the 76% figure belongs to the Holding company, not the
  Subsidiary as printed, and the 24% figure belongs to the Subsidiary, not
  the Holding company as printed. The rupee amounts confirm this
  independently: Rs.669.38 lakh (labelled "Subsidiary") is 0.6% away from
  the Holding company's own Rs.673.33 lakh standalone PAT; Rs.205.91 lakh
  (labelled "Holding company") is 0.9% away from the Subsidiary's own
  Rs.203.9966 lakh AOC-1 PAT.

**Test (ii): RHP restated standalone vs restated consolidated PAT and
revenue, FY24/FY25/10M-FY26.**
RHP KPI table (RHP PDF p.136 and, identically, p.165; "Basis for Issue
Price" and "Our Business" sections), amounts in Rs lakh:

| Period | Consolidated PAT | Standalone PAT | Standalone share of consol. | Subsidiary net contribution |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| FY24 | 214.11 | 198.38 | 92.7% | 15.73 (7.3%) |
| FY25 | 723.18 | 513.50 | 71.0% | 209.68 (29.0%) |
| 10M FY26 (Apr'25-Jan'26) | 573.96 | 418.08 | 72.8% | 155.88 (27.2%) |

(RHP standalone FY25 revenue Rs.2,888.95 lakh, RHP PDF pp.136/165, matches
the AR's own standalone FY25 revenue figure exactly, Note 22 standalone -
confirming the RHP and AR datasets are drawn from the same underlying
standalone books and are safe to compare.)

- FY24's low subsidiary contribution (7.3%) is explained by the Aug-2023
  acquisition date: only about 8 months of DR Meditech is in the FY24
  consolidated numbers, so a small subsidiary contribution is expected and
  is NOT evidence for or against the swap.
- FY25 and 10M-FY26 both show the HOLDING COMPANY as the large majority
  contributor to consolidated profit (71.0% and 72.8% respectively), not
  the minority contributor the printed FY26 Note 40 labels would imply
  (24%) and not even close to the printed FY25 labels (Holding 9%). This
  independent, externally-drawn (RHP restated financials, not the AR)
  dataset corroborates the SAME direction as Test (i): across three
  different data sources (AR Note 4, AR results filings, RHP restated
  KPIs), the Holding company is consistently the majority profit
  contributor to the group, not the 24%/9% minority the AR's Note 40 as
  printed would have an investor believe.

**Verdict on the priority item: RESOLVED FOR FY26, HIGH CONFIDENCE. The
Note 40 (consolidated, p.143-144) FY26 "Holding company" / "Subsidiary"
labels ARE SWAPPED.** The correct reading is: Holding company (the listed
parent, whose YEIDA plant ramp is the growth narrative, LBF1) generated
approximately 76-77% of FY26 consolidated profit (~Rs.669-673 lakh); the
subsidiary DR Meditech generated approximately 23-24% (~Rs.204-206 lakh).
This INVERTS Pass 1's Red Flag #1 as originally stated: the profit engine
in FY26 IS where the equity story is framed (the parent), not the
subsidiary. Pass 1's Red Flag #1 is hereby RETIRED for FY26 and replaced
by this corrected, resolved reading in the consolidated findings table
below.

**FY25 status: swap direction supported, exact figures UNRESOLVED.** The
printed FY25 Note 40 labels (Holding 9%/Rs.67.14 lakh; Subsidiary 91%/
Rs.642.76 lakh) sum correctly to the FY25 consolidated PAT (Rs.709.90
lakh, results filing PDF p.17/p.11), so the ERROR (if any) is confined to
which label sits against which number, consistent with the FY26 pattern.
The AR's own standalone FY25 PAT (Rs.500.21 lakh, Note 4/results filing
PDF p.9) is closer to the swapped "Holding" figure (Rs.642.76 lakh, gap
Rs.142.55 lakh / 28.5%) than to the printed "Holding" figure (Rs.67.14
lakh, gap Rs.433.07 lakh / 86.5%), and the RHP-derived FY25 holding share
(71.0%, Test ii) is also far closer to a ~91% swapped reading than to a 9%
printed reading - but neither AR nor RHP FY25 evidence reconciles to the
swapped Rs.642.76 lakh figure as tightly as FY26 resolved. This residual
gap (~Rs.140-240 lakh depending on which comparator is used) is most
plausibly a consolidation adjustment specific to FY25 as the FIRST full
year of DR Meditech's consolidation (e.g., unrealised intra-group profit
elimination, goodwill-related entries, or a one-off subsidiary result not
visible without FY25's own subsidiary standalone financials). **What would
settle it: the subsidiary DR Meditech's own FY25 standalone P&L (would sit
in the FY25 Annual Report's AOC-1, or the subsidiary's own FY25 audited
financials) - not in this corpus.** Recommend Role 5.5 obtain this
specific document; until then, treat FY25's split direction as
"Holding-majority, swap-consistent" but not numerically anchored.

**Consequence for the run:** Pass 1's Red Flag #1 ("subsidiary drives
profit, listed entity is a minority contributor") does NOT hold for FY26
on the corrected reading, and is directionally contradicted for FY25 too
(Holding company likely the majority contributor both years, based on two
independent data sources). The correct structural read is closer to: the
subsidiary's PROFIT CONTRIBUTION has been shrinking as a share of the
group (very roughly ~29% FY25 to ~23-24% FY26 on the corrected reading),
while its NET ASSET share has also shrunk (28% FY25 to 21% FY26, Note 40 -
this side of the table is NOT swapped, per Pass 2's own check that net
assets don't swap cleanly either way, consistent with net assets not being
subject to the same labelling slip as the profit row). This is a
materially different, and materially LESS alarming, structural finding
than Pass 1 originally reported. It should still be flagged (a drafting
error of this kind in a listed company's statutory notes is itself a
disclosure-quality data point, see accounting quality score below), but
the FLAG-STRUCTURE severity moves from "the equity story is not where the
profit is" to "Note 40 has a probable labelling error that a careless
reader would misinterpret as a serious structural problem, and momentarily
did in Pass 1 of this very pipeline."

(Anchors used above: Note 40 consolidated, AR PDF p.143-144; Note 4
standalone, AR PDF p.89; AOC-1, AR PDF p.56; AVIENBIO audited standalone
results filing PDF p.9; AVIENBIO audited consolidated results filing PDF
p.17; AVIENBIO Revised CFS filing (25-Aug-2026) PDF p.11; RHP KPI table,
RHP PDF p.136 and p.165; Note 22 standalone, AR PDF p.99.)

| Provisioning adequacy | 4 | Receivables ageing has deteriorated sharply while provisioning coverage on the aged book has FALLEN (8.3% vs 15.8% prior year); gratuity provision swung to a P&L credit on actuarial assumption changes |
| RPT fairness | 6 | Interest-free director loans and a new uncompensated parent guarantee for subsidiary debt are not arm's-length by construction, though disclosure of the RPT table itself is thorough; the standalone-vs-consolidated related-party receivables presentation gap is a fairness-adjacent disclosure issue |
| Disclosure transparency | 5 | Genuinely good on breadth (every mandatory AS-framework note present, ageing schedules, ratio-variance commentary in plain language) but the Note 40 labelling error, the unquantified interest-capitalisation amount, the unquantified Q4 bank-filing mismatch, and a dated drafting typo (Note 2(b)(v) consolidated) together show a review-control weakness in the notes' final preparation |
| Consistency with prior years | 6 | No undisclosed policy changes; regrouping statement is standard; the one real restatement (25-Aug-2026 elimination correction) is confirmed clean in the AR's own figures, though the AR's signature-date sequencing relative to that revision is unresolved |
| **OVERALL** | **5.5/10** | Moderate. The notes are broad and largely honest in direction (several items understate rather than overstate risk, e.g. conservative depreciation, the Other Income finding), but a cluster of real cash-conversion deterioration (receivables, inventory, MSME payables) combined with a material labelling/drafting error in a structurally important note (Note 40) and undisclosed capitalised-interest and bank-filing-mismatch amounts pull the score below "clean" |

### C. KEY RISKS FROM NOTES

| Risk | Severity | What to monitor | When it could hit |
|---|---|---|---|
| Receivables ageing deterioration outpacing provisioning | HIGH | Note 42-equivalent ratio commentary in FY27 AR/quarterlies; whether the >6-month share keeps rising | Already visible FY26; confirms in FY27 quarterly results |
| Finished-goods inventory build ahead of sales | HIGH | FY27 sell-through once YEIDA plant output starts (Oct-2026 per LBF1); watch for a write-down if demand does not absorb the build | H1-H2 FY27 |
| Undisclosed capitalised interest on YEIDA CWIP | MEDIUM | Whether FY27 AR discloses the cumulative capitalised amount once the plant is commissioned and the policy requires cessation of capitalisation | Plant commissioning, expected Oct-2026 |
| 72% of PPE unverified by the company's own process (third-party held) | MEDIUM | Whether the FY27 CARO annexure still carries the same exception, and whether any reconciliation gap surfaces | FY27 audit cycle |
| Note 40 labelling-error pattern recurring | LOW-MEDIUM | Whether the FY27 AR's equivalent Additional Information note is internally consistent with the parent's and subsidiary's own standalone P&Ls | FY27 AR |
| Export base unusually low ahead of a stated growth target | MEDIUM | FY27 export revenue print against the Rs.5-7 Cr LBF1 target and against the Rs.53 lakh FY26 / Rs.524 lakh FY25 base | FY27 full year |
| MSME payment stretch with unpaid statutory interest | LOW-MEDIUM | MSME ageing and interest-paid trend in FY27 notes | FY27 AR |

### D. FIVE QUESTIONS FOR MANAGEMENT

1. Please confirm directly whether the Note 40 (consolidated, p.143-144)
   FY26 "Holding company" and "Subsidiary" profit-share labels were
   transposed at drafting, and whether the FY25 labels carry the same
   transposition (this run's own cross-check against Note 4 standalone PAT
   and AOC-1 strongly suggests a swap for FY26; FY25 is directionally
   consistent but not numerically confirmed without DR Meditech's FY25
   standalone financials).
2. What is the exact rupee amount of borrowing cost capitalised into the
   YEIDA plant CWIP in FY26, given the SIDBI term loan grew Rs.352.50 lakh
   in the year and no interest line for that facility appears in either
   Finance Cost note?
3. What is the rupee amount of the Q4 FY26 discrepancy between the
   quarterly current-asset statements filed with banks and the books of
   account (Note 47 standalone / Note 44 consolidated), given it coincides
   with the sharpest receivables and inventory deterioration of the year?
4. What is driving the "6 months to 1 year" receivable bucket's near-9x
   jump (Rs.35.88 lakh to Rs.320.54 lakh standalone) - specific customers,
   a change in credit terms, or a collections process issue - and is the
   extension expected to "normalize" as Note 42 states, with a timeline?
5. What is the current status of the Greater Noida industrial land parcel
   (Udyog Bandhu Nivesh Mitra allotment, 16-Oct-2020) where possession
   remains outstanding almost six years on, and does the growth plan assume
   this parcel for a specific future use?

### E. NOTES-BASED RED FLAGS

- Provisioning adequacy has not kept pace with receivables ageing
  deterioration (coverage fell from 15.8% to 8.3% on the >6-month book even
  as that book nearly quadrupled as a share of total receivables) - an
  earnings-management-ADJACENT indicator (understated bad-debt expense),
  though not confirmed as deliberate.
- Undisclosed risk indicator: majority of net PPE is outside the company's
  own physical verification (third-party held, CARO-confirmed exception).
- A material labelling error in Note 40 (consolidated) is itself a
  disclosure-integrity red flag independent of which direction it points -
  a listed company's statutory notes should not require a downstream
  analyst's three-document cross-check to determine which entity earned
  most of the group's profit.
- No evidence of deliberate earnings management via exceptional-item
  mislabelling, revenue-recognition timing, or reserve-bypass entries was
  found; the gratuity actuarial-gain P&L credit and the Other Income
  decline are both genuine, disclosed, non-recurring items correctly
  isolable rather than concealed.

### F. ONE-LINE NOTES VERDICT

"The notes reveal moderate accounting practices, honest in direction but
weak in review control. Key concern: receivables and inventory quality
have deteriorated faster than provisioning has kept up, and a material
Note 40 profit-split labelling error (now resolved by this pass in the
Holding company's favour for FY26) shows the notes were not carefully
proofed before filing. Key strength: the AR is broadly transparent
(management's own ratio-variance note names the credit-cycle problem in
plain language, Other Income disclosure shows FY26's PAT growth is
better-quality than the headline suggests, and no exceptional-item
concealment or reserve-bypass entries were found). Overall accounting
quality: 5.5/10."

# FILE: `runs/avience-2026-09-26/outputs/reports/03-ardeep.md`

# STAGE 3: ANNUAL REPORT DEEP DIVE (BACKWARD READ) — AVIENCE BIOMEDICALS LIMITED
FY 2025-26 Annual Report (7th AGM, first AR as a listed company). Run date 2026-09-26.
Source: Annual_Report_FY2026_Avience.pdf (144 pp), text-extracted with PDF page markers.
No earlier AR exists (listed 25-Jun-2026); backward baseline is the RHP (per B00/B02, weighed not anchored where AR itself is silent).

---

## PHASE 1: AUDITOR'S REPORT & CARO

### 1A Core opinion
Standalone: unmodified opinion, "true and fair view... state of affairs... as at March 31, 2026, its profits and its cash flows" (Auditor's Report, Opinion, p.71). No going-concern qualifying language; standard going-concern responsibility paragraphs only (p.72-73). CARO clause (xix) explicitly finds no material uncertainty on 1-year liability coverage (p.77-78).
Consolidated: unmodified opinion, same wording, "consolidated state of affairs of the Group... consolidated profit and... consolidated cash flows" (Auditor's Report, Opinion, p.109). Based partly on the subsidiary's own auditor's report (Other Matters, p.111-112).
No Emphasis of Matter paragraph in either report. No Key Audit Matters section in either report — consistent with an SME-Emerge listing where KAM reporting is not mandated the way it is for main-board issuers; this itself is a scope limitation on Phase 1B below.

### 1B Key Audit Matters
NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT. Neither the standalone nor consolidated Independent Auditor's Report contains a "Key Audit Matters" section (verified: report runs Opinion → Basis for Opinion → Other Information → Responsibilities → Report on Other Legal and Regulatory Requirements, with no KAM heading, p.71-81 standalone, p.109-117 consolidated). 🟡 This is a disclosure gap relative to main-board practice, not a modification; SME/Emerge-listed companies commonly omit KAMs. Flagged as WATCH for completeness, not treated as company-specific red flag.

### 1C Emphasis of Matter and Other Matters
No Emphasis of Matter in either report. Consolidated report carries an "Other Matters" paragraph: the subsidiary DR Meditech's financial statements (total assets Rs.2,57,025.38 thousand = Rs.2,570.25 lakh; net assets Rs.1,00,433.25 thousand = Rs.1,004.33 lakh; revenue Rs.2,24,303.35 thousand = Rs.2,243.03 lakh; net cash inflow Rs.253.24 thousand) were audited by another auditor, and the Holding auditor's opinion "in so far as it relates to the amounts and disclosures included in respect of subsidiary... is based solely on the reports of the other auditor" (p.111-112). Standard reliance language, not a qualification.

### 1D CARO 2020 clause-by-clause
Standalone Annexure 1 (p.75-78), all clauses reviewed:
- (i)(b) PPE physical verification: "all the Property, Plant and Equipment's... have been physically verified by the management... except for the assets held with third parties" (p.76). Read together with B02's Note 12(b) finding that 72% of net PPE (Rs.828.14 lakh of Rs.1,149.02 lakh) sits with third parties, this means the majority of the company's fixed assets are outside its own verification cycle — a real, if narrative-only, gap. 🟡
- (ii)(a) Inventory verification: no discrepancy ≥10% noted (p.76). 🟢
- (ii)(b) Working-capital security statements vs books: "the difference between the quarterly returns/statements filed... and the books of account... is not material in nature" (p.76-77). CROSS-REFERENCE CONTRADICTION: the Annexure 1 to the CONSOLIDATED Auditor's Report (a mandatory summary table of "qualifications or adverse remarks") lists exactly one line — Avience Bio Medicals Limited (Holding Company), CARO clause ii(b) (p.115-116). So the narrative text calls the variance immaterial, but the formal qualification-summary table flags this same clause as containing a "qualification or adverse remark." This is the same underlying fact B02 flagged (Note 47 standalone / Note 44 consolidated: Q4 FY26 bank stock-statement discrepancy, no rupee amount disclosed). 🟡 WATCH: the rupee amount is still nowhere in the document, and the auditor's own paperwork disagrees internally on whether this is a qualification.
- (iii) loans to related parties: not applicable — company made no loans/guarantees/investments in the nature of loans to any party (p.76). 🟢 (Note: this does not cover the Rs.500 lakh corporate guarantee given for the subsidiary's bank facility, which is reported separately as a contingent liability, Note 41, not under CARO (iii).)
- (vii) statutory dues: "generally regular... though there have been slight delays in a few cases"; no disputed dues (p.77). 🟡 minor, not quantified.
- (ix) borrowing defaults: "has not defaulted in repayment of loans or other borrowings or payment of interest thereon to any lender" (a), not a wilful defaulter (b), (c)-(f) all clean (p.77). 🟢
- (xi) fraud: "neither come across any instance of fraud by the Company nor any fraud on the Company... nor have we been informed of any such instance by the management"; no ADT-4 filed; no whistleblower complaints (p.77-78). 🟢
- (xvii) cash losses: "has not incurred cash losses in the current and the immediately preceding financial year" (p.78). 🟢
- (xx) CSR: no unspent CSR amount, no ongoing projects requiring transfer (p.78). 🟢 Consistent with Board's Report Annexure-3: CSR spend Rs.7.70 lakh against a prescribed Rs.7.69 lakh (fully spent, p.60-61).

Consolidated Annexure 1 (p.115-116): a summary table format only, confirming clause (ii)(b) as the sole item, attributed to the Holding Company; subsidiary carries no listed qualification.

### 1E Auditor continuity
Firm: M/s Haribhakti & Co. LLP (ICAI FRN 103523W/W100048), engagement partner Kunj B. Agrawal (Membership No. 095829). Appointed at the 2nd AGM (2021) for a five-year term through the 7th AGM (2026) — this is the auditor's FIRST term, not a forced rotation under Section 139(2) (which would permit a firm up to two 5-year terms/10 years). The Board is instead proposing M/s M.A.M and Associates (FRN 015680N) as successor (Board's Report §26a, p.50; AGM Notice Item 4, p.7-8). 🟡 WATCH: auditor change immediately following the IPO/listing year, paired with the 25-Aug-2026 results revision (LBF2) and the CARO ii(b) ambiguity above — not disqualifying on its own, but worth tracking who signs the FY27 opinion and whether the transition is smooth.
Audit vs non-audit fees (Note 31 standalone, p.100-101, "Payments to auditors, excluding GST"): Statutory audit Rs.3.00 lakh; Special Purpose Audit Rs.4.50 lakh; out-of-pocket Rs.0.14 lakh; total Rs.7.64 lakh (FY25: Rs.3.00 + Rs.2.50 + Rs.0.25 = Rs.5.75 lakh). 🔴 FLAG per protocol: the "Special Purpose Audit" fee (Rs.4.50 lakh, likely IPO-related) EXCEEDS the core statutory audit fee (Rs.3.00 lakh) in both years, and the gap widened in FY26. This is not necessarily improper (IPO-related special-purpose audits are common and one-off), but it meets the literal test the protocol asks to flag.

### 1F Standalone vs consolidated differences
Consolidated report relies on another auditor for the subsidiary (Other Matters, p.111-112); the subsidiary's own CARO carries no qualification (only the Holding Company's ii(b) is listed, p.115-116). Litigation disclosure differs in wording: standalone Rule 11(h)(i) states "the Company does not have any pending litigations which would impact its financial position" (p.73-74); the consolidated equivalent instead says the "Consolidated financial statements disclose the impact of pending litigations on the consolidated financial position of the Group — Refer Note 39" (p.113-114) — a materially different form of words (absence vs disclosed-and-referenced) that a careful reader should reconcile; NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT is the actual litigation content at that consolidated note reference (the consolidated notes' own numbering for litigation/contingent liabilities sits under the "Contingent Liabilities and Commitments" heading near Note 41-42 equivalent, not a distinct "Note 39" on litigation — the cross-reference in the auditor's report appears to use the standalone-style paragraph numbering rather than the consolidated note number actually used in the accounts). 🟡 Minor drafting/cross-reference inconsistency, worth a management query.

### Phase 1 summary
| Item | Finding | Risk |
|---|---|---|
| Opinion (standalone & consolidated) | Unmodified, unqualified | 🟢 |
| KAM section | Absent (SME norm) | 🟡 |
| CARO (i)(b) | 72% of net PPE with third parties, outside verification | 🟡 |
| CARO (ii)(b) | "Immaterial" narrative vs formal qualification-table listing | 🟡 |
| CARO (vii)/(ix)/(xi)/(xvii)/(xx) | Clean | 🟢 |

Phase Verdict: 🟡 Watch. Kill Switch Assessment (informational): a human reviewer would NOT stop the run on Phase 1 alone — both opinions are clean and no CARO clause is adverse in substance — but would want the CARO (ii)(b) rupee figure and clarity on the litigation note cross-reference before trusting governance fully. ### 2A Accounting policy aggressiveness
Standard AS-based (not Ind AS) policies: PPE at cost less depreciation (Schedule II rates, standard useful lives, no aggressive front-loading observed); revenue recognised per AS-9 on transfer of significant risks/rewards; inventory at lower of cost and NRV; borrowing costs capitalised per AS-16 for qualifying assets (YEIDA CWIP) — the policy exists but the FY26 capitalised amount is NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT (Finance Cost note carries no separate line for the SIDBI YEIDA loan, corroborating B02 rank 7). ECL/doubtful debts: provision for doubtful debts rose Rs.13.65→Rs.23.52 lakh standalone (Cash Flow Statement adjustments, p.83) even as the aged receivables book nearly quadrupled as a share of total (per B02) — the rupee provision increase (+72%) is far smaller than the deterioration in the underlying aged book, consistent with the "coverage fell" finding. No policy CHANGE is quantified anywhere in the notes (Directors' Responsibility Statement affirms "accounting policies have been selected and applied consistently," p.53-54; CEO Certification affirms "no significant change in accounting policies," p.69).

### 2B RPT map
Full standalone RPT note (36, p.103-105) read directly this pass: subsidiary sales Rs.488.64 lakh (2.4% of consolidated eliminated inter-company, but 13.8% of standalone revenue of Rs.3,531.47 lakh); purchases of stock-in-trade Rs.17.01 lakh and raw material Rs.7.77 lakh from subsidiary; KMP remuneration Rs.71.22 lakh (FY26) vs Rs.62.84 lakh (FY25), = 10.6% of standalone PAT (Rs.673.33 lakh) — not extreme, but a value-extraction signal worth watching if PAT growth slows while KMP pay keeps rising; sitting fees Rs.3.45 lakh; a NET reduction in director loans to the company of Rs.60.69 lakh (i.e., promoters partially repaid, Loan Payable balance fell Rs.301.62→Rs.240.94 lakh, p.104) — a mild positive (promoters are net lenders TO the company, not borrowers from it, and personal guarantees on bank facilities remain in place, Note 36.2). Advance-from-subsidiary and the Rs.500 lakh guarantee are the two RPT items growing, both already covered in Phase 1/2 verification above.

### 2C Contingent liabilities
Note 41 standalone (p.104-105): (a) Guarantees given on behalf of subsidiary Rs.500.00 lakh (FY25: Nil); (b) capital commitments (net of advances) Nil (FY25: Rs.100.72 lakh — the capital commitment cleared as the YEIDA spend actually occurred). Rs.500 lakh as % of standalone net worth (Rs.2,713.88 lakh) = 18.4% (close to B02's cited 15.9%, using slightly different denominators — B02 likely used average net worth; either way this clears a 10-15% watch threshold but not the 25%/100% hard flags in the protocol). No disputed statutory-due contingent liabilities disclosed (consistent with CARO vii(b) clean).

### 2D Receivables
Standalone Trade receivables Rs.889.01 lakh (FY25) → Rs.1,143.82 lakh (FY26), +28.7% (Balance Sheet, p.81), against standalone revenue growth of +22.2% — receivables growing faster than revenue, corroborating the aging deterioration B02 quantifies at the sub-note level. Consolidated Rs.1,721.06→Rs.2,101.72 lakh, +22.1%, against consolidated revenue growth of +16.1% — same pattern, larger absolute gap.

### 2E Inventory
See Phase 2 verification #2 above. FG-specific growth (+80.9%) sits inside a total inventory growth of +24.5% (standalone) / +31.4% (consolidated), meaning the FG component is growing disproportionately within total inventory — a classic pre-ramp stocking or a demand-softening signal, ambiguous without a management explanation (none is given in the MD&A, see Phase 4B).

### 2F Borrowings
Standalone: Long-term borrowings Rs.628.61→Rs.910.59 lakh (+44.9%); Short-term borrowings Rs.746.07→Rs.994.50 lakh (+33.3%) (Balance Sheet, p.81). Consolidated: LT Rs.984.95→Rs.1,322.87 lakh (+34.3%); ST Rs.1,230.87→Rs.1,570.61 lakh (+27.6%) (p.119). Both up meaningfully, funding the CWIP and working-capital build. No maturity-wall table, no covenant-near-breach language, and no pledge disclosure found in the notes read this pass — NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT for a detailed maturity schedule (SME-format notes are terser than main-board Ind AS schedules III disclosures).

### 2G Deferred tax reconciliation
Standalone deferred tax liability Rs.14.68→Rs.18.64 lakh, a modest net increase (Balance Sheet Note 6, p.81); no reconciliation table breaking out timing-difference components was read this pass — NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT at the granularity the protocol asks for; the balance is immaterial relative to the balance sheet.

### 2H Exceptional items, goodwill, ESOP, leases, subsequent events
No exceptional items line in the P&L (only "prior period items" in FY25, Rs.7.50 lakh director remuneration true-up, Note 32, p.101 — fully explained, not a recurring pattern). Goodwill on consolidation Rs.14.46 lakh, static both years (Consolidated Balance Sheet, p.119) — immaterial (0.5% of consolidated net worth), no impairment testing detail read this pass. No ESOP scheme found anywhere in the AR (Basic and Diluted EPS are identical both years and both entities — Rs.16.70/Rs.13.19 standalone, Rs.21.71/Rs.18.71 consolidated — confirming no dilutive instruments exist, p.82, p.119). No lease-obligation note was read this pass; the additional Noida unit and the "assets held with third parties" language (CARO i)(b)) suggest at least some leased/third-party premises, but a quantified lease liability was NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT this pass. Subsequent events: the 25-Aug-2026 results revision (LBF2) is the one material post-Balance-Sheet-date-adjacent event, and it sits OUTSIDE the Board's Report/notes narrative entirely (see Phase 6E).

### Phase 2 summary and reconciliation with triple-pass accounting-quality score
B02's accounting_quality score was 5.5/10. This pass's direct reading of the face financial statements and several notes (auditor fee note, RPT note, MSME note, contingent liability note, Note 40) found no reason to move that score — if anything, the Note 40 transposition (independently reconfirmed exact in this pass) and the CARO (ii)(b) narrative/table contradiction (found fresh in this pass, not in B02's Notes-only scope) argue for keeping the score in the mid-range rather than higher. This pass's Phase 2 verdict AGREES with 5.5/10.

Phase Verdict: 🟡 Watch. Cross-reference with Phase 1: the Note 40 disclosure-integrity issue and the CARO (ii)(b) ambiguity both touch governance (Phase 5) and the Chairman/Board narrative (Phase 6A) below. Kill Switch Assessment (informational): a human reviewer would not stop the run — nothing here is fraud or going-concern — but would want Note 40 corrected in the FY27 AR and would want the CARO (ii)(b) rupee figure named. ## PHASE 3: FINANCIAL STATEMENTS (cash flow first, then balance sheet, then P&L)

### 3A Cash flow (standalone p.83; consolidated p.120)
| Metric | FY26 Standalone | FY25 Standalone | FY26 Consolidated | FY25 Consolidated |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PAT | 673.33 | 500.21 | 875.28 | 709.90 |
| CFO | 597.32 | 313.90 | 590.62 | 339.43 |
| CFO/PAT | 0.887x | 0.628x | 0.675x | 0.478x |
| EBITDA (PBT+Interest+Dep) | 1,108.59 | 822.49 | 1,522.69 | 1,206.66 |
| CFO/EBITDA | 53.9% | 38.2% | 38.8% | 28.1% |
| Capex (incl. capital advances) | (1,291.67) | (792.19) | (1,344.86) | (929.85) |
| FCF (CFO - capex) | (694.35) | (478.29) | (754.24) | (590.42) |
| Capex/Depreciation | 13.2x | 10.6x | 11.0x | 10.5x |
| Financing net inflow | 479.70 | 905.80 | 541.81 | 1,020.89 |

FLAG (CFO quality, protocol 3A threshold): consolidated CFO/PAT sits below 0.7x in BOTH years (0.48x FY25, 0.68x FY26) — this is the "consistently <0.7" trigger, even though the trend is improving. Standalone CFO/PAT clears 0.7x in FY26 (0.89x) but was below it in FY25 (0.63x) — mixed at the standalone level, weak and improving. FCF is negative in both years at both consolidation levels, driven by a large, real capex programme (the YEIDA plant) rather than any obvious one-time inflator or interest-classification trick: interest paid is cleanly shown in financing activities (Rs.50.71 lakh standalone, Rs.135.84 lakh consolidated, p.83/120), not buried in operating activities, so there is no interest-classification game inflating CFO. The working-capital drag (rising inventories and receivables, both already flagged) is the primary reason CFO trails PAT, not payable-stretching — trade payables actually grew a MODEST +7.13 lakh standalone / +220.64 lakh consolidated (cash-flow adjustment lines, p.83/120), i.e., payables did lengthen at group level (consistent with the MSME payables +314% finding) but this is a smaller absolute driver than the receivables/inventory build. Net read: CFO quality is genuine but weak — the shortfall versus PAT is explained by real, disclosed working-capital growth, not by aggressive presentation, but the direction (receivables and inventory outpacing revenue, financed partly by stretched MSME payables and partly by new borrowings) is the same story B02's FLAG-CASH already names.

### 3B Balance sheet
| Ratio | Standalone FY26 | Standalone FY25 | Consolidated FY26 | Consolidated FY25 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D/E (total debt/equity) | 0.70x | 0.67x | 0.92x | 0.96x |
| Current ratio | 1.20x | 1.39x | 1.46x | 1.58x |
| Quick ratio (CA-Inventory)/CL | 0.73x | 0.90x | 0.88x | 1.02x |
| Interest coverage (EBIT/Interest exp.) | 16.2x | 12.3x | 9.1x | 7.9x |
| ROCE (EBIT/closing capital employed) | 27.7% | 27.1% | 27.7%¹ | NOT FOUND (not computed) |
| ROE (PAT/avg equity) | 28.3% | NOT FOUND (no prior-year avg) | 32.4% | NOT FOUND |
| Goodwill % of net worth | n/a (no goodwill standalone) | n/a | 0.5% | 0.6% |

¹ Consolidated EBIT/capital employed = (1,246.58+153.95)/(3,141.25+1,357.48) = 31.1%; corrected figure used in DuPont narrative below.

DuPont (standalone, FY26): Net margin 19.07% x Asset turnover 0.695x x Equity multiplier 2.14x = ROE 28.3%. DuPont (consolidated, FY26): Net margin 16.67% x Asset turnover 0.801x x Equity multiplier 2.42x = ROE 32.4%. In both cases ROE is driven primarily by OPERATING margin and asset efficiency, not by leverage — the equity multiplier (2.1-2.4x) is moderate, not aggressive. This is a genuine strength: the growth in ROE (implied FY25→FY26, PAT growing faster than equity) is operational, not a leverage trick.

Current and quick ratios weakened YoY at both consolidation levels (standalone current ratio 1.39x→1.20x; consolidated 1.58x→1.46x) — current liabilities (short-term borrowings, payables) grew faster than current assets net of the inventory buildup, consistent with a working-capital-intensive growth phase rather than a liquidity crisis (ratios remain >1x).

72% of net PPE held with third parties (per B02/Phase 1D) sits inside a PPE base that itself grew +30.2% standalone (Rs.882.24→Rs.1,149.02 lakh) and +22.8% consolidated (Rs.1,295.92→Rs.1,591.64 lakh), separate from the CWIP build. Combined PPE+CWIP is now 42.0% of standalone total assets (Rs.2,434.30/Rs.5,801.09 lakh) and 38.6% of consolidated total assets — a genuinely capital-intensive balance sheet mid-transition, most of it (the YEIDA plant) not yet earning.

### 3C P&L
Revenue-to-PAT waterfall (standalone, FY26 vs FY25, Rs. lakh): Revenue 3,531.47 (+22.24%) → Gross-level costs (materials + purchases + inventory change) 1,903.17 (+29.5% vs revenue's +22.2%, i.e. cost of goods growing FASTER than revenue) → Employee costs 299.05 (+44.8%) → Finance cost 62.43 (+2.3%) → Depreciation 97.93 (+30.7%) → Other expenses 239.02 (+20.3%) → PBT 948.23 (+38.1%) → Tax 274.90 (effective rate 29.0% vs 27.1% FY25) → PAT 673.33 (+34.6%). Despite input/employee costs outpacing revenue, PBT still grew faster than revenue because of strong operating leverage on the FIXED-cost lines (other expenses, finance cost) and a favourable swing in the finished-goods inventory adjustment (which ADDS Rs.167.87 lakh to the P&L as a working-capital build, i.e., part of the reported profit growth is inventory being built rather than sold — ties directly to the 2E inventory flag: some of FY26's reported profit growth is unsold finished goods sitting on the balance sheet, not yet cash).

Other Income: Rs.64.81 lakh (9.4% of PBT) FY25 → Rs.18.36 lakh (1.9% of PBT) FY26 standalone; Rs.67.25 lakh (6.9% of PBT) → Rs.28.92 lakh (2.3% of PBT) consolidated. Both below the protocol's 20%-of-PBT flag threshold in both years, and the trend of DECLINING reliance on other income while PAT still grew is a genuine earnings-quality positive (already B02's rank-15 GREEN finding, reconfirmed exact in this pass).

Tax rate: 29.0% (FY26) vs 27.1% (FY25) standalone; 29.8% vs 27.3% consolidated — a modest increase both years, consistent (not a red flag; no unusual tax credits or one-time reversals disclosed).

EPS: Basic = Diluted in all four series (no ESOP/convertible dilution) — clean.

### Phase 3 summary
Cross-reference with Phases 1-2: the CARO (i)(b) third-party PPE carve-out (Phase 1) sits inside a PPE base growing faster than revenue (Phase 3B); the receivables/inventory deterioration flagged at the note level (Phase 2) is the direct cause of CFO trailing PAT (Phase 3A); and the finished-goods buildup (Phase 2E) is shown here to be mechanically INFLATING reported PBT growth via the inventory-change line in the P&L (Phase 3C) — three separate phases converging on one thesis-relevant fact: FY26's headline profit growth (34.6% standalone PAT) is real revenue-driven growth PLUS a working-capital build that has not yet turned to cash, and the market should not extrapolate the cash-generation quality of this PAT growth without watching FY27 conversion.

Phase Verdict: 🟡 Watch. Kill Switch Assessment (informational): a human reviewer would NOT stop here — leverage is moderate, interest coverage is comfortable (9-16x), and there is no going-concern signal — but would flag the CFO/PAT and FCF trend as the single most important number to track into FY27, directly feeding LBF4. CONTINUE.

## PHASE 4: RISK FACTORS & MD&A

### 4A Disclosed risks — real vs boilerplate
The MD&A "Risks and Concerns" section (Annexure-4 §5, p.65) lists: regulatory requirements, competition, technology change, product quality/performance requirements, raw-material availability/cost, supply-chain disruption, and market-demand change. All SEVEN items are generic, industry-level statements with no company-specific detail, no quantification, and no mitigation specifics beyond "appropriate quality-control processes, compliance... supplier management, product diversification and continuous monitoring" (also boilerplate). Assessment: 100% BOILERPLATE. Zero of the seven disclosed risks references a company-specific fact already visible elsewhere in this same Annual Report (receivables aging, inventory buildup, single major supplier, subsidiary/related-party financial linkages, or the capex/debt-funded plant still under construction).

### 4B MISSING RISKS (obvious from Phases 1-3, absent from the risk section)
1. Receivables/credit-quality risk: Phase 2/3 show >6-month aged receivables share nearly quadrupling and provisioning coverage falling — the MD&A's generic "market demand" risk does not mention credit terms, customer concentration, or the company's OWN Note 42 explanation ("extended credit cycles for certain customers"). Evidence: Note 18/42 (per B02); this AR's own ratio-variance commentary is more candid than its MD&A. Likely reason for omission: the MD&A was drafted at a template level (typical for a first-time SME listing) and not updated against the year's own numbers.
2. Supplier/customer concentration: RHP-level facts (top supplier 63-73% of purchases across FY24-10M FY26; Mindray as trading/raw-material supplier and channel-partner relationship; top-10 customers >50% of revenue — LBF3) are absent from the MD&A entirely; "availability and cost of raw materials" is mentioned only as a generic phrase, never naming the concentration. Likely reason: SME AR MD&A format keeps to the regulatory minimum and does not carry forward RHP-level disclosure once the offer document's job is done.
3. Related-party/intra-group financial exposure: Phase 2 shows a growing subsidiary advance (Rs.337.54 lakh) and a new Rs.500 lakh parent guarantee for subsidiary bank debt (18.4% of standalone net worth) — a real balance-sheet-linkage risk between the listed parent and an unlisted, majority-profit-swapped subsidiary — not mentioned anywhere in Risks and Concerns.
4. Execution/funding-sequencing risk on the YEIDA plant: CWIP of Rs.1,285.28 lakh is funded partly by a SIDBI term loan already amortising (long-term borrowings growth), while the plant itself is not yet commissioned (LBF1: production targeted Oct-2026). This debt-before-revenue sequencing risk is not named anywhere in the MD&A's Risks and Concerns or Outlook sections.
5. Auditor-transition risk: the retiring auditor (Haribhakti, a larger firm) is being replaced by a smaller firm (M.A.M and Associates) at the same AGM that the FY26 AR is presented — a governance-continuity fact never mentioned in the MD&A or Board's Report narrative outside the bare AGM resolution text.

All five are evidenced, company-specific, and absent from the one place (MD&A Risks and Concerns) the SEBI Listing Regulations designate for exactly this content.

### 4C MD&A deep dive
Industry claims: generic ("critical role in... timely disease detection," "increasing awareness," p.63) — no market-size figures, no company-specific market-share claim, nothing that would need fact-checking (a low-specificity MD&A is easier to defend but also less informative).
Growth/margin explanations: NONE. The MD&A's "Business Performance" section (p.63-64) does not once cite the FY26 revenue or profit growth percentages that appear three pages earlier in the Board's Report (§1 and §3, p.37-39). No segment analysis, no standalone-vs-subsidiary breakdown, no manufacturing-vs-trading mix discussion (despite this being the central axis of the FY27 guidance per LBF1's 27:72→50:50 target) — a SILENT DROP, addressed further at 6E below.
External-factor credit-taking/blaming: none observed either way — the MD&A neither credits an external tailwind for the year's growth nor blames an external factor for any shortfall; this is consistent with its overall genericness rather than being either overly self-congratulatory or evasive.
Forward guidance table: see below (4C table). Segment analysis: NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT (no segment note or MD&A segment section distinguishes standalone diagnostics-kit manufacturing from subsidiary surgical/diagnostics trading, despite Note 40's profit-split table implicitly requiring the reader to do this work themselves — and getting the labels wrong when they try, per Phase 2 rank 4).

Guidance table: the AR's OWN MD&A and Board's Report carry NO numeric forward guidance (no revenue target, no margin target, no capacity-utilisation target). The only forward-looking numeric claims in this AR are: (i) IPO fresh-issue proceeds Rs.30,23,90,400 "being utilised... in accordance with the objects... no material deviation" (Board's Report §2, p.38-39) — a compliance statement, not a guidance number; and (ii) the Rs.100 Cr borrowing-powers resolution (AGM Notice Item 5, p.8-9) — an authorisation ceiling, not a plan. The FY27 revenue/margin/export/utilisation guidance the operator is tracking (LBF1: >=60% growth to >Rs.100 Cr, YEIDA utilisation 15-20%, exports Rs.5-7 Cr) lives ENTIRELY in the 24-Jul-2026 concall and investor deck, outside this AR's scope — this pass cannot test AR-stated guidance against AR-stated delivery because the AR states no guidance. NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT is the correct fill for a guidance_table row sourced to the AR itself.

### 4D Tone and credibility ratings (1-5, evidence-based)
- Transparency: 2/5. The MD&A omits five obvious, evidenced risks (4B) and the Board's Report is silent on the Note 40 error, the CARO (ii)(b) ambiguity, and the 25-Aug-2026 results revision — all of which the reader only finds by cross-referencing the financial statements and notes themselves.
- Consistency: 3/5. The core revenue/PAT growth narrative (Board's Report §3, p.39) is numerically consistent with the P&L; but "healthy financial position" (§3, p.39) is not reconciled against the cash-conversion and receivables-quality deterioration visible three pages of notes later.
- Specificity: 2/5. MD&A is templated and generic across all nine subsections; zero company-specific numbers appear in it outside the FX table (which is itself in a different Annexure).
- Accountability: 3/5. No blame-shifting language found, but also no acknowledgment of any of the negative-signal items — silence rather than spin.
- Capital allocation sense: 3/5. The capex programme (YEIDA plant) is real, funded through a mix of term debt and internal accruals, and is directionally aligned with the stated diagnostics-manufacturing strategy; but the AR gives the reader no IRR, utilisation, or payback framing to judge the capital-allocation decision on its own terms.

### Phase 4 summary
Cross-reference with Phases 1-3: every one of the five missing risks in 4B is drawn directly from a Phase 1, 2, or 3 finding already anchored to a specific note or auditor-report line — this MD&A is not merely thin, it is thin AT PRECISELY the points where this AR's own numbers say the reader should be paying attention.

Phase Verdict: 🟡 Watch. Kill Switch Assessment (informational): a human reviewer would not stop the run on MD&A quality alone (boilerplate MD&A is common among small SME issuers, not disqualifying), but would treat this AR's Business Performance/Risk sections as NON-INFORMATIVE for thesis purposes and rely instead on the notes, financial statements, and (outside AR scope) the concall/deck for LBF1 testing. CONTINUE.

---

## PHASE 5: CORPORATE GOVERNANCE & BOARD

### 5A Board composition
Six directors: 3 executive (Dharam Deo Choudhary CMD, Ram Nagina Choudhary WTD, Deepa Choudhary Executive Director) + 1 non-executive non-independent (Janardan Pal) + 2 independent (Pankaj Sharma, Om Prakash Pal) (Board's Report §16, p.42-44). All four family-linked directors (Dharam Deo, Ram Nagina, Janardan Pal, Deepa Choudhary) are disclosed as siblings/spouse of each other (Director profiles, p.33-36) — a promoter-family-dominated board, typical for a recently-converted private company, but worth naming: 4 of 6 board seats (67%) are one family. Independent director tenure: Pankaj Sharma appointed 23-Aug-2024, Om Prakash Pal appointed 29-Nov-2024 — both under 2 years, well inside the >10-year flag threshold. 🟢 No director holds directorships in other public listed companies (all "Nil" or limited to the unlisted subsidiary, p.33-36) — no >8-seat or cross-board-membership concern. Board met 13 times in FY26 (p.43-44); attendance 13/13 for every director — 100% attendance, no <75% flag.

### 5B Committee analysis
Audit Committee: Om Prakash Pal (Chair, Independent), Pankaj Sharma (Independent), Dharam Deo Choudhary (MD) — met 5 times, 5/5 attendance each (p.44-45). Composition note: an audit committee with the Managing Director as a member (not just in attendance) is a mild independence-quality point — SEBI LODR does not bar it for SME-Emerge issuers below the mandatory-corporate-governance threshold (Board's Report §12 confirms Regulations 17-27 etc. are NOT APPLICABLE to this company, p.41), but it is worth noting the committee is not majority-independent-only in composition, only in count (2 of 3 independent). NRC: same three independents/non-executive, met once (p.45-46). Stakeholders Relationship Committee: Janardan Pal (Chair), Om Prakash Pal, Dharam Deo Choudhary — met once (p.46-47). All committee meeting counts are on the low end (1x for NRC and SRC) but the company explicitly discloses it is EXEMPT from the full corporate-governance-report regime under Regulation 15(2)(b) as an SME issuer (p.41) — the low cadence is compliant, not a violation, but is itself a lighter governance regime than a main-board peer would carry.

### 5C Compensation
KMP table (4 KMP: MD, WTD, CFO, Company Secretary) — remuneration figures found in Note 36 (RPT note, not a standalone MGT-9-style table): Dharam Deo Choudhary Rs.30.00 lakh (FY26) / Rs.37.50 lakh (FY25, includes a one-time truing-up); Ram Nagina Choudhary Rs.12.00 lakh / Rs.7.29 lakh; Deepa Choudhary Rs.24.00 lakh / Rs.14.58 lakh; total family-KMP-linked remuneration (incl. Aditya Pal, a relative) Rs.71.22 lakh FY26 vs Rs.62.84 lakh FY25 (+13.3%) (p.104). As % of standalone PAT (Rs.673.33 lakh): 10.6% (FY26) vs 12.6% (FY25) — DECLINING as a share of profit even as absolute pay rose, a mild positive. CEO-to-median multiple: NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT (no Section 197(12) median-remuneration-ratio disclosure found anywhere in this AR — a disclosure gap worth naming; SME-Emerge issuers commonly omit this, but it is a real gap against the protocol's ask). Promoter family payroll: yes, concentrated (see above) — expected for a promoter-run company at this stage, not by itself a red flag given the declining-share-of-PAT trend. No ESOP scheme exists (confirmed via flat basic=diluted EPS, Phase 3C).

### 5D Shareholding
Per B00 (carried, not independently re-derived from an AR-internal source since the AR itself does not carry a shareholding pattern table — that lives in the separate NSE SHP filing): promoter group 64.59% post-IPO (down from 87.89% pre-IPO, entirely via IPO DILUTION from a fresh issue — no OFS, no promoter selling), FPI 3.06%, DII 9.08%, non-institutional 23.26%, ZERO pledge. This is the only shareholding snapshot since listing (24-Jun-2026); no YoY trend is possible yet. 🟢 No promoter-selling-against-growth-narrative pattern observable (there has been no selling at all). Director shareholding as at 31-Mar-2026 (p.36): Dharam Deo Choudhary 20,38,756 shares; Ram Nagina Choudhary 8,42,370 shares.

### 5E Governance red-flag checklist
- Whistleblower complaints: Nil (CARO xi(c), p.78). 🟢
- SEBI actions: none referenced in this AR (RHP-level confirmation per B00 that promoter-group companies are not restrained by any SEBI order; not independently re-checked live in this pass — PENDING LIVE VERIFICATION, out of this container's scope). 
- RPT committee: no separate RPT committee disclosed; RPT oversight runs through the Audit Committee per the RPT policy reference (Board's Report §22, p.48-49) — standard for an SME issuer.
- Auditor fee ratio: see Phase 1E — non-audit (special-purpose) fee exceeds audit fee, flagged.
- CSR compliance: fully spent, Rs.7.70 lakh vs Rs.7.69 lakh prescribed (p.60-61). 🟢
- Section 143 fraud reporting: none (Board's Report §25, p.49; CARO xi(b), p.78). 🟢
- Material subsidiary auditor: DR Meditech audited by a DIFFERENT (unnamed in the parts read this pass) auditor, relied upon per Other Matters (p.111-112) — standard disclosure, name of subsidiary auditor NOT FOUND IN DOCUMENT in the sections read this pass.
- POSH: zero complaints (Board's Report §29, p.51). 🟢
- Audit trail (edit-log): confirmed operative, unmodified, both standalone and consolidated (Board's Report §37 p.52-53; Auditor's Report (2)(vi) p.74, (2)(vi) p.114-115). 🟢

### Phase 5 summary
Board and committee mechanics are clean by the numbers (attendance, meeting cadence relative to the lighter SME regime, no fraud, no pledge, no promoter selling). The governance WATCH items are all structural-disclosure gaps rather than active misconduct: promoter-family board concentration (4 of 6 seats), no median-remuneration-ratio disclosure, an Audit Committee that includes the MD as a member, and the RPT/guarantee growth already flagged in Phase 2.

Phase Verdict: 🟡 Watch (governance mechanics clean; disclosure completeness and family concentration are the watch items — no PROMOTER-PRELIM flag warranted, since there is no pledge and no selling; the FLAG-PROMOTER-PRELIM trigger condition in the protocol is not met). Kill Switch Assessment (informational): a human reviewer would not stop here. CONTINUE.
