# FILE: `runs/avience-2026-09-26/outputs/reports/06-peers.md`

Source: `run/avience-2026-09-26`

# STAGE 6: PEER CONCALL VERIFICATION — AVIENCE (2026-09-26)

Protocol v1.1. Company: Avience Biomedicals Ltd (AVIENCE). Peers: Q-Line Biotech
Ltd (QLINE, closest structural mirror), Molbio Diagnostics Ltd (MOLBIO, closed-loop
destination model), Tarsons Products Ltd (TARSONS, adjacent lab-consumables maker
with a real multi-quarter new-plant ramp record). 6 transcripts total against a 12-
transcript contract ceiling: QLINE and MOLBIO are recent listings with only one call
each; that is stated, not treated as a gap in "using" them.

Identity/quarter confirmed from page 1-2 of each transcript, not the filename:
- QLINE-Concall_Jun_2026_Transcript.txt: Q-Line Biotech Limited, "H2 and FY26
  Earnings Conference Call," 23-Jun-2026 [page 2]. Its only call.
- MOLBIO-Concall_Sep_2026_Transcript.txt: Molbio Diagnostics Limited, "Q1 FY27
  Earnings Conference Call," 08-Sep-2026 [page 2]. Recently listed (BSE 544866);
  its first call as a listed company.
- TARSONS-Concall_Nov_2025_Transcript.txt: "Q2 & H1 FY'26 Earnings Conference
  Call," held 13-Nov-2025 [page 2].
- TARSONS-Concall_Feb_2026_Transcript.txt: "Q3 & 9-Months FY26 Earnings
  Conference Call," held 06-Feb-2026 [page 2].
- TARSONS-Concall_Jun_2026_Transcript.txt: "Q4 & FY'26 Earnings Conference
  Call," held 25-May-2026, filed 01-Jun-2026 [page 1-2].
- TARSONS-Concall_Aug_2026_Transcript.txt: "Q1 FY'27 Earnings Conference Call,"
  held 11-Aug-2026 [page 1-2].

## PART 1: CLAIM-BY-CLAIM VERIFICATION (B05.peer_questions)

### Q1. Export revenue swing comparable to Avience's FY25->FY26 -89.9% collapse (Rs
524.30 lakh to Rs 53.11 lakh)?

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Claim | Avience's FY26 manufactured-goods export base collapsed -89.9% YoY, unremarked on the H2/FY26 call. |
| Verdict | UNVERIFIABLE (no peer discloses a comparable single-year collapse) |
| Peer evidence | QLINE: exports "approximately INR1.2 crores" FY26, targeting 5x growth FY27, no collapse language (QLINE, H2/FY26 call, 23-Jun-2026, transcript p.6, p.8-9). TARSONS: exports declined -13.4% in Q4 FY26 alone (Middle East conflict, container/freight disruption) but full-year FY26 standalone exports still grew ~3% YoY (TARSONS, Q4/FY26 call, 25-May-2026, transcript p.2-3); Q2 FY26 also saw an export dip from BL-issuance delay (TARSONS, Q2/H1 FY26 call, 13-Nov-2025, transcript p.4). Exports then rebounded +29% YoY in Q1 FY27 (TARSONS, Q1 FY27 call, 11-Aug-2026, transcript p.4). MOLBIO: Q1 FY27 export revenue ~Rs67 crore (16% of revenue), described as including "a catch-up of some of the deferrals which happened in Q4 FY26 due to the situation in Gulf" (MOLBIO, Q1 FY27 call, 08-Sep-2026, transcript p.11) — a swing, but a multi-quarter deferral/catch-up pattern, not a single-year 90% base collapse. |
| Peers silent | None of the three discuss a single-year export collapse of Avience's magnitude; all three describe macro-driven quarter-to-quarter volatility (freight, tariffs, geopolitics) in the 10-30% range, not a 90% base wipeout. |
| Net read | The sector shows real export volatility from external shocks (Middle East conflict, US tariffs, container/BL delays), but nothing near Avience's scale. This makes Avience's -89.9% swing look anomalous versus the sector's experience, not a common industry pattern the FY27 Rs5-7 Cr guidance can lean on for cover. |

### Q2. Government tender order sizes, award-to-billing lag, payment-cycle terms
comparable to Avience's cited 45-120 days?

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Claim | Avience management cites 45-120 day payment terms on government-linked orders (implicitly behind the ~Rs 47 Cr Uttarakhand order). |
| Verdict | PARTIALLY VERIFIED (one peer corroborates the receivable-day range; neither peer gives tender-size or award-to-billing-lag detail) |
| Peer evidence | QLINE: FY26 receivable days ~134, trending toward a targeted 90-120 day cycle; ~65% of revenue is B2G, billed through a UP-based distributor who supplies government labs under a PPP/turnkey model (QLINE, H2/FY26 call, 23-Jun-2026, transcript p.11-14). MOLBIO: trailing-12-month receivable days are much tighter, 87 days in June 2026 vs 86 in March 2026 (MOLBIO, Q1 FY27 call, 08-Sep-2026, transcript p.9); its TB testing runs on a "two-year rate contract" with the government, just renewed (transcript p.20), which is a contract-structure detail, not a billing-lag figure. |
| Peers silent | Neither peer discloses tender award-to-billing lag or typical individual order size in rupee terms; MOLBIO's receivable cycle is notably faster than either QLINE's or Avience's own stated terms. |
| Net read | QLINE's 120-150-day receivable range is directionally consistent with Avience's 45-120 day claim and gives it a plausible sector anchor; but MOLBIO's much tighter 87-day cycle shows government-linked collection speed varies widely by contract structure, so Avience's number cannot be assumed typical without its own tender terms disclosed. |

### Q3. Does Molbio (or a peer) disclose reagent-rental/closed-system margins
comparable to Avience's claimed 40-60%, and closed-loop revenue share?

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Claim | Avience claims 80-90% closed-loop reagent mix and 40-60% rental margins as its core margin engine. |
| Verdict | PARTIALLY VERIFIED (direction corroborated by two peers; Avience's margin band sits below both peers' disclosed ranges) |
| Peer evidence | MOLBIO: blended gross margin "60% to 63%" across devices and test kits, closed system by design ("our reagents, our consumables work only on our devices and vice versa... no third-party kits can be used on the device") (MOLBIO, Q1 FY27 call, 08-Sep-2026, transcript p.16-17); ~75% of revenue currently from recurring test kits (transcript p.9). QLINE: manufactured-reagent gross margin 60-65%, traded-reagent margin ~50%, instrument manufacturing margin 15-20%, blended 52-55%; management states 75-80% of business is "closed system" (QLINE, H2/FY26 call, 23-Jun-2026, transcript p.11-12, p.16). |
| Peers silent | Neither peer breaks out a standalone "rental margin" line the way Avience frames its 40-60% band; both report reagent/kit gross margins clustering at the higher 50-65% end. |
| Net read | Both peers corroborate that closed-loop reagent/kit business is high-margin (50-65%) and that a 75-90% closed-loop revenue mix is achievable and sector-typical (QLINE 75-80%, MOLBIO ~75% via test kits, vs Avience's claimed 80-90%). But Avience's claimed 40-60% margin band sits at or below the bottom of both peers' disclosed ranges, making the low end of Avience's claim plausible but its "closed-loop margin engine" framing look conservative-to-optimistic depending on where in the 40-60% range actual delivery lands; nothing here confirms the high end. |

### Q4. Tarsons's capex-to-utilisation ramp timeline for Panchla vs Avience's YEIDA
15-20% FY27 utilisation guidance?

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Claim | Avience guides YEIDA plant utilisation at ~15-20% in FY27, its first year of commercial production. |
| Verdict | PARTIALLY VERIFIED (single peer, but exceptionally direct and repeated corroboration of both the ramp-curve shape and, coincidentally, the exact first-year percentage) |
| Peer evidence | TARSONS gave the most load-bearing evidence in this run, across all four of its transcripts: (1) initial capex plan Rs550-650 Cr for Panchla+Amta, guided full commissioning by Q4 FY26, ramp for genuinely new product lines "3- to 5-year timeline," existing-product capacity expansion utilised faster at "2- to 3-year timeline" (TARSONS, Q2/H1 FY26 call, 13-Nov-2025, transcript p.11-12); (2) explicit year-by-year curve: "15%, 20% capacity going in the first year, 30%, 35% by year 2" for new product lines (TARSONS, Q3/9M FY26 call, 06-Feb-2026, transcript p.11-12); (3) by the Q4/FY26 call, the "entire capex program" was pushed to "fully commissioned during the first half of the current financial year" (H1 FY27) rather than by Q4 FY26 as earlier guided — a real slippage on the company's own prior guidance (TARSONS, Q4/FY26 call, 25-May-2026, transcript p.4-5); (4) by Q1 FY27, that slipped again: "Panchla commercial commissioning has been delayed... shifted to second half of FY'27," with management citing "the sheer scale and size and the number of projects" and multiple external-vendor-dependent commissioning issues, and Panchla+Amta now guided to reach only 20-25% of standalone revenue by FY28, two years after the plant was first meant to be complete (TARSONS, Q1 FY27 call, 11-Aug-2026, transcript p.7-8, p.16). |
| Peers silent | QLINE and MOLBIO gave no comparable new-plant ramp-timeline data (QLINE's Unit 4 commentary describes utilisation targets but not a granular year-by-year curve for a wholly new facility with no operating history; MOLBIO has no major greenfield ramp underway, running at 58%/42% utilisation on existing lines). |
| Net read | Tarsons's own disclosed ramp curve (15-20% year 1, 30-35% year 2) matches Avience's stated FY27 utilisation guidance almost exactly in shape, which makes the 15-20% number itself plausible and not obviously optimistic as a standalone figure. But the same peer's lived experience shows the commissioning DATE itself slipped twice in nine months (Q4 FY26 -> H1 FY27 -> H2 FY27) even after the plant was substantially built — directly relevant to B00 LBF1's "ramp realism" test and a specific reason to treat Avience's Oct-2026 YEIDA commercial-production date as at meaningful risk of slippage, independent of whether the eventual utilisation percentage proves right. |

### Q5. Industry growth rates, raw-material cost trends, CDSCO approval-timeline
experience comparable to Avience's claims?

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Claim | Avience claims IVD-diagnostics market growth broadly in line with peers, and a CDSCO approval average of ~3 months vs a sector-normal 6-9 months. |
| Verdict | PARTIALLY VERIFIED (industry growth rate) / UNVERIFIABLE (CDSCO approval-timeline; raw-material trend not transferable) |
| Peer evidence | Growth rate: QLINE states "the diagnostic market... is growing by 8% to 9%" (QLINE, H2/FY26 call, 23-Jun-2026, transcript p.10); MOLBIO cites a global point-of-care diagnostics TAM of ~USD30 billion growing at 17.9% CAGR (MOLBIO, Q1 FY27 call, 08-Sep-2026, transcript p.5) — global, not India-specific, but directionally supportive of a growing category. Raw material: TARSONS discusses severe polymer/resin price spikes (25-70% Feb-Aug 2026, driven by the Middle East conflict and Asia-sourced feedstock) with partial, lagged pass-through (TARSONS, Q4/FY26 and Q1 FY27 calls) — but this is plastics/polymer input cost, a different commodity base from Avience's diagnostic-reagent chemistry, so not transferable evidence for Avience's specific inputs. CDSCO approval timelines: none of the three peers discloses a specific regulatory approval-cycle duration; QLINE references getting "NOD" from CDSCO for new instruments but gives no month count. |
| Peers silent | No peer discloses a CDSCO (or equivalent) approval-timeline metric in months; this sub-claim remains wholly untestable from this corpus. |
| Net read | The general "diagnostics/IVD demand is growing at a healthy mid-to-high single digit or better pace" framing is loosely corroborated (QLINE's India-specific 8-9%; MOLBIO's larger but global figure). Avience's specific regulatory-speed claim (3 months vs 6-9 sector-normal) has zero independent evidence either way and should not be treated as verified on plausibility per the mandatory rule. |

## PART 2: UNPROMPTED CROSS-READ

**2A Demand environment.** All three peers describe FY27 as a recovery/growth year
after a subdued FY25-26: QLINE guides ~30-35% FY27 revenue growth off a 9% FY26 print
(QLINE, transcript p.4-5); MOLBIO guides ~25% topline growth, explicitly non-linear
because it "operate[s] in public health" (MOLBIO, transcript p.9-10); TARSONS
delivered a 21% Q1 FY27 growth surprise after several tepid years and management now
frames "15%" as an achievable baseline with upside from new products (TARSONS, Q1
FY27 call, transcript p.9, p.12-13). This is a broadly consistent, sector-wide
picture of improving demand into FY27, which loosely supports Avience's optimistic
framing of the period. But none of the three peers guides anywhere close to
Avience's >=60% FY27 growth target; Avience's number is an outlier in scale even
against a genuinely improving sector backdrop, and that gap is exactly what rests on
the single unverified Uttarakhand order.

**2B Pricing and input costs.** No peer discusses diagnostic-reagent-specific raw
material trends. TARSONS (plastics/polymer inputs) reports a sharp, geopolitically
driven cost spike with only partial, lagged pass-through and continued domestic
price competition from post-COVID overcapacity; QLINE and MOLBIO report margin
EXPANSION over the period (QLINE EBITDA margin 22.5% -> 28.7%; MOLBIO EBITDA margin
holding near 24-25%), with no commentary on reagent/chemical input cost pressure.
This gives no direct read on Avience's own margin narrative, but shows that within
this peer set, the businesses closer to Avience's reagent/instrument category
(QLINE, MOLBIO) were margin-expanding in the same window Avience reports flattish-
to-improving margins, which is at least not contradictory.

**2C Capex cycle.** QLINE (4th manufacturing facility commissioned FY26, planning
further utilisation) and TARSONS (Rs550-650 Cr multi-year Panchla/Amta build, still
mid-ramp) are both active capacity expanders, similar to Avience's YEIDA build.
MOLBIO is the outlier: it explicitly states it needs "minimal capex" to double
revenue given 58%/42% existing utilisation on test kits/devices, deploying only
~Rs72 Cr of its Rs200 Cr IPO proceeds to automation rather than new capacity (MOLBIO,
transcript p.6, p.14). Read together: this is an industry-wide capacity-building
period, not a lone-expander situation — two of three peers are mid-capex-cycle at
the same time as Avience, which is the less alarming of the two scenarios named in
the brief, but it does NOT mean the capex will convert on schedule (see Q4 above).

**2D Competitive mentions.** No peer mentions Avience, Mindray, or an unmistakable
description of either across any of the six transcripts. Silence noted, not treated
as evidence either way.

**2E Risks peers discuss that Avience's own materials do not.**
- TARSONS names a structural government-procurement risk not raised anywhere in
  Avience's corpus: the Government e-Marketplace (GeM) "L1" lowest-bid process has
  "significantly brought down business volumes" for government-linked lab-consumable
  sales over 5-7 years, with government now only ~15-20% of the ~Rs1,300 Cr Indian
  plastic labware market versus a materially higher historical share, because
  research institutes have "abolished rate contracts" in favour of per-purchase GeM
  bidding (TARSONS, Q1 FY27 call, 11-Aug-2026, transcript p.17-18). This is directly
  relevant to B00 LBF1's government-order-plausibility test: it is a live example, in
  an adjacent lab-consumables business, of government-channel revenue proving less
  durable and less linear than a single "order in hand" headline implies.
- MOLBIO's TB testing runs on a periodically renegotiated "two-year rate contract"
  with the government (just renewed), a renewal-risk structure Avience's Uttarakhand
  order narrative never addresses (no stated contract duration or renewal terms).
- QLINE discloses that a material share of its business runs through a related-party
  distributor (its largest, UP-based distributor is a promoter entity) and that it
  is actively winding down historical corporate guarantees extended to related
  parties (QLINE, transcript p.13-14) — a related-party governance risk category
  structurally analogous to (though not identical to) Avience's own related-party
  exposure to its subsidiary DR Meditech (B00 LBF3).
- TARSONS names acute, externally driven raw-material and logistics volatility
  (Middle East conflict, container/freight availability, US tariff uncertainty) as a
  recurring, multi-quarter margin and export-timing risk. Avience's call does not
  discuss import/logistics risk on its Mindray-sourced traded reagents/instruments at
  all, despite ~72% of FY26 revenue being traded goods.

## PART 3: PEER COVERAGE MAP

| Peer | Quarter | Used how | Key contribution |
|---|---|---|---|
| QLINE | H2/FY26 (23-Jun-2026) | SUBSTANTIVE | Reagent/instrument margin bands (60-65% mfg reagent, ~50% traded reagent, 15-20% instrument), 75-80% closed-loop mix, receivable days ~134 trending 90-120, related-party distributor and corporate-guarantee wind-down, India diagnostic market growth 8-9%. |
| MOLBIO | Q1 FY27 (08-Sep-2026) | SUBSTANTIVE | Closed-system reagent economics (60-63% blended margin, ~75% recurring test-kit revenue), receivable days ~87, minimal-capex growth path (contrast to Avience's capex-heavy model), two-year government rate-contract structure, global POC TAM growth 17.9% CAGR. |
| TARSONS | Q2/H1 FY26 (13-Nov-2025) | SUBSTANTIVE | Baseline Panchla/Amta capex plan (Rs550-650 Cr), initial 3-5 year new-product ramp timeline vs 2-3 year for capacity-expansion products, 80% utilisation on existing plant. |
| TARSONS | Q3/9M FY26 (06-Feb-2026) | SUBSTANTIVE | Explicit year-by-year ramp curve: 15-20% capacity utilisation year 1, 30-35% year 2 — the single most load-bearing peer data point in this run, directly comparable to Avience's own YEIDA FY27 guidance. |
| TARSONS | Q4/FY26 (25-May-2026) | SUBSTANTIVE | First disclosed commissioning slippage: full commissioning pushed from Q4 FY26 to H1 FY27; raw-material cost spike and margin compression detail. |
| TARSONS | Q1 FY27 (11-Aug-2026) | SUBSTANTIVE | Second disclosed commissioning slippage (H1 FY27 -> H2 FY27); Panchla+Amta guided to reach only 20-25% of standalone revenue by FY28; GeM government-procurement structural risk. |

All six transcripts are SUBSTANTIVE; none are CITED-ONLY or UNUSED. The two peers
below the 12-transcript ceiling (QLINE, MOLBIO) are recent listings with one call
each by construction, not an analytical gap in how they were used.

## PART 4: TRIANGULATION SUMMARY

- Claims verified (two-plus independent peer corroboration): 0 of 5.
- Claims partially verified: 4 of 5 (Q2 payment terms, Q3 margin/mix, Q4 YEIDA ramp
  timeline, Q5 growth rate sub-claim).
- Claims contradicted: 0 of 5.
- Claims unverifiable: 1 of 5 outright (Q1 export-collapse comparability), plus one
  named sub-claim within Q5 (CDSCO approval-timeline).
- Single most consequential finding (not a contradiction, but the highest-value
  result): Tarsons's own disclosed Panchla ramp curve (15-20% year 1, 30-35% year 2)
  makes Avience's YEIDA utilisation number itself look sector-plausible, but the same
  peer's commissioning date slipped twice within nine months even on a plant that
  was substantially built — a direct, evidenced reason to discount the Oct-2026
  YEIDA commercial-production date's reliability independent of the utilisation
  percentage.
- Single strongest independent confirmation: QLINE and MOLBIO both disclose closed-
  loop/reagent gross margins in the 50-65% range and closed-loop revenue mixes of
  75-80%, which corroborates the general shape of Avience's razor-and-blade claim
  (though not its specific 40-60% margin band, which sits at or below both peers'
  ranges).
- Overall: the peer set COMPLICATES the main company's narrative rather than cleanly
  supporting or undercutting it. It lends real, if partial, support to two of the
  quieter numbers (the YEIDA utilisation percentage, the general reagent-margin
  shape), while adding no corroboration at all to the single largest and most load-
  bearing claim (the ~Rs 47 Cr Uttarakhand order remains untouched by any peer
  evidence) and surfacing two risks — GeM-style government-procurement erosion and
  capex-commissioning slippage — that Avience's own call and AR do not address.

## PART 5: CROSS-PEER HYPOTHESIS

All three peers with a recurring-revenue "placed-hardware, sold-consumable" model
(QLINE and MOLBIO directly; TARSONS at one remove through its cell-culture/bioprocess
push) anchor the credibility of that model in specific, disclosed UNIT ECONOMICS:
QLINE gives per-analyzer lifetime value (~Rs 25,000-30,000/month reagent spend,
~Rs 30 lakh lifetime value over a 10-year life, split by analyzer type); MOLBIO gives
device counts, tests-per-disease, and a stated device replacement cycle (still zero
replacements after 6-7 years); TARSONS, while not a razor-blade business, discloses
concrete asset-turnover assumptions (0.7-0.8x) and per-facility breakeven revenue
figures for its own capex. Avience discloses none of the equivalent numbers for its
own placed-instrument base: no average reagent revenue per placed instrument, no
count of currently-active rental contracts, no per-instrument lifetime value (flagged
as NOT FOUND in B04's unit_economics block). The testable hypothesis: sector practice
at this business-model stage is to disclose per-unit economics as the evidentiary
backbone of a recurring-revenue thesis, and Avience's failure to disclose any
equivalent figure is not merely a data gap but a signal that its own placed-
instrument base and reagent attach-rate may be too small, too recent, or too poorly
tracked internally to support the same disclosure standard its comparable peers
already meet at a similar or earlier stage of listing. This should be tested directly
by asking management for active-installation count and per-instrument reagent
run-rate at the next opportunity (already flagged in B04's mgmt_questions); if the
company cannot produce it, the razor-and-blade claim stays structurally unverifiable
regardless of how many quarters pass.

# STAGE 7: EMERGING MOAT SCAN, 22 CATEGORIES — AVIENCE BIOMEDICALS LTD
Run date: 2026-09-26 | Model: claude-sonnet-5
Taxonomy note: this is the Emerging Competitive Advantages scan (22 categories + R1). It is NOT FTTCP. FTTCP is a separate synthesis inside the valuation stage. The two are never conflated in this run.

Sources read this stage: FY26 Annual Report (144pp, text), RHP (535pp, text + PNG renders for image-only pages), H2/FY26 concall transcript (24-Jul-2026), Investor Presentation (29pp, all pages read, most image-only), B01-gate0, B02-notes, B03-ardeep, B04-bizmodel, B05-concall (promise-delivery record), B00-inputs.

LOAD-BEARING FACTS CHECKED FIRST (B00 LBF1-LBF4): LBF1 (guidance vs delivery, Uttarakhand/PSU order) is directly load-bearing for this scan's H2 and catalysts sections. LBF3 (Mindray concentration, top supplier 63-72.7% of purchases) is load-bearing for B2/H2. LBF4 (cash conversion) informs the G1/G2 negative findings below. None of the four load-bearing facts changed on this pass; all cross-check consistently.

## SECTION 1: FUTURE PRODUCT & REVENUE STREAM ANALYSIS

Pres. slide 19); WHO Prequalification is a distinct, product-specific, higher bar not yet held anywhere in the corpus |

Note on evidence discipline: the deck's WHO-GMP certificate (slide 19, dated Nov-2023) certifies the EXISTING Noida facility's manufacturing practice, predating the YEIDA plant by nearly three years. It must not be read as evidence that the new plant is WHO-validated; the call (p.302-304) and RHP (p.121) are explicit that WHO Prequalification for the new plant is still "subject to audit, verification and certification" / "after trial run." This distinction is load-bearing for Section 4.

### 1B Diversification direction

- **Product**: incremental (more CDSCO-licensed IVD SKUs within existing categories: biochemistry, rapid test, haematology, molecular, microbiology). Evidence: 📄 (licence counts, deck slide 14) + 🎙️ (call p.71, p.181-190). Timeline: near-medium (FY27 target 175 products).
- **Customer**: B2G expansion (government tenders, state-government engagement, the ~₹47 Cr / ₹47.46 Cr Uttarakhand/PSU order). Evidence: 🎙️ (call p.92-95, p.107) + 📄 (Inv. Pres. slide 23, names the order as "from a PSU"). No Reg 30 filing corroborates it (carried from B05 FLAG-UNVERIFIED-ORDER). Timeline: near (FY27).
- **Geographic**: exports to >6 countries (RHP p.34: Dubai, Ethiopia, Ghana, Somalia, Nepal, Zambia); FY27 export guidance Rs 5-7 Cr (🎙️ call p.4) set against a FY26 base that COLLAPSED to 1.01% of revenue (Rs 53.11 lakh) from 18.15% (Rs 524.30 lakh) FY25 (📄 Inv. Pres. slide 28, cross-confirms B02's Note 39(b) finding). This is diversification narrative running directly against a documented deterioration, not a clean tailwind.
- **Channel**: managed-laboratory model (see 1A) would add a direct-service channel; still concept-stage.
- **Vertical integration**: manufacturing:trading mix shift target ~27:72 to ~50:50 (🎙️ call p.237-240, p.266-268), funded by the new YEIDA plant. This is the single largest declared diversification vector and the one most load-bearing for the FY27 guidance.

### 2B Utilisation trajectory per facility

- Existing Noida facility (rented, 3,700 sq ft per RHP): Rapid Test utilisation 84.12% (10M FY26), Reagents 85.23%, Analysers 80.57%, Culture Media 74.53% (RHP pdf p.169-170, 📄). Already running near-full on most lines, which is the stated rationale for the new plant.
- YEIDA new facility: management guides FY27 utilisation at only 15-20% (🎙️ call p.253), against a peak-capacity claim of ~₹250-265 Cr. Existing facility to be discontinued 3-4 months after the new plant stabilises (🎙️ call p.234-236).

### 2C Growth embedded in capex: arithmetic

- Historical fixed-asset turnover (FY26): Revenue from Operations ₹52.51 Cr / net PPE ₹15.92 Cr (Inv. Pres. slide 27, consolidated) = **3.30x**.
- Capex under execution (RHP total project cost, the incremental YEIDA investment): ₹30.18 Cr.
- Implied incremental revenue at the historical turnover ratio: ₹30.18 Cr × 3.30 = **₹99.6 Cr**.
- As a percentage above current (FY26) revenue: (₹99.6 Cr / ₹52.51 Cr) − 1 ≈ **+90% above current revenue**.
- **capex_embedded_growth_pct = 90** (rounded; arithmetic method, 📄 turnover inputs from the deck's own balance sheet slide).
- 🔍 ANALYST INFERENCE and flag: this fixed-asset-turnover-derived figure (+90%) is materially BELOW management's own "peak capacity" framing of ₹250-265 Cr (+376% to +405% over FY26 revenue, call p.206). The gap is explained by FY27's guided 15-20% utilisation of the new plant: full nameplate capacity is a multi-year, back-half-loaded outcome, not a near-term one. Both figures should be carried to Stage 11/FTTCP with this reconciliation attached, not just the larger management number.

### 2D New geography or market entries

- Uttarakhand: new state territory via the ~₹47 Cr / ₹47.46 Cr order (🎙️/📄 deck slide 23 names it a PSU order; unverifiable per B05 FLAG-UNVERIFIED-ORDER — no Reg 30 filing, no customer name, no tender reference anywhere in the 18-filing NSE announcements list since listing).
- North India Mindray exclusivity (Delhi NCR + eastern UP is the CURRENT territory; discussion is for an EXPANDED North India exclusive) — 🎙️ claim, unsigned.
- Export markets: >6 countries already served per RHP (Dubai, Ethiopia, Ghana, Somalia, Nepal, Zambia) but the revenue base collapsed to 1.01% of FY26 revenue; FY27 guidance of Rs 5-7 Cr is a recovery/expansion claim against a depressed base, not a new-market entry claim.

## SECTION 3: THE 22-CATEGORY SCAN

**FAMILY A, PRODUCT & TECHNOLOGY**

- **A1 rare manufacturing capability**: NO EVIDENCE FOUND. CDSCO manufacturing licensing (9-12 month average timeline, deck slide 13) is a genuine regulatory barrier but is standard for the IVD-manufacturing peer set (QLINE, MOLBIO also hold CDSCO licences); no evidence of a globally scarce capability, unusually long qualification cycle versus peers, or long-lead specialised equipment. This category's "rare manufacturing capability" test (global manufacturer count, 12-36 month qualification) is not met; the licence-barrier evidence is more properly a Section 4 (R1) regulatory-moat item, scored there.

- **A2 patent and IP pipeline**: NO EVIDENCE FOUND. No patent filing, IP licensing revenue, or lab partnership is disclosed anywhere in the AR, RHP, call or deck. The RHP's own risk factors (line 2974, 3011) discuss third-party patent infringement RISK to Avience, never an Avience-owned patent.

- **A3 process innovation**: NO EVIDENCE FOUND. No yield disclosure, unit-cost trend, or automation metric is quantified. The deck's manufacturing/QC slide (11) shows a largely manual assembly process (gloved hand-packing of rapid-test kits) alongside some automated equipment; no cost-per-unit or yield trend is given.

- **A4 product platform / modular architecture**: WEAK. SKU count growth (88 approved -> 105 with the 17 recently added -> targeted 175 by end-FY27 -> 288+ longer-term per deck) without any disclosed R&D headcount or R&D-spend growth (B00 M6: "no R&D-to-revenue figure disclosed anywhere in the corpus"). 🎙️ management claim (call p.87-90, deck slide 22). Strength: Weak. Time to materialise: near-medium (FY27).

**FAMILY B, SUPPLY CHAIN**

- **B1 backward integration and RM security**: WEAK-MODERATE. Country-wise raw-material procurement shows China's share falling from 31.33% (FY24) to 22.47% (FY25) to 2.99% (10M FY26), with India rising to 97.01% (RHP pdf p.217-218, table reproduced in this stage's research). 📄 documented, three-year consistent trend. This is genuine import-substitution / supply-security evidence, though it is a sourcing-mix shift, not captive raw-material production or a long-term supply agreement. Strength: Moderate. Time to materialise: already underway.

- **B2 qualification lock-in**: MODERATE, WITH A CAVEAT. ISO 13485:2016, ISO 9001:2015, CE (UK Cert Ltd), and WHO-GMP compliance certificates are held for the existing facility (📄 Inv. Pres. slide 19, dated 2023, valid to 2025-2027 per the certificate details shown). The reagent-rental model places 80-90% of instruments under closed-loop, barcoded, 5-year contracts (🎙️ call p.163-165, p.246-250: "generally for five years... the customer purchases reagents and consumables from us at agreed prices"). CAVEAT: this mechanic is ALREADY substantially reflected in FY26 financials — traded + manufactured reagents/consumables together are 74.45% of FY26 revenue (B04 revenue_streams) — so it is better classified as an EXISTING moat already counted in Gate 0's moat_score (18, STRONG per B01), not a newly-forming one. The EMERGING piece is only the incremental scale-up via the new plant's larger closed-loop base and the still-unsigned Mindray exclusivity/OEM discussion (captured separately in the optionality register, not double-scored here per the CLAUDE.md rule against crediting one improvement twice). Strength: Moderate (existing mechanic); Weak (emerging incremental piece). Time to materialise: ongoing / near for the incremental piece.

- **B3 supply chain network effect**: NO EVIDENCE FOUND. Avience is a channel partner/distributor for Mindray and a manufacturer selling through dealers; no platform, hub-and-spoke, or fragmented-supply-aggregation structure is described anywhere.

**FAMILY C, CUSTOMER**

- **C1 customer ecosystem / embedded relationships**: NO EVIDENCE FOUND (as a category distinct from B2). AMC/CMC service revenue exists but is 0.64% of FY26 revenue (call p.267-268) and is explicitly "modest yet growing" only since FY25 (RHP p.169-170). No ERP/workflow integration, no disclosed multi-product cross-sell rate, and the switching-cost mechanic (reagent-rental lock-in) is already scored under B2. Scoring C1 separately here would double-credit the same underlying fact per the CLAUDE.md rule; this category is left at NO EVIDENCE FOUND for scoring purposes.

- **C2 customer concentration improving**: NO EVIDENCE FOUND. Top-10 customers are >50% of revenue with names undisclosed for confidentiality (RHP pdf p.34); no multi-year trend of top-5/10 concentration DECLINING is disclosed. B00/B04 note only that the top RAW-MATERIAL supplier's share is declining (63.0%/66.3%/72.7%, a supplier-concentration fact, not a customer-concentration fact); the two must not be conflated.

**FAMILY D, DATA & DIGITAL**

- **D1 proprietary data asset**: NO EVIDENCE FOUND.
- **D2 digital platform**: NO EVIDENCE FOUND.

**FAMILY E, GEOGRAPHIC & ACCESS**

- **E1 geographic first-mover**: NO EVIDENCE FOUND. The YEIDA Medical Device Park land allotment (2100 sq. m, allotted 17-Apr-2023, RHP pdf p.100-101) is documented, but no source claims Avience is the first, or among the first, occupants of the park, nor does it claim a logistics advantage over named competitors. The call (p.308-309) itself lists FOUR competing medical device parks (YEIDA, Ujjain, Visakhapatnam, Nalagarh) receiving similar government support, undercutting any first-mover claim.

- **E2 China+1 beneficiary**: NO EVIDENCE FOUND — and the honest finding runs the OPPOSITE direction. Manufactured-goods export FOB value collapsed -89.9% YoY (Rs 524.30 lakh to Rs 53.11 lakh, standalone, AR Note 39(b), independently confirmed by the deck's own Revenue Mix slide 28 showing exports falling from 18.15% to 1.01% of total revenue FY25 to FY26). No new international certification, no supply-chain-shift language crediting Avience as a beneficiary, and no analyst on the single call asked about the collapse (carried from B05 FLAG-SILENT-BASE). This is scored 0 and flagged, not force-fit as a tailwind.

**FAMILY F, TALENT & ORGANISATIONAL**

- **F1 talent density**: NO EVIDENCE FOUND — and a negative structural fact. The RHP states explicitly the Company "does not have any Employee Stock Option Scheme or other similar scheme" (RHP pdf p.209, line 14781). No R&D headcount, PhD count, or specialist-retention mechanic is disclosed anywhere (B03 input_gaps). The RHP's "Talented Workforce" competitive-strength claim (RHP pdf p.208) is generic boilerplate with zero named individual, headcount, or credential evidence, and is explicitly the kind of "management quality" narrative Amendment 20-Aug-2026's I1 test requires to be scored 0.

- **F2 execution moat**: WEAK. Mixed record: 43 new production licences were obtained on schedule during FY24-25 per the RHP's own capex-rationale narrative (RHP pdf p.106, 📄), and the company added 17 more licences by the July-2026 call (🎙️). Against this, the YEIDA plant's intermediate construction sub-milestone slipped ~3 months (June to September 2026, per B05 timeline_slippages, cross-referenced against AR Note 13.2's "not overdue" assertion), and the single promise-delivery data point available (B05, no-concall mode) shows 0 delivered / 1 partial / 0 missed against a company nine weeks into its first quarter as a listed issuer — genuinely UNTESTED, not proven. Strength: Weak. Time to materialise: the first real test is the H1 FY27 result due by Nov-2026 (per B05), after this run date.

**FAMILY G, FINANCIAL & STRUCTURAL**

- **G1 war chest**: NO EVIDENCE FOUND — negative finding. FCF is negative in ALL THREE years shown (FY24 approx -Rs 2.88 Cr, FY25 -Rs 5.91/-5.90 Cr, FY26 -Rs 7.54/-6.94-7.54 Cr across standalone/consolidated per B01/B03), consolidated CFO/PAT below 0.70x in both years disclosed (0.478x FY25, 0.675x FY26, B03). This is the direct opposite of a war chest; growth is externally funded (SIDBI debt + IPO proceeds), not internally generated.

- **G2 WC improvement trajectory**: NO EVIDENCE FOUND — negative finding. WC days WIDENED FY25 to FY26 (186.8 to 199.8 days per B01), debtor days worsened 124 to 146, and receivables aged >6 months nearly quadrupled as a share of the book (10.2% to 38.9% standalone) while provisioning coverage on that aged book FELL (15.8% to 8.3%, B02 top finding #1). This is a clear deterioration, not an emerging improvement.

**FAMILY H, ECOSYSTEM & EXTERNAL**

- **H1 industry consolidation beneficiary**: NO EVIDENCE FOUND. No competitor exit, compliance-driven shakeout, anti-dumping measure, or bolt-on acquisition by Avience is disclosed. The deck's industry-outlook slide (16) names Roche, Abbott, Siemens Healthineers, Transasia and Beckman Coulter as the sector's leading players — all larger, entrenched incumbents, not exiting competitors.

- **H2 strategic partnerships**: WEAK. The existing Mindray channel-partner relationship is real and documented (RHP pdf p.169, Inv. Pres. slide 4/17: "Authorized Distributor for Mindray in India") but explicitly NON-EXCLUSIVE and reallocable at Mindray's discretion (call p.136-139; carried as FLAG-CONCENTRATION in B04). The genuinely emerging piece — an exclusive North-India Mindray arrangement plus an OEM/private-label deal — is "in discussions" only, with no signed term sheet, no timeline, and no confirm signal beyond a future Reg 30 filing (B05 trigger #6, conviction "L"). Strength: Weak. Time to materialise: medium (B05 puts a kill signal at "no deal disclosed by FY28").

- **H3 ESG moat**: WEAK. A ZED (Zero Defect Zero Effect) Gold certificate under the Ministry of MSME's sustainable-manufacturing scheme is documented (Inv. Pres. slide 19, certificate issued to Avience Biomedicals Private Limited, dated 10-Feb-2024). No renewables percentage, no SBTi commitment, no ZLD, and no customer-imposed ESG requirement is disclosed. Strength: Weak (a real credential, but narrow and not customer-demand-driven). Time to materialise: already in place, immaterial to differentiation.

**FAMILY I, STRUCTURAL ASYMMETRIES**

- **I1 talent asymmetry (Category 21)**: SCORE 0, BY DESIGN. Leg (a) — no named inventor, no ex-DRDO/HAL/global-major concentration, no AR remuneration annexure showing above-norm technical pay is found anywhere in the corpus (the AR itself carries no Section 197(12) median-remuneration disclosure, B03 input_gaps). Leg (b) — no competitor pay-scale-ceiling arithmetic exists to test. The RHP's own "Talented Workforce" language is the textbook "strong team" narrative the operator ruling requires to be scored 0, not credited as evidence.

- **I2 cannibalization barrier (Category 22)**: SCORE 0. Testing every moat claimed in this scan against the "what would the best-resourced competitor have to destroy" test: the CDSCO licence pipeline requires no destruction, only time and capital (Roche/Abbott/Siemens could apply for the same licences without breaking their own economics — this is execution lead, not configuration). The Mindray channel-partner relationship is Avience's dependency on Mindray, not a barrier protecting Avience FROM a competitor; a rival channel partner in a different territory faces no destruction requirement to compete for the SAME Mindray relationship elsewhere. The reagent-rental lock-in binds EXISTING placed customers for the life of their 5-year contracts but does not require a competitor to destroy anything internally to win the NEXT placement. The honest answer across every claimed moat in this scan is "nothing must be destroyed" — this is Amendment 20-Aug-2026's textbook execution-lead pattern, not a configuration-level cannibalization barrier. Scored 0.

📄 recount performed: 5 documented items across 4 categories (B1: country-wise procurement table showing China share collapse; B2: ISO/CE/WHO-GMP certificate suite; H3: ZED Gold certificate; R1: YEIDA land allotment + CDSCO licence count). This is well inside the 3-6-category base rate the completionist guard describes; no re-examination for over-crediting management claims as documented was triggered.

---

## SECTION 4: REGULATORY & POLICY TAILWINDS (Category R1)

### 4A Regulatory approvals in pipeline

| Body | Status | Timeline | What it unlocks | Which competitors have it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CDSCO Manufacturing Licence (new YEIDA plant) | To be applied for, after installation of plant and machinery (RHP pdf p.121-122) | After Oct-2026 installation | Legal manufacture at the new site | QLINE, MOLBIO and other CDSCO-licensed IVD makers already hold equivalent licences for their own plants; not a differentiator |
| ISO 13485:2016 / ISO 9001:2015 (new plant) | To be applied for, after installation | Post-installation | Continued quality-system certification | Industry-standard; most organised peers hold these |
| CE (new plant) + WHO:GMP (new plant) | To be applied for, after installation | Post-installation | EU market access; WHO health-mission tender eligibility (framed qualitatively) | Not disclosed for peers |
| WHO Prequalification, 2 products | To be applied for, "after trial run" | Post-commissioning, undated | Access to markets/tenders requiring WHO-PQ (e.g., UN procurement) | 📄 no peer WHO-PQ status found in this corpus; NOT FOUND |
| European CE (Directorate-General for Communication, EU) | To be applied for, after trial run | Undated | EU market access for new-plant output | NOT FOUND for peers |

🔍 ANALYST INFERENCE: every new-plant regulatory approval in this table is POST-commissioning and undated beyond "after installation" or "after trial run." None is a documented approval in hand today; all are pipeline/pending. This materially tempers the "strong regulatory entry barrier" framing on deck slide 20 ("Manufacturing license approval involves lengthy regulatory processes, creating strong entry barriers for new players") — the barrier argument is generically true of the CDSCO regime for ANY new entrant, Avience included, and is not a demonstrated Avience-specific edge over existing peers who already cleared the same bar years ago.

### 4B Government policy tailwinds

| Scheme | Amount/benefit | Duration | Enrolment status | Do competitors share it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| YEIDA Medical Device Park incentives (concessional land, ~₹3.50/unit electricity, ESI/PF employer-contribution support, ~7% interest subsidy on eligible P&M funding, international-exhibition and NABL product-testing expense support) | Quantum not disclosed at rupee level in the AR or RHP; only described qualitatively by management on the call (🎙️ call p.305-318) | Per applicable scheme conditions, not specified | Avience holds land allotment inside the park (📄 documented, RHP pdf p.100-101); the SPECIFIC subsidy quanta received/accrued are NOT FOUND at rupee level anywhere in this corpus | Yes, explicitly — the call names FOUR competing medical device parks (YEIDA, Ujjain in Madhya Pradesh, Visakhapatnam, Nalagarh) receiving comparable government support (call p.308-309); this is a shared policy tailwind across an entire industrial-policy category, not an Avience-specific edge |
| PLI Scheme for Pharmaceuticals (covers in-vitro diagnostic devices among other categories) | Incentives on incremental sales for 6 years, tenure FY 2020-21 to FY 2028-29; FIVE industry applicants were selected nationally (RHP pdf, Industry Overview chapter, lines 8452-8456) | FY21-FY29 | Avience is NOT named among the five selected applicants anywhere in this corpus | This is national industry commentary, not an Avience-specific benefit; flagged here explicitly so it is not later mis-read as a company tailwind |
| Make in India / import substitution (deck slide 17, "well positioned to benefit from import substitution & Make in India") | Qualitative only | Ongoing | No quantified benefit disclosed | Shared across the entire domestic-manufacturing IVD sector |

### 4C Regulatory moat assessment

Active vs emerging: the company's CURRENT regulatory position (88 CDSCO licences, existing-facility ISO/CE/WHO-GMP certs) is an ACTIVE moat already reflected in Gate 0's moat_score (18, STRONG per B01) and in the current P&L. The EMERGING piece — new-plant certifications, WHO Prequalification, and the medical-device-park subsidy quantum — is entirely undated pipeline, contingent on the Oct-2026 commissioning and a subsequent, unscheduled "trial run." Time to kick in: medium (FY27-28 at the earliest for WHO-PQ; unknown for the specific subsidy rupee amount). Sustainability: the park-level subsidies are shared with named competitors in other parks running the same central/state schemes, so they do not confer a durable Avience-specific edge; the licence-count barrier is a real cost-of-entry for ANY new entrant but does not differentiate Avience from its already-licensed direct peers (QLINE, MOLBIO).

---

## SECTION 5: EMERGING MOAT SCORECARD

Scoring: raw score from likelihood × impact (HH=4, HM/MH=3, HL/MM/LH=2, ML/LM=1, LL=1, no evidence=0) × evidence quality (📄 1.0x, 🎙️ 0.7x, 🔍 0.5x).

| A3 | Process innovation | — | 0 | — | 0 |
| A4 | Product platform | M/M | 2 | 🎙️ 0.7x | 1.4 |
| B1 | Backward integration | M/M | 2 | 📄 1.0x | 2.0 |
| B2 | Qualification lock-in | H/H | 4 | 🎙️ 0.7x (core quantum management-claimed; certs documented but quantum not) | 2.8 |
| B3 | Network effect | — | 0 | — | 0 |
| C1 | Customer ecosystem | — | 0 (not separately scored; see B2 caveat) | — | 0 |
| C2 | Concentration improving | — | 0 | — | 0 |
| D1 | Data asset | — | 0 | — | 0 |
| D2 | Digital platform | — | 0 | — | 0 |
| E1 | Geographic first-mover | — | 0 | — | 0 |
| E2 | China+1 beneficiary | — | 0 (contra-evidence) | — | 0 |
| F1 | Talent density | — | 0 (negative: no ESOP) | — | 0 |
| F2 | Execution moat | M/M | 2 | 🎙️ 0.7x | 1.4 |

| I2 | Cannibalization barrier | — | 0 (by design) | — | 0 |

| **TOTAL** | | | | | **11.4 ≈ 11** |

I1/I2 contribution to the total: 0 (neither fired). No threshold crossing via I1/I2 points on this run; nothing to add to the operator's 10-15-scan review-checkpoint list.

**em_score = 11. Classification: <12 -> NO MEANINGFUL EMERGING MOAT.**

This is a borderline result (11.4 rounds to 11, one point below the 12-24 MODEST band). The operator should treat B2's "Moderate (existing mechanic)" tier as the swing item: if the reagent-rental lock-in's incremental (new-plant-driven) scale-up is judged to independently clear the HM bar with 📄-tier evidence once the YEIDA plant commissions and placement counts are disclosed, this would move into MODEST MOAT DEVELOPMENT. Today, with placement counts, per-instrument reagent revenue, and the plant not yet commercial, the evidence does not support that tier.

---

## OPTIONALITY REGISTER

|---|---|---|---|
| Mindray North-India exclusive distribution + OEM/private-label arrangement | A signed distribution/OEM agreement, disclosed with territory and product scope | Reg 30 filing; next AR's related-party/major-contract note | Medium (B05 kill signal: no deal by FY28) |
| ~₹47 / ₹47.46 Cr Uttarakhand/PSU order "in hand" | A Reg 30 order-win filing, named customer/tender reference, or a Uttarakhand/govt-linked revenue line in H1 FY27 results | NSE Reg 30 filing; H1 FY27 half-yearly results | Near (H1 FY27 results due by Nov-2026) |

---

## SECTION 6: TIMELINE, RISKS & COMBINED ASSESSMENT

### 6A Moat evolution timeline

- **Next 12 months (to Sep-2027)**: YEIDA plant commissioning (Oct-2026, watch the CWIP-to-PPE capitalisation entry); H1 FY27 results (Nov-2026) as the first test of ALL FY27 guidance including the Uttarakhand order, export ramp, and mix shift; possible Reg 30 filing confirming or denying the ₹47/47.46 Cr order.
- **12-24 months**: manufacturing:trading mix reaching (or failing to reach) ~50:50; new-plant CDSCO/ISO/CE licensing completing; Mindray North-India exclusivity/OEM decision (or its absence); FY28 revenue aspiration (~₹160-165 Cr) tested against actual FY27 delivery.
- **24-36 months**: WHO Prequalification outcome (if pursued to completion); YEIDA plant utilisation trajectory beyond the guided 15-20% FY27 level; managed-laboratory model funding decision, if any.
- **3-5 years**: full YEIDA nameplate utilisation (management's ~₹250-265 Cr peak-capacity claim) tested against the fixed-asset-turnover-derived ~₹99.6 Cr incremental-revenue arithmetic in Section 2C; whether the reagent-rental installed base has scaled enough to disclose per-unit economics.

### 6B Risks to each top-scoring emerging moat

| Moat | Early warning sign of failure |
|---|---|
| B1 backward integration (China sourcing decline) | China share reversing upward in the next disclosed period; RM cost inflation forcing a return to China suppliers |
| B2 qualification lock-in (reagent-rental, existing mechanic) | Placed-instrument churn at contract renewal (5-year terms mean the first meaningful renewal test is years away); Mindray reallocating the channel-partner territory, which would remove the supply of instruments to place |
| R1 regulatory/policy tailwinds | New-plant licensing delays beyond Oct-2026; park subsidy quantum turning out to be immaterial once disclosed; a competing medical device park (Ujjain/Vizag/Nalagarh occupant) capturing the same tender opportunities Avience is chasing |
| F2 execution moat (weak) | A second timeline slippage beyond the already-observed 3-month construction slip; H1 FY27 growth materially below the >=60% guided rate |
| H2 strategic partnerships (weak) | No Mindray exclusivity/OEM deal signed by FY28 (B05's own kill signal) |

### 6C Combined Gate 0 + Emerging Moat table

| Metric | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Gate 0 core_score | 69 | B01 |
| Gate 0 moat_score | 18 | B01 |
| Gate 0 moats_confirmed | 4 | B01 |
| Gate 0 moat_class | STRONG | B01 |
| Gate 0 classification (backward) | GOOD (capped by deal-breaker: Block B <8, FCF negative all 3 years) | B01 |
| Emerging Moat em_score | 11 | This stage |
| Emerging Moat classification (forward) | NO MEANINGFUL EMERGING MOAT (<12) | This stage |

### 6D Combined classification

Backward GOOD + forward NO MEANINGFUL EMERGING MOAT does not fit the transition-alpha setup this operation hunts (a GOOD or AVERAGE backward score paired with an EXPANSION or STRENGTHENING forward score). With no forward moat expansion to layer on top of an already deal-breaker-capped backward GOOD, the combined read is **GOOD** (unchanged from Gate 0), not an upgrade. This is NOT a HIGH POTENTIAL or TURNAROUND case: the backward financial picture already carries a hard cap (FCF negative three straight years, Block B 2/20) that the forward scan does not offset, because the forward scan finds mostly PIPELINE and CLAIM-tier evidence, not documented, executing moat expansion. The two candidate "moats" scoring Moderate (B1 backward-integration sourcing shift, R1 regulatory tailwind) are real but narrow and partly shared with competitors; neither rises to a rung-migration-scale re-rating engine per the CLAUDE.md Quality Ladder framework.

### 6E Final output card

**Moat evolution map (existing -> emerging, per family):**
- Family B (Supply Chain): existing reagent-rental/closed-loop mechanic (already ~74% of FY26 revenue, counted in Gate 0) -> emerging incremental scale via new-plant capacity and RM sourcing shift away from China (weak-moderate, underway).
- Family H (Ecosystem): existing non-exclusive Mindray channel partnership -> potential emerging exclusive North-India/OEM arrangement (weak, unsigned, discussion-stage only).
- Family R (Regulatory): existing 88-licence CDSCO base and existing-facility WHO-GMP/ISO/CE certs -> emerging new-plant licensing and WHO Prequalification (weak-moderate, entirely pipeline, undated beyond "post-commissioning").
- All other families (A, C, D, E, F, G, I): NO EVIDENCE FOUND, or negative findings (FCF, WC days, exports, ESOP absence) that run against an emerging-moat narrative rather than supporting one.

**Catalysts to watch in the next 12 months:** YEIDA plant commercial production and PPE capitalisation (Oct-2026); H1 FY27 results as the first hard test of the >=60% growth / >Rs 100 Cr guidance (due Nov-2026); Reg 30 filing (or continued absence of one) confirming the ~₹47/47.46 Cr Uttarakhand/PSU order; manufacturing:trading mix disclosure in H1 FY27; FY27 export FOB value against the collapsed FY26 base.

**Biggest risk to the emerging moats:** the entire forward narrative is concentrated in ONE unverified order (the Uttarakhand/PSU contract), ONE capex project running on a fixed October deadline that has already shown a 3-month intermediate slip, and cash-conversion metrics moving in the WRONG direction (WC days widening, receivables ageing worsening, provisioning coverage falling) even as the growth story is being told. If the Uttarakhand order underperforms or the YEIDA plant slips further, there is no war chest (FCF has been negative for three straight years) to fund a delay; the company would need to draw further external financing at exactly the point its own cash-conversion metrics are already deteriorating.

---

## ANALYST NOTE

The scan found a genuinely thin emerging-moat picture, consistent with a nine-week-old listed company running its first-ever earnings call. Three-to-six category base rate held (3 Moderate, 4 Weak, 15 None/negative, 23 rows total); the completionist guard was not triggered. The single most important interpretive decision in this pass was NOT crediting the reagent-rental/closed-loop mechanic (B2) as a fully emerging moat: at ~74% of FY26 revenue, it is already an EXISTING moat counted in Gate 0's moat_score, and only its incremental new-plant-driven scale-up belongs in this forward-looking scan. Two other honest negative findings deserve emphasis for the operator: (1) the FY27 export guidance is framed against a base that collapsed 89.9% YoY, which the company's own investor deck confirms (exports fell from 18.15% to 1.01% of revenue) yet no analyst on the call asked about; and (2) the RHP's own "Talented Workforce" and "Innovative Product Portfolio" competitive-strength language is generic and evidence-free, precisely the pattern the I1 operator ruling (20-Aug-2026) designed the 0-score test to catch. The em_score of 11 sits one point below the MODEST MOAT DEVELOPMENT band; this is a genuine borderline case, not a rounding artefact to be argued away, and is flagged as such for the operator.

---

# Stage 8: Promoter Background Check — Avience Biomedicals Ltd (AVIENCE)

Run date: 2026-09-26. Company: Avience Biomedicals Ltd (formerly Avience
Biomedicals Pvt Ltd), CIN L74999DL2019PLC359158, NSE Emerge SME (symbol
AVIENCE), listed 25-Jun-2026, ISIN INE0V9I01017. No BSE code.

Evidence taxonomy used throughout: VERIFIED (official filing/order/record),
MEDIA REPORTED (credible media, unconfirmed), FORUM/SOCIAL (unverified
signal), UNVERIFIED (single/dubious source, flagged).

---

## SECTION 1: PROMOTER IDENTITY & FAMILY MAPPING

### 1A Family tree, roles, tenure, holdings, other directorships

Four promoters, all related as siblings/spouses [VERIFIED, RHP pdf p.264,
269, "Relationship of Promoters with our Directors" and "Family
Relationship Between KMP"]:

| Name | DIN | Role | Age (RHP) | Pre-issue holding | Relationship |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dharam Deo Choudhary | 02804625 | Chairman cum Managing Director | 58 | 20,38,756 sh / 50.57% | Spouse of Deepa Choudhary; brother of Ram Nagina Choudhary and Janardan Pal |
| Ram Nagina Choudhary | 02804667 | Whole-Time Director | 46 | 6,42,370 sh / 15.93% | Brother of Dharam Deo and Janardan; brother-in-law of Deepa |
| Janardan Pal | 08647037 | Non-Executive Director | 43 | (part of family; RHP p.264 shows "10.43%" line under a director table, cross-check pending) | Brother of Dharam Deo and Ram Nagina; brother-in-law of Deepa |
| Deepa Choudhary | 09267149 | Executive Director | 37 | 4,38,400 sh / 10.88% | Spouse of Dharam Deo; sister-in-law of Ram Nagina and Janardan |

Two independent directors complete the six-member board: Om Prakash Pal
(DIN 10841456, Audit Committee Chairperson) and Pankaj Sharma (DIN
07077130) [VERIFIED, FY26 AR p.3, RHP p.264-265]. Family holds 4 of 6
board seats and both executive leadership roles (Chairman/MD and
Whole-Time Director) [VERIFIED, AR p.3].

Founder history: Dharam Deo Choudhary founded DR Meditech Surgical and
Diagnostics (India) Pvt Ltd in 2010 (trading in diagnostics/surgical
equipment); Ram Nagina Choudhary was a founding member of the same entity.
Avience itself was incorporated 23-Dec-2019 [VERIFIED, RHP pdf p.264,
269]. DR Meditech became a wholly owned subsidiary of Avience on
08-Aug-2023 via a share purchase agreement, paid for in Avience equity at
Rs 125/share issued 21-Jul-2024 [VERIFIED, RHP pdf p.87-88]. This is a
founder-promoter structure with no succession dispute or inheritance
event found in the corpus or on the web.

Other directorships found: Dharam Deo Choudhary and Ram Nagina Choudhary
are directors of DR Meditech (the subsidiary) [VERIFIED, RHP]. Deepa
Choudhary is promoter/director of Imperative Healthcare Pvt Ltd
[VERIFIED, RHP pdf p.273-274]. Web search (MCA/Tofler/Zauba) surfaced no
other, unrelated directorships for any of the four promoters and no
struck-off or dormant company under their names; this is a search-depth
limitation (public search engines index only a fraction of MCA director
master data), not a positive clearance — recorded as a gap, not a clean
finding, under 1B below. [❓ UNVERIFIED by web search absence; ✅ VERIFIED
by RHP's own promoter-group Group Company disclosure, which is
authoritative and complete for SEBI ICDR purposes.]

### 1B Promoter group entities

RHP's own "Our Promoters" and "Our Group Companies" chapters are the
authoritative source (SEBI ICDR-mandated disclosure) [VERIFIED, RHP pdf
p.271-276]:

- **Imperative Healthcare Pvt Ltd** — the sole disclosed "Group Company."
  Incorporated 08-Aug-2021, CIN U33119DL2021PTC384695. Promoters: Deepa
  Choudhary and Geeta Choudhary (50:50), same business line (diagnostic
  instruments/reagents trading). Tiny: FY25 total income Rs 110.23 lakh,
  PAT Rs 0.84 lakh, net worth Rs 2.27 lakh [VERIFIED, RHP pdf p.274]. Not
  listed, not defunct, not sick, no SEBI restraint (RHP declaration).

- **Dharam Deo Choudhary HUF** and **Ram Nagina Choudhary HUF** — named

Non-compete agreements dated 26-Nov-2024 exist between Avience and
**Ayush Diagnostics & Surgicals** and **Biocorn Healthcare** only

## SECTION 2: LEGAL & REGULATORY RECORD

### 2A SEBI actions

RHP declares, and web search corroborates by absence of any adverse hit:
no SEBI order, no market ban, no insider-trading or manipulation
proceeding, no wilful-defaulter or fugitive-economic-offender
designation, against the Company, its Promoters, Directors, or Group

2026-09-26 found no contradicting media or SEBI-order-list hit]. Caveat:
this container has no live SEBI SCORES/enforcement-order database access;
the web-search clearance is corroborative, not exhaustive. ✅ CLEAN,
subject to that caveat.

### 2B Criminal cases

One criminal proceeding **against** the Company: Akash Dubey vs Avience
Biomedicals Pvt Ltd, CC NI ACT/560/2026, Rs 81,791, a Negotiable
Instruments Act (cheque) matter, pending [VERIFIED, RHP pdf p.384]. This
is a routine trade-payment dispute, not an economic-offence or fraud
matter.

The Company and its subsidiary DR Meditech have themselves **filed**
roughly 16 NI Act / civil-recovery suits against customers/debtors for
unpaid dues, amounts ranging from Rs 18,615 to Rs 8,18,956 per case
[VERIFIED, RHP pdf p.385-398]. This pattern — the company chasing many
small debtors through court rather than the reverse — corroborates
B02/B03's FLAG-CASH finding (receivables deterioration, aged-book share
rising, provisioning coverage falling) with primary litigation evidence:
customers are not paying, and management is resorting to court recovery
at meaningful volume. This is an operating/cash-conversion signal, not a
promoter-integrity finding.

One truncated RHP text fragment under "Criminal proceedings against the
Promoters & Directors" reads only "Disposed (Uncontested – Lok Adalat)
Case stands disposed of in Lok Adalat" with no case name, number, or
amount legible, immediately followed by the boilerplate "there are no
outstanding criminal proceedings…except as stated below" [RHP pdf p.394].
This reads as an already-resolved historical matter but the extraction is
corrupted. ❓ UNVERIFIED — flagged, not treated as fact; likely immaterial
given it is already disposed, but named here rather than silently
dropped.

No PMLA, ED, FEMA, SFIO, or DRI matter found against the Company, its
subsidiary, promoters, or directors, in the corpus or on the web.

RHP's Tax Proceedings table shows NIL for the Company and subsidiary

No RBI, IRDAI, TRAI action found. No MCA compounding or director

No family partition suit, no oppression petition, no JV dispute, no
defamation suit found. The only civil matters are the trade-recovery
suits catalogued under 2B (both directions), which are business-as-usual
SME litigation, not promoter-vs-promoter or promoter-vs-minority
disputes.

### 2F Legal red flag summary table

| Sub-area | Finding | Rating |
|---|---|---|
| SEBI | No action found | ✅ |
| Criminal | 1 minor NI Act case against company (Rs 81,791); ~16 NI Act/recovery cases filed by company/subsidiary; 1 unresolved extraction fragment re: promoters (❓) | ✅ (company/promoter integrity); ⚠️ (receivables-collection pattern, cross-refs FLAG-CASH) |
| Tax | 1 small resolved-in-favour matter (CMD); NIL elsewhere | ✅ |
| Other regulators | None found | ✅ |
| Civil | Only routine trade-recovery suits | ✅ |

---

## SECTION 3: BUSINESS CONDUCT & ETHICAL TRACK RECORD

### 3A Related-party transaction history

- Trade balances with Ayush Diagnostics, Imperative Healthcare, and
  Biocorn Healthcare all **declined** FY25 to FY26 (see 1B) — not a
  growing extraction pattern.
- New Rs 500.00 lakh corporate guarantee, Avience (parent) for DR
  Meditech's (subsidiary) ICICI cash-credit facility, Nil in FY25
  [VERIFIED, AR Note 41/36, corroborating B02/B03's FLAG-RPT]. This is
  15.9-18.4% of standalone net worth depending on the base used across
  B02/B03's two computations. Intra-group (parent-subsidiary, both
  wholly promoter-controlled), not a related-party leakage to an outside
  entity, but it does expand cross-obligations inside the group at a
  material scale relative to net worth.
- Advance from subsidiary (DR Meditech) to parent grew Rs 160.09 lakh to
  Rs 337.54 lakh (+110.9%) [VERIFIED, AR Note 10/36, corroborating B00
  LBF3 and B02/B03].
- Rs 51.77 lakh of related-party trade receivables (Ayush, Imperative,
  Biocorn combined) is disclosed **only at the consolidated level**; the
  standalone note states no such receivables exist [B02/B03
  FLAG-RPT/DISCLOSURE, cross-referenced here]. A reader of the standalone
  financial statements alone would miss a genuine related-party exposure
  visible only in the consolidated set.
- Personal guarantees: Dharam Deo Choudhary, Ram Nagina Choudhary, Kapil
  Deo Choudhary (a promoter-group relative, not himself a promoter),
  Deepa Choudhary, Janardhan Pal, and Sumitra Devi (a relative) have
  given personal guarantees on ICICI Bank credit facilities [VERIFIED,
  RHP pdf p.383]. Promoters standing personally behind company debt is a
  mild positive alignment signal (skin in the game), not a red flag.

### 3B Capital allocation behaviour

- Dilution history: rights issues in 2021-2023 (all to the four
  promoters, pro-rata-ish), a 1:3.379 bonus issue in Mar-2023, a
  loan-to-equity conversion (21-Jul-2024, Rs 125/share, to Dharam Deo and
  Ram Nagina Choudhary for the DR Meditech share-purchase consideration),
  and a private placement (03-Aug-2024, also Rs 125/share, to 19 outside
  investors including Stockify Fintech Pvt Ltd — a known, web-verified
  pre-IPO/unlisted-shares platform — and NPRS Financial Services Pvt Ltd,
  which web search could not independently verify) [VERIFIED for pricing
  and allottee list, RHP pdf p.86-88; ❓ UNVERIFIED for NPRS Financial
  Services' identity/track record]. **The promoter loan-conversion and
  the outside private placement used the identical Rs 125/share price on
  overlapping dates** — the promoters did not receive a cheaper price
  than the arm's-length round. This is a governance positive: no evidence
  of promoter-favourable cheap allotment ahead of the IPO.
- The Rs 125/share pre-IPO price sits at roughly 60% of the eventual Rs
  208 IPO price, about 11-22 months later — a normal SME pre-IPO markup,
  not an extreme one.
- No promoter selling in the IPO (Fresh Issue only, no OFS component
  found in the corpus); promoter stake fell mechanically from 87.89%
  pre-issue to 64.59% post-issue purely through dilution [VERIFIED, B00,
  RHP].
- No dividend history exists yet (company converted to public form
  03-Sep-2024; FY26 AR is the first as a listed entity) — too early to
  assess dividend-vs-hoarding conduct.
- Borrowing powers sought to Rs 100 Cr at the 30-Sep-2026 AGM (Item 5,
  Section 180(1)(c)) [VERIFIED, AR pdf p.7-9]. This is a headroom
  resolution, not evidence of imminent over-leverage by itself, but
  combined with FY26's already-negative FCF (B02/B03) it is a
  capital-discipline item to watch, not yet a finding against the
  promoters.

### 3C Promoter share transactions

Zero pledge, zero non-disposal undertaking, zero other encumbrance,
across the entire promoter and promoter-group holding, per the NSE
shareholding pattern XBRL as on 24-Jun-2026 (the only pattern filed since
listing) [VERIFIED, shareholding/NSE_SHP_AVIENCE fields
`WhetherAnySharesHeldByPromotersAreEncumberedUnderPledged = false`, etc.,
across all four encumbrance-type flags]. 100% of promoter-group shares
are locked-in (3,542,885 of 3,542,885) [VERIFIED, same source]. No
creeping acquisition, no sale-timed-to-highs pattern possible yet (three
months of listed history, no post-listing pattern filed). ✅ CLEAN.

### 3D Minority treatment

No unfair delisting attempt, no RPT bypassing minority approval beyond
the standalone/consolidated disclosure gap already named in 3A, no
proxy-advisory (IiAS/SES/InGovern) coverage found (expected: recently
listed SME below their typical coverage threshold), no SCORES complaint
found on web search (search-depth caveat applies), no AGM controversy
found.

### 3E Auditor relationship

Haribhakti & Co. LLP (FRN 103523W/W100048) was appointed at the 2nd AGM
in 2021 for a five-year term and **retires at the 30-Sep-2026 AGM by
completion of that term**; the Reg 30 board-meeting-outcome filing
(07-Sep-2026) records the incoming auditor M.A.M and Associates (FRN
015680N) under "Reason for change: **Appointment**," not resignation
[VERIFIED, announcements/AVIENBIO_07092026184854 pdf, lines 100-105].
**This does NOT meet the deal-breaker bar of "auditor resignation within
3 years"** — it is a standard end-of-term rotation matching the exact
five-year tenure disclosed. Web search could not independently verify M.A.M
and Associates' track record, size, or client history (❓ UNVERIFIED —
no search hits beyond the AGM notice itself); this is a downgrade in name
recognition (a recognised mid-tier firm to an unverified small firm) even
if procedurally clean, and is worth the operator's own direct check.

Non-audit fee **exceeds** audit fee in both years shown: Statutory Audit
Rs 3.00 lakh vs Special Purpose Audit Rs 4.50 lakh (FY26); Rs 3.00 lakh
vs Rs 2.50 lakh (FY25) [B03, AR Note 31, corroborated here]. "Special
Purpose Audit" plausibly reflects IPO-related certification work in the
listing year, but the gap widened even as statutory audit fee stayed
flat — a fee-independence watch item, not conclusive.

Two disclosure-integrity items already surfaced by B02/B03 sit squarely
in the promoter/board's area of accountability and are restated here
because they bear directly on Section 6's scorecard: (1) Note 40
consolidated's FY26 profit-split labels were transposed (resolved with
high confidence: the Holding company, not the subsidiary, generated
~76-77% of FY26 group profit); (2) Board's Report Section 26a states the
Auditor's Report carries "no qualification, reservation, adverse remark
or disclaimer," directly contradicted by the Auditor's own CARO Annexure
1 summary table, which lists clause (ii)(b) (bank stock-statement vs
books discrepancy) against the Holding Company. Neither rises to fraud;
both are first-year-listed disclosure-discipline gaps that the promoters
and the outgoing auditor should have caught before filing.

---

## SECTION 4: REPUTATION & PUBLIC PERCEPTION

### 4A Media reputation

Coverage is limited to standard IPO-listing and review sites (Chittorgarh,
IPO Watch, ipoji, Groww, ICICI Direct, MNCL, Value Research, etc.), all
neutral-to-positive on the growth narrative (revenue Rs 24.37 Cr FY24 to
Rs 45.97 Cr FY25, PAT Rs 2.14 Cr to Rs 7.23 Cr) [MEDIA REPORTED, multiple
sources, 2026]. One review site flagged unspecified "valuation/execution
risk" generically, consistent with any small-cap IPO commentary; no site
raised a promoter-integrity concern. No adverse media (fraud allegation,
regulatory-raid report, short-seller note) found. ✅ CLEAN, but coverage
is thin and recent (3 months since listing) — a clean record with a short
runway, not a long clean track record.

### 4B Employee reputation

Glassdoor and Indeed listing pages exist for Avience Biomedicals; one
visible Glassdoor review title reads "Nice startup" (positive valence)
but full review text/rating count was not retrievable through search
snippets [💬 FORUM/SOCIAL, single data point, insufficient sample —
UNVERIFIED as a pattern either way].

### 4C Investor and analyst perception

No broker research exists (confirmed empty in B00's input_gaps). No
activist campaign, no short-seller report found. Expected for a 3-month-
old SME listing below institutional research coverage thresholds.

### 4D Political and government connections

No donation, government position, or disproportionate government-
contract/land-allotment pattern found beyond the two YEIDA/Greater Noida
industrial-land allotments already covered in B02/B03 as execution-risk
(not political-favour) items. No finding either way on dependence.

### 4E Industry peer reputation

Avience is a confirmed authorised regional channel partner for Mindray
Medical India (biochemistry analysers, haematology equipment) [MEDIA
REPORTED/corroborated by company's own materials and B00 LBF3]. No
adverse industry commentary found. Molbio Diagnostics is a named
sector peer in this run's peer set but no direct commercial relationship
between Avience and Molbio was found.

### 4F Philanthropy

CSR contribution of Rs 7.70 lakh to Manjulika Human Welfare Society
[VERIFIED, AR Annexure-3 CSR Report p.60-61, per B03]. This sits at
mandatory-CSR-spend scale for a company of this size; no evidence of
philanthropy meaningfully beyond the statutory minimum, so this is a
neutral rather than a positive signal.

## SECTION 5: CXO & BOARD QUALITY

### 5A CXO turnover (5 years)

The company has no five-year listed history to test turnover against.
Both current CFO (Saurabh Verma, appointed 21-Aug-2024) and Company
Secretary/Compliance Officer (Manoj Kumar, appointed 24-Sep-2024) were
hired in the run-up to public-company conversion (03-Sep-2024) — a
one-time governance build-out for listing readiness, not evidence of
churn, since there is no prior CFO/CS this run's corpus shows them
replacing [VERIFIED, RHP pdf p.266].

### 5B Board quality

Six-member board: two executive-family directors (Dharam Deo Choudhary
CMD, Ram Nagina Choudhary WTD), two additional family directors (Janardan
Pal non-executive, Deepa Choudhary executive), and two independent
directors (Om Prakash Pal, Pankaj Sharma) who together chair/sit on the
Audit, Nomination & Remuneration, and Stakeholders Relationship
Committees [VERIFIED, AR p.5-6]. Family occupies 4 of 6 seats and both
executive leadership roles — a concentrated-control structure typical of,
but not unique to, promoter-led SME listings. Independent directors'
domain expertise and prior board experience could not be verified from
this corpus or from web search (their DINs — 10841456 and 07077130 —
were not cross-checked against other board memberships beyond Avience in
this pass; a gap, named rather than assumed clean).

### 5C Key-person risk and succession

Dharam Deo Choudhary (58, CMD) is the named strategic and financial
architect of the group (30 years' experience, the only promoter with a
professional finance credential). Ram Nagina Choudhary (WTD) runs
operations/supply chain. No named professional non-family successor or
succession plan is disclosed anywhere in the corpus. Key-person
concentration on one individual (the CMD) is a real, if common,
structural risk for a company of this size.

## SECTION 6: PROMOTER QUALITY VERDICT

### 6A Scorecard (10 dimensions, strictly from Sections 1-5)

| # | Dimension | Rating | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Family/group structure & non-compete coverage | ⚠️ | Group Company (Imperative Healthcare) excluded from non-compete despite competing business objects (Sec 1B) |
| 2 | Education/background verifiability | ✅ | All four promoters' credentials named, dated, plausible (Sec 1C) |
| 3 | SEBI/securities-law record | ✅ | No action found, RHP self-declaration + web corroboration (Sec 2A) |
| 4 | Criminal/economic-offence record | ✅ | Only routine NI Act cheque litigation both directions; no fraud/economic offence (Sec 2B) |
| 5 | Tax/regulatory record | ✅ | One small resolved matter (CMD); NIL elsewhere (Sec 2C-2D) |
| 6 | RPT discipline | ⚠️ | Growing subsidiary guarantee (15.9-18.4% of net worth), advance more than doubled, related-party receivables disclosed only at consolidated level (Sec 3A) |
| 7 | Capital allocation/share dealing | ✅ | No promoter selling, zero pledge, 100% locked-in, arm's-length-priced pre-IPO round (Sec 3B-3C) |
| 8 | Auditor/disclosure governance | ⚠️ | Clean term-end auditor rotation, but non-audit fee exceeds audit fee, plus Note 40 transposition and Board's Report/CARO contradiction (Sec 3E) |
| 9 | Reputation/media/employee | ✅ | Clean but thin (3 months listed) (Sec 4) |
| 10 | Board independence/key-person risk | ⚠️ | Family holds 4 of 6 board seats plus both executive roles; unverified independent-director track record; single key-person concentration (Sec 5) |

Scorecard: clean 6, caution 4, red 0.

### 6B Classification

**CAUTION.** No red flags anywhere in the five sections; the four caution
items (non-compete gap, RPT/guarantee growth, disclosure-integrity gaps
in the first listed-year AR, and family-concentrated board) are real but
are the kind of gaps typical of a company three months into its first
year as a listed entity, not evidence of promoter bad faith. Zero pledge,
no promoter selling, no SEBI/criminal/tax adverse finding, and an
arm's-length-priced pre-IPO round keep this above CONCERN.

### 6C Deal-breaker checks (recorded, not enforced)

| Deal-breaker | Triggered? | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| SEBI market ban | No | Sec 2A |
| Economic-offence conviction | No | Sec 2B |
| Live SFIO | No | Sec 2C |
| PMLA with attached assets | No | Sec 2B-2C |
| Auditor resignation within 3 years | **No** | Term-end rotation after a full 5-year appointment, filed as "Appointment" not resignation (Sec 3E) |
| Pledge > 40% | No | 0% pledge (Sec 3C) |
| Multiple mid-term independent-director exits within 3 years | No | No exit found; insufficient history (3 months listed) to test |
| Restatement cutting past profits > 10% | No | The 25-Aug-2026 revision was a balance-sheet elimination correction with explicit NO profit/equity impact (B00 LBF2) |

None triggered.

### 6D Transition evidence scan

- New professional CFO and Company Secretary from outside the family
  (Saurabh Verma, Manoj Kumar), both hired ahead of the public-company
  conversion in 2024 [VERIFIED, RHP pdf p.266].
- Institutional/AIF entry: Alternative Investment Funds hold 395,000
  shares (7.2% of post-issue capital) as on the 24-Jun-2026 shareholding
  pattern [VERIFIED, SHP XBRL, `AlternativeInvestmentFunds_ContextI`
  fields]; individual fund names not resolved from this filing's flat
  dump — a gap, not a negative.
- Governance build-out coincident with listing: two independent directors
  appointed, plus a new secretarial auditor (GR Gupta & Associates) and a
  new internal auditor (V C A N & CO LLP), both first-time appointments
  disclosed in this AR [VERIFIED, AR pdf p.7-8, 50; B03
  ar_new_downstream_entities].
- Statutory auditor transition from a recognised mid-tier firm
  (Haribhakti & Co. LLP) to an unverified smaller firm (M.A.M and
  Associates) is **not** an upgrade signal; recorded as a neutral-to-
  negative item under this scan, not a positive.
- No pledge-reduction trend (never had a pledge), no exit of a
  problematic family member (none identified as problematic), no stake
  sale to a credible strategic investor.

Read as a whole: this is a company building IPO-readiness governance
infrastructure for the first time, not a legacy-CONCERN name proving a
turnaround from a documented troubled past. The distinction matters for
how the operator should read "CAUTION with transition evidence" here —
it is closer to "young company assembling its first governance layer"
than "problem promoter cleaning up its act."

### 6E Final verdict card

```
PROMOTER: Dharam Deo Choudhary, Ram Nagina Choudhary, Janardan Pal,
          Deepa Choudhary (Avience Biomedicals Ltd)
VERDICT: CAUTION
SCORECARD: 6 clean / 4 caution / 0 red
DEAL-BREAKERS TRIGGERED: none
PLEDGE: 0% (stable; single shareholding pattern on record)
TRANSITION EVIDENCE: present (IPO-readiness governance build-out; see 6D)
DECISIVE BASIS: Clean SEBI/criminal/tax record, zero pledge, no promoter
  selling, and an arm's-length-priced pre-IPO round set a solid floor;
  a non-compete gap on the one true Group Company, a growing intra-group
  guarantee/advance, and this AR's own first-year disclosure-integrity
  gaps (Note 40 transposition; Board's Report vs CARO contradiction) are
  real but are consistent with governance infrastructure still catching
  up to a new listing, not with promoter bad faith.
```

## analyst_note

The dominant frame here is "young company, first listed year," not
"legacy problem promoter." Every caution item traces to governance
infrastructure that is either brand-new (independent directors, CFO, CS,
secretarial/internal auditors all appointed 2024-2026) or a first-year
filing slip (Note 40, Board's Report vs CARO) rather than a pattern
repeated over time. The one item worth the operator's own direct check
beyond this run is M.A.M and Associates' actual track record and client
base — web search returned nothing on this incoming auditor, which is
itself a data point (a small, low-visibility firm taking over statutory
audit duties for a newly listed, cash-conversion-stressed SME) worth
weighing alongside the fact that the outgoing auditor's own CARO Annexure
already carries an unquantified qualification the Board's Report denies
exists.
