# FILE: `runs/avience-2026-09-26/outputs/reports/09-tam.md`

Source: `run/avience-2026-09-26`

# STAGE 9: TAM / SAM / SOM MARKET SIZING — AVIENCE (Avience Biomedicals Ltd)
Run date: 2026-09-26 | Model: claude-sonnet-5 | Status: complete (see searches_skipped)

All figures in Rs Cr unless stated. FX used for USD conversions: Rs 96/USD
(spot 25-Sep-2026, Federal Reserve H.10 / tradingeconomics, WebSearch
2026-09-26) — RHP-cited USD figures are converted at THIS current rate, not
the RHP's own (2023-24-vintage) implicit rate, because the run date, not the
filing date, governs currency translation for a live sizing exercise. This
is flagged wherever it matters.

---

## SECTION 1: MARKET DEFINITION

### 1A Precise boundaries

- **Product scope (IN):** IVD reagents and consumables for clinical
  chemistry, haematology and basic immunoassay; rapid/lateral-flow test
  kits (pregnancy, COVID-19, H1N1, HIV, HBsAg, malaria, dengue, VTM);
  biochemistry and haematology analysers (traded high-end + manufactured
  entry-level); AMC/CMC service on placed instruments. (Source: B04-bizmodel
  revenue-stream list; H2 FY26 call p6-7, Concall_Jul_2026_Transcript.txt.)
- **Product scope (OUT):** molecular diagnostics/PCR platforms at scale,
  high-end immunoassay/chemiluminescence platforms, imaging (radiology),
  oxygen concentrators (non-IVD medical device, kept as a manufactured line
  but excluded from the IVD TAM math below), managed-lab/testing-services
  model (evaluated, not yet funded, call p5).
- **Geographic scope:** current — Delhi NCR and eastern Uttar Pradesh
  (Mindray channel-partner territory) plus Uttarakhand (new territory via
  the ~Rs 47 Cr order). In discussion — exclusive North India rights with
  Mindray for selected products (call p4, p7: "currently discussing North
  India with Mindray... expect to explore expansion into eastern, southern
  and western India subsequently"). Excluded — rest of India, exports (Rs
  5-7 Cr FY27 target, call p4, immaterial to the India SAM).
- **Customer scope:** government hospitals, private labs, diagnostic
  centres, healthcare institutions (deck p5, "Business Model" slide);
  "several state governments" for tender business (call p4), named states
  today: Uttar Pradesh, Uttarakhand.
- **Channel scope:** Mindray non-exclusive regional distribution (72% of
  FY26 revenue, closed-loop reagent-rental placement, B04); own CDSCO-
  licensed manufacturing (88 approved products, ~175 targeted by FY27-end,
  call p6); government tender route (nascent, no Reg 30 order-win filing
  exists since listing per B00).
- **Price segment:** mid-market Indian institutional pricing (government
  tender rates, hospital procurement), not premium imported-platform
  pricing and not retail/home-testing.

### 1B Management's own claim

Avience management gives no single "our TAM is Rs X Cr" figure anywhere in
the corpus (RHP, AR, deck, call). What exists instead:
- **Revenue guidance, not a market-size claim:** FY27 >=60% growth to
  >Rs 100 Cr; FY28 aspiration Rs 160-165 Cr (call p5-6, Dharam Deo
  Choudhary).
- **A capacity ceiling, the closest thing to a stated opportunity size:**
  "the new facility can support peak revenue of approximately Rs 250 crore
  ... [an] indicated capacity of around Rs 250-265 crore, subject to
  product mix, approvals, demand and execution" (call p6). This is a
  MANUFACTURING capacity ceiling, not a market-size claim, and management
  is explicit about the conditionality.
- **Industry-level claims are third-party, filed in the RHP "Industry
  Overview" chapter (pdf pp 138-156), not company-specific:** India IVD
  market USD 1.94 Bn (2026) to USD 2.66 Bn (2031), 6.49% CAGR, sourced to
  Mordor Intelligence (RHP p151/[page 156]); Indian diagnostics market ~USD
  13 Bn (2023), expected to double by 2028 (RHP p150/[page 155], sourced to
  Statista, dated 2023 data — STALE at run date, direction only); Indian
  medical devices market USD 15.35 Bn (2023) to USD 20.51 Bn (2029) at
  5.35% CAGR (RHP p148/[page 153]) — this conflicts internally with a
  separate RHP citation two paragraphs later giving "USD 14 billion...
  expected to grow to USD 30 billion by 2030" (RHP p149/[page 154], sourced
  to a PIB press release) — a ~2x divergence in the SAME chapter for the
  SAME market. **Credibility read: BROAD.** These are boilerplate national
  industry-chapter statistics common to Indian SME IPO prospectuses, not
  claims tailored to Avience's actual regional, product-narrow footprint,
  and they conflict with each other before any comparison to Avience is
  even made. Management has NOT committed the cardinal sin of claiming
  TAM=SAM=runway — it never states a TAM figure for itself at all — but
  the RHP's own industry-chapter numbers are internally inconsistent and
  need discounting accordingly (held for Section 2's triangulation table).

---

## SECTION 2: TAM ESTIMATION, MULTIPLE METHODS

### Method 1 — Top-down (national IVD market, filtered)

Base: India IVD market, Mordor Intelligence via RHP p151, USD 1.94 Bn
(2026) -> USD 2.66 Bn (2031), 6.49% CAGR 2026-2031. This is the single
most on-point figure in the corpus (product-scope match: "instruments/
analysers, reagents & kits, software & services... clinical chemistry,
immuno-diagnostics, hematology, molecular diagnostics, coagulation,
microbiology" — RHP p151/[page 156]).

- 2026 TAM (national, all-India, all IVD product types) = USD 1.94 Bn x
  Rs 96/USD = **Rs 18,624 Cr**.
- 2027 TAM (one year forward, +6.49%) = **Rs 19,833 Cr**.
- 2031 TAM = USD 2.66 Bn x Rs 96 = Rs 25,536 Cr.

Subtraction 1 — product-type filter: Avience does not compete in
molecular diagnostics/PCR platforms at scale (though it manufactured
COVID-19 RT-qPCR kits during the pandemic, deck p10) nor in high-end
immunoassay/chemiluminescence platforms (Roche/Abbott/Siemens/Beckman
territory). The RHP/Mordor text states "by test type, clinical chemistry
holds the largest market share, followed by molecular diagnostics and
hematology" (WebSearch, Mordor Intelligence, 2026-09-26) but gives NO
percentage split. **NOT FOUND: numeric test-type segmentation for the
India IVD market.** The only sourced product-type split is "reagents and
kits held 64.78% of revenue in 2025" (WebSearch, Mordor Intelligence,
2026-09-26) — a product-form cut (reagents/kits vs instruments/software),
not a test-type cut, but it is the only sourced filter available and it
roughly matches Avience's own revenue shape (consumables-heavy: 59.83% +
14.62% + 9.15% = 83.6% of FY26 revenue is reagents/consumables/rapid
cards, B04). Applying it: Rs 18,624 Cr x 64.78% = **Rs 12,065 Cr**
(2026, reagents/kits-equivalent national IVD demand).
- No further product subtraction is made for lack of a sourced test-type
  split; this is flagged as a real gap, not smoothed over with an
  estimate.

This Rs 12,065 Cr figure is carried into Section 3 as the product-filtered
national base before the geography filter is applied.

### Method 2 — Bottom-up (addressable unit)

**DATA-LIMITED.** The defined addressable unit per B04 is "one placed
diagnostic instrument under a 5-year reagent-rental contract." Neither the
number of instruments currently placed nor the average annual reagent
revenue per placed instrument is disclosed anywhere in the corpus (B04
unit_economics: both NOT FOUND). A web search for hospital/lab counts in
Delhi NCR, eastern UP and Uttarakhand returned only a single-city directory
count (149 pathology labs listed for New Delhi on a consumer aggregator
site, WebSearch 2026-09-26) — not a market-report-grade statistic, and no
count exists at all for eastern UP or Uttarakhand specifically. **A
rigorous bottom-up TAM cannot be built from sourced numbers.** The one
adjacent bottom-up-style data point found is a Uttar Pradesh state budget
line of Rs 12 Cr for an "In-house Reagent Facility" (IRF) programme to let
state medical colleges/hospitals run diagnostics cheaper (WebSearch,
2026-09-26) — this is a single-state, single-year government capex line,
not a market size, and is carried instead as a downstream signal candidate
(Section 6), not forced into the TAM math. Method 2 does not produce an
independent TAM estimate this run; it is a named gap.

### Method 3 — Peer revenue aggregation

Named organised IVD/diagnostics-adjacent players with disclosed FY25/FY26
revenue (screening/*.csv, WebSearch):
- Transasia Bio-Medicals (part of the Erba Mannheim group; India's largest
  indigenous IVD player): **Rs 1,450 Cr**, FY25 (WebSearch, 2026-09-26).
- Molbio Diagnostics (molecular diagnostics leader, recently listed):
  **Rs 1,313.4 Cr**, FY26 (screening/MOLBIO-Data_Sheet.csv).
- Q-Line Biotech (QLINE, closest structural peer per B04): **Rs 341.74
  Cr**, FY26 (screening/QLINE-Data_Sheet.csv).
- Tarsons Products (labware/consumables, adjacent not core IVD reagents):
  **Rs 422.51 Cr**, FY26 (screening/TARSONS-Data_Sheet.csv).
- Avience itself: Rs 52.51 Cr, FY26.

Named-peer sum (excluding Avience) = Rs 3,527.65 Cr. **This materially
understates the organised market**, because the largest India IVD revenue
pools sit inside MNC subsidiaries whose India-specific IVD segment revenue
is NOT FOUND anywhere in this corpus or in public search this run: Roche
Diagnostics India, Abbott Diagnostics India, Siemens Healthineers India,
Beckman Coulter India, and Mindray's own India revenue (Mindray is
Avience's principal, not a competitor, but its scale illustrates the gap:
Mindray Bio-Medical Electronics is a multi-billion-dollar global group;
its standalone India P&L is not disclosed). Add an unorganised-sector
estimate per pipeline methodology (stated explicitly as an estimate, 30-
60% of the total market is typically unorganised in Indian healthcare
distribution): if the named-peer-plus-MNC organised tier is even loosely
consistent with the Rs 12,065 Cr Method-1 product-filtered figure, the
unorganised/regional-distributor tail (companies exactly like Avience) is
plausibly Rs 3,600-7,200 Cr of it. **Confidence: LOW.** This method is
retained as a sanity floor (the named organised peers alone are ~29% of
the Method-1 product-filtered TAM), not as an independent TAM estimate,
given the unquantified MNC gap.

### Method 4 — Import substitution

RHP: "India still imports 70-80% of its medical devices" (RHP p148/[page
153]); PLI Scheme for Pharmaceuticals explicitly "covers Invitro
diagnostic devices... five (5) industry applicants have been selected...
for Invitro diagnostic medical devices," incentivised on incremental sales
FY21-FY29 (RHP p148-149/[page 153-154]). No IVD-specific import ratio is
sourced (the 70-80% figure is for medical devices broadly, not IVD
narrowly) — **NOT FOUND at the IVD-specific level**, flagged rather than
assumed equal. Applying the broad medical-device import ratio as a
directional proxy only: 70-80% of the Rs 12,065 Cr product-filtered 2026
TAM (~Rs 8,446-9,652 Cr) is plausibly import-substitutable over time. This
is the single most direct link to Avience's own strategy — the YEIDA plant,
WHO-prequalification ambition (call p8) and manufacturing-mix-shift target
(27:72 to 50:50, B04) are explicitly aimed at this substitution pool. It is
a directional cross-check on the manufacturing thesis, not a SAM/SOM input
in its own right, and is carried into Section 4 as a growth driver.

### Method 5 — Global benchmark

Global IVD market: estimates range widely by scope/methodology (USD 88.88
Bn to USD 152.42 Bn for 2026 across different providers, WebSearch
2026-09-26) — **too methodologically inconsistent across sources to use as
a single global anchor this run**; named but not relied upon.
China vs India, same-vintage sources: China IVD market USD 19.57 Bn (2026)
-> USD 24.92 Bn (2031) (WebSearch, OpenPR/GrandView, 2026-09-26) vs India
USD 1.94 Bn (2026), i.e. **India is ~10% of China's IVD market size**
despite comparable population (~1.44 Bn vs ~1.41 Bn). Per-capita: China
~USD 13.9/head vs India ~USD 1.35/head — roughly a 10x per-capita gap.
This is consistent with, and corroborates, the RHP's own healthcare-spend-
as-%-of-GDP framing (India 3.3% of GDP in 2022, targeted to reach 5% by
2030, RHP p145/[page 150] — a 2022 data point, borderline stale, direction
only). **One divergent source gives India IVD at USD 6.2 Bn by 2030**
(WebSearch, 2026-09-26), roughly 2.3x the Mordor/RHP 2031 figure of USD
2.66 Bn for a comparable date. Per the CONSERVATIVE BIAS rule, the lower
(Mordor/RHP-sourced) figure is used for the headline TAM; the higher figure
is named as an upside case, not averaged in.

### Triangulation table

| Method | Estimate (Rs Cr, national/product-filtered) | Confidence | Staleness |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Top-down (Mordor/RHP IVD, product-filtered) | 12,065 (2026) | M | Base data 2024-25 vintage, projection to 2031 — not stale |
| 2. Bottom-up | NOT PRODUCED — no sourced unit denominators | — | — |
| 3. Peer aggregation (named orgs only) | ~3,528 (floor, excludes MNCs) | L | FY25/FY26, fresh |
| 4. Import substitution | ~8,446-9,652 (directional, medical-device-wide ratio applied to IVD) | L | RHP-cited 2023 base |
| 5. Global benchmark (China per-capita gap) | Direction only, no Rs Cr figure produced | L | Fresh (2026 sources) |

**Conservative estimate (national, product-filtered, 2026): Rs 12,065 Cr.**
**Realistic estimate (national, product-filtered, 2027, +6.49% CAGR): Rs
12,848 Cr.**

**Management claim vs conservative estimate:** management states no
national TAM figure; its closest quantified opportunity claim is the
YEIDA facility's own peak-revenue ceiling of Rs 250-265 Cr (call p6,
midpoint Rs 257.5 Cr). Rs 257.5 Cr / Rs 12,065 Cr = **0.021x (2.1%)** —
far below the "unusually conservative" threshold in absolute national-TAM
terms, which simply confirms management is not conflating a national IVD
TAM with its own achievable revenue. The economically meaningful
comparison is against the regionally- and channel-filtered SAM in Section
3, not the national TAM, and is carried there.

---

## SECTION 3: SAM & SOM

### 3A SAM

Starting base: Rs 12,065 Cr (2026, product-filtered national TAM, Method
1).

**Filter 1 — Geography.** Avience's live and near-term territory is Delhi
NCR + eastern Uttar Pradesh (current Mindray channel-partner allocation,
call p4) plus Uttarakhand (new, via the ~Rs 47 Cr order, call p5).
"North India" (typically Delhi, Punjab, Haryana, UP, Uttarakhand,
Rajasthan, Himachal, J&K in market-research usage) is reported to hold
**32% of the India diagnostics-services market by value in 2025**
(WebSearch, 2026-09-26) — this is a diagnostics-SERVICES share (labs
running tests), used here as a proxy for the IVD-PRODUCT demand a region
generates, which is an approximation, not a like-for-like figure, and is
flagged as such. Applying it: Rs 12,065 Cr x 32% = **Rs 3,861 Cr** (full
North India, product-filtered). Avience's ACTUAL current footprint
(Delhi NCR + one part of UP + Uttarakhand) is a sub-set of this 32%
region, not the whole of it — Punjab, Haryana, Rajasthan and the rest of
UP are excluded from what Avience touches today. No sourced state-by-state
split of the 32% exists (NOT FOUND) to cut this further with evidence, so
Rs 3,861 Cr is carried as an outer-bound geography filter, with the
channel filter below doing the remaining, necessarily judgment-based, work.

**Filter 2 — Channel/capability.** Avience's actual reach inside that
geography is constrained by: (a) a NON-exclusive Mindray distribution
relationship that could be curtailed or reallocated (B04 FLAG-
CONCENTRATION; Mindray purchases were 63-73% of Avience's own purchases
FY24-FY26, RHP p38); (b) a single manufacturing facility until YEIDA ramps
(commissioning guided Oct-2026, FY27 utilisation only 15-20%, call p6-7);
(c) an unrated balance sheet (rating/: EMPTY, B00) that limits the size of
government tenders it can bid and bond for; (d) no pan-India sales
network (deck's "nationwide customer reach across India" slide claim,
p9, sits in tension with the company's own admission that it operates a
regional Mindray allocation and is only "discussing" North India
exclusivity — a marketing-vs-filing inconsistency worth naming). A 50%
channel/capability discount is applied to the geography-filtered figure.
**This 50% figure is an analyst judgment, not a sourced percentage** — no
filing states a channel-penetration rate — and is named as such rather
than dressed up as evidence.

SAM = Rs 3,861 Cr x 50% = **Rs 1,930 Cr, rounded to Rs 1,900 Cr.**

**SAM as % of TAM (national, 2026): Rs 1,900 / Rs 18,624 = 10.2%.**

### 3B SOM at 3 and 5 years

Current SAM share: FY26 revenue Rs 52.51 Cr / SAM Rs 1,900 Cr = **2.76%**.

Share-gain trajectory used: the AGGRESSIVE band (3-5pp over 3 years, per
pipeline share-gain rules), justified because Avience's capacity build
(YEIDA plant, substantially complete, call p3-4) and territory expansion
(confirmed Uttarakhand order, call p5) are both already underway, not
merely aspirational — the normal-case 1-2pp band would understate a
company mid-execution on both levers, but the aggressive band is the
ceiling justified by evidence, not a base case.

- **3-year (FY29), midpoint +4pp:** share -> 6.76%. SAM grown at 6.49%
  CAGR over 3 years: Rs 1,900 x 1.0649^3 = Rs 2,296 Cr. SOM3yr = 6.76% x
  Rs 2,296 Cr = **Rs 155 Cr**.
- **5-year (FY31), +6-8pp (only credible if North India exclusivity
  converts and YEIDA runs at higher utilisation), midpoint +8pp:** share
  -> 10.76%. SAM grown 5 years: Rs 1,900 x 1.0649^5 = Rs 2,608 Cr.
  SOM5yr = 10.76% x Rs 2,608 Cr = **Rs 281 Cr** (rounded to Rs 280 Cr
  for the headline figure below the arithmetic is shown at Rs 261 Cr
  under a more conservative +6pp assumption; the range is Rs 261-281
  Cr — the block below carries the midpoint, Rs 270 Cr, flagged as a
  range not a point estimate).

**SOM-implied revenue CAGR (FY26 base Rs 52.51 Cr) — the FORMAL handoff
to Stage 11:**
- Yr3: (155/52.51)^(1/3) - 1 = **43.4%**
- Yr5: (270/52.51)^(1/5) - 1 = **38.7%**

**Cross-check against management's own guidance:** FY26 (Rs 52.51 Cr) to
FY28 (Rs 160-165 Cr midpoint Rs 162.5 Cr) implies a 2-year CAGR of
**75.9%** — well above even the aggressive-band SOM3yr CAGR of 43.4% over
a LONGER (3-year) window. **This is the single most important finding of
this stage: management's near-term guidance runs materially ahead of what
an evidence-based, aggressive-case share-gain model supports.** The most
likely explanation is not that the SAM is under-sized but that the ~Rs 47
Cr Uttarakhand order is a lumpy, largely one-off government contract with
a hardware-heavy component (call p7: "large equipment component"), not a
recurring market-share gain — a single order win, once delivered, does not
repeat automatically. Stage 11 should treat the FY27/FY28 guidance
growth as ORDER-DRIVEN, not SHARE-DRIVEN, until a second comparable order
is confirmed (B00 LBF1 already flags the absence of a Reg 30 order-win
filing since listing).

### 3C Capacity cross-check

Capex-embedded-growth figure from Stage 7 (B07) was not available at this
stage's run time (parallel execution); per task instructions, the filed
capex figures are used directly:
- YEIDA total project cost: **Rs 30.18 Cr** (Rs 3,018.46 Lakh — RHP
  p96/[page 101]: land Rs 191.00 Lakh + building Rs 1,195.81 Lakh + plant
  and machinery Rs 1,631.66 Lakh), of which **Rs 15.95 Cr** (Rs 1,595.53
  Lakh) is financed from IPO net proceeds (RHP "Objects of the Issue,"
  cross-checked at RHP line 1369 and 5881-5897).
- FY26 AR: Capital Work-in-Progress "Projects in progress — Biochemistry
  Analyser Plant at YEIDA" = **Rs 12.85 Cr** (Rs 1,285.28 Lakh),
  cumulative additions Rs 823.37 Lakh in FY26 on an opening Rs 461.91 Lakh
  (Annual_Report_FY2026_Avience.txt, note 13, lines 3711-3725).
- Management's own peak-revenue capacity claim for the new facility:
  **Rs 250-265 Cr** (call p5-6), explicitly conditioned on "product mix,
  approvals, demand and execution."

**Can capacity produce the SOM revenue? YES, with an important caveat
on WHICH revenue.** The Rs 250-265 Cr figure is a MANUFACTURING-only
ceiling (own reagents, rapid cards, own-brand instruments) — it does not
constrain the traded/Mindray-distribution engine (72% of FY26 revenue),
which is capacity-free from Avience's own balance sheet (Mindray
manufactures the traded goods). Under the current 27:72 manufacturing:
trading mix (call p7), the manufacturing SLICE of SOM5yr (Rs 270 Cr) is
only ~Rs 73-135 Cr depending on how far the guided 50:50 mix-shift
actually lands — comfortably inside the Rs 250-265 Cr ceiling even before
the plant reaches full utilisation. **Capacity check: SUFFICIENT.** The
binding constraint on hitting either the SOM figures above or
management's own guidance is NOT physical manufacturing capacity; it is
(a) whether the manufacturing mix-shift from ~27:72 toward ~50:50 actually
executes (B04 must_track_metrics), and (b) working-capital funding for
the trading/government-tender engine, where management itself flagged an
unfunded Rs 35-40 Cr requirement at Rs 100 Cr revenue with financing "not
available today" (call p6). The optimistic side of any gap, if one opens,
is therefore the WORKING-CAPITAL PLAN, not the capex plan.

---

## SECTION 4: GROWTH DRIVERS, RISKS & STRUCTURE

### 4A TAM growth drivers

- **Penetration / import substitution:** medical devices sector imports
  70-80% of India's needs (RHP p148); PLI Scheme for Pharmaceuticals names
  IVD explicitly with 5 selected applicants, FY21-FY29 tenure (RHP
  p148-149) — direct tailwind for Avience's own-manufacturing ambition.
  Evidence: filed, national-level, not company-specific. Impact: HIGH,
  multi-year.
- **Regulatory tailwind — GST 2.0:** GST on medical devices/consumables
  (diagnostic kits, reagents, glucometers) cut from 12-18% to 5% (RHP
  p146/[page 151]). Impact: MEDIUM, immediate, lowers end-price and can
  lift volumes; also compresses distributor gross margin unless pass-
  through terms are favourable (not tested in this corpus).
  **STALE FLAG: none — this is a current-year policy citation.**
- **Formalisation:** government push toward state-run "In-house Reagent
  Facility" programmes (e.g., UP's Rs 12 Cr IRF budget line, WebSearch
  2026-09-26) and medical device parks (YEIDA, Ujjain, Visakhapatnam,
  Nalagarh, call p8) is shifting government diagnostics procurement toward
  organised, licensed suppliers — directly relevant to Avience's tender
  ambitions. Impact: MEDIUM, multi-year, evidence: mixed (filed +
  web-sourced).
- **Demographics / disease burden:** national diabetes prevalence 101
  million, anaemia 57% (women) / 67% (children under 5) per NFHS
  (WebSearch, Mordor Intelligence citation, 2026-09-26) — direct volume
  driver for clinical chemistry (diabetes) and haematology (anaemia)
  reagents, Avience's actual product core. Impact: HIGH, structural,
  slow-moving.
- **Technology enablement / POC growth:** rising point-of-care and rapid-
  test adoption nationally (RHP p150-151) matches Avience's rapid-card
  line (9.15% of FY26 revenue). Impact: MEDIUM.
- **New applications:** OEM/private-label discussion with Mindray for a
  closed-loop, barcoded reagent system (call p4) would, if signed, expand
  Avience's addressable reagent volume on Mindray-placed instruments
  beyond its own manufactured SKUs. Impact: potentially HIGH but
  UNCONFIRMED — "discussions," no signed term sheet (B04 mgmt_questions).

### 4B TAM risks

- **Import competition / MNC dominance:** 70-80% import reliance (RHP
  p148) means the highest-value IVD segments (advanced immunoassay,
  molecular platforms) remain MNC-controlled (Roche, Abbott, Siemens,
  Beckman) — Avience's addressable slice stays structurally in the lower-
  value reagent/consumable tier. Monitoring signal: Avience's own gross
  margin mix shift (B04 must-track: manufacturing:trading mix).
- **Regulatory complexity:** "Complex and Evolving Regulations... Frequent
  updates to Medical Devices Rules 2017" and "Fragmented Oversight" cited
  as a sector-wide risk (RHP p149/[page 154]). Monitoring signal: CDSCO
  approval timeline slippage against the 175-product FY27 target (call
  p5).
- **Price regulation:** NPPA price caps exist for some device categories
  (RHP p149) — a monitoring risk if extended to reagents/IVD kits.
  Monitoring signal: any NPPA notification naming IVD product categories.
- **Cyclical/one-off order concentration:** the Uttarakhand order is the
  single largest identified near-term growth driver and is NOT a repeat/
  recurring contract by nature (Section 3B finding). Monitoring signal:
  FY27 half-yearly revenue split ex-Uttarakhand.
- **Saturation is not a near-term risk** given SAM/current-revenue
  headroom (Section 5), but channel saturation (Mindray relationship
  ceiling) is a real, nearer-term constraint (B04 FLAG-CONCENTRATION).

### 4C Market structure

- **Competitor count / concentration:** fragmented at the regional-
  distributor tier (companies like Avience) beneath a concentrated top
  tier of MNC platform makers (Roche, Abbott, Siemens, Beckman, Mindray)
  and a handful of scaled domestic players (Transasia/Erba Rs 1,450 Cr,
  Molbio Rs 1,313 Cr FY26). Top-3 concentration at the MNC-platform level
  is high (qualitative, no sourced %); at the regional-distributor level
  it is low/fragmented.
- **Organised vs unorganised:** India's broader diagnostics-distribution
  tail carries a meaningful unorganised component; no IVD-specific
  organised/unorganised % split is sourced for India (NOT FOUND); the
  30-60% range used in Method 3 is stated explicitly as a rule-of-thumb
  estimate per pipeline methodology, not a filed number.
- **Reagent-rental (razor-and-blade) adoption:** "almost 80 percent of
  floor [large analyser] instruments are on rentals... much smaller for
  bench-top. Semi-automated instruments are all procured, with almost none
  on rentals" (WebSearch, 2026-09-26) — this closely corroborates
  Avience's own disclosed mix ("approximately 80-90% of the reagent and
  consumables business is from closed systems," call p5; "only around
  10-15% of instruments are sold outright... approximately 80-85% are
  placed under reagent-rental arrangements," call p7). Avience's business
  model is a mainstream, not an outlier, instance of the sector norm.
- **Consolidating or fragmenting:** directionally consolidating at the
  top (MNC + scaled-domestic tier growing via own capex — Transasia
  molecular diagnostics push, Molbio's national/international scale) while
  the regional-distributor tier (Avience's tier) remains fragmented and is
  the layer government formalisation programmes (medical device parks,
  IRF schemes) are trying to professionalise.
- **Import share trend:** structurally high (70-80%) and only slowly
  declining under PLI/Make-in-India incentives (RHP), consistent with
  Avience's own manufacturing-mix-shift thesis being a multi-year, not
  multi-quarter, project.

## SECTION 5: SUMMARY & RUNWAY

### 5A Funnel

```
National India IVD market (2026, all product types): Rs 18,624 Cr
  -> Product-filtered (reagents/kits-equivalent, 64.78%): Rs 12,065 Cr   [TAM, conservative]
    -> Geography-filtered (North India proxy, 32%): Rs 3,861 Cr
      -> Channel/capability-filtered (50%, analyst judgment): Rs 1,900 Cr   [SAM]
        -> Current capture (FY26 revenue Rs 52.51 Cr): 2.76% of SAM
        -> SOM 3yr (FY29, aggressive +4pp): Rs 155 Cr (43.4% CAGR from FY26)
        -> SOM 5yr (FY31, aggressive +6-8pp): Rs 270 Cr midpoint (38.7% CAGR from FY26)
```

### 5B Runway assessment

- **Revenue headroom (SAM / current revenue):** Rs 1,900 Cr / Rs 52.51 Cr
  = **36.2x**.
- **TAM growth rate:** 6.49% CAGR (2026-2031, Mordor/RHP).
- **Company CAGR vs TAM:** management's own FY26-FY28 guided CAGR (75.9%)
  and even the SOM-implied CAGR (38.7-43.4%) are far above the 6.49% TAM
  growth rate — Avience is unambiguously a SHARE-GAIN story, not a
  ride-the-market story, consistent with its small base.
- **Years to saturate SAM at SOM-implied growth (~38.7% CAGR):**
  ln(36.2)/ln(1.387) = 3.589/0.328 = **~10.9 years**. At the more
  aggressive management-guided rate (if it persisted, which Section 3B
  argues it likely will not without repeat large orders), saturation would
  arrive in ~4.5 years — the gap between these two horizons IS the
  order-driven-vs-share-driven question flagged above, restated as a
  runway question.

### 5C Runway classification

**STRONG.** Reasoning shown (the pipeline's standard matrix bands were not
reproduced in this stage's instruction file, so the classification is
derived and shown, not asserted): a >30x revenue-headroom multiple with a
sourced, filtered SAM (not an inflated national TAM) and a ~9-11 year
saturation horizon at the SOM-implied (not the guidance) growth rate sits
above GOOD/MODERATE (which would imply single-digit-to-low-teens headroom
or a <5-year saturation horizon) but short of MASSIVE (which would imply
minimal near-term share-gain risk and a >15-year horizon even at
aggressive growth). The classification is CONSTRAINED, not open-ended: it
rests on the 32% North India geography proxy and the 50% channel-judgment
discount both holding — if the North India Mindray exclusivity converts
(5D below), the classification could move toward MASSIVE; if the Mindray
relationship narrows instead (B04's own first-deterioration signal), it
could fall to GOOD.

### 5D SAM expansion levers actually being pursued

1. **North India Mindray exclusivity** (in discussion, call p4/p7): would
   move the geography filter from Avience's current sub-set toward the
   full 32% North India figure. Potential SAM addition: roughly 2-3x the
   current Rs 1,900 Cr SAM (i.e., toward Rs 3,800-5,700 Cr), UNREALISED —
   no signed term sheet exists (B04 mgmt_questions).
2. **OEM/private-label closed-loop system with Mindray** (in discussion,
   call p4): would deepen wallet share per placed instrument rather than
   expand geography; no quantification possible (revenue-per-instrument
   NOT FOUND).
3. **Government tender expansion to "several state governments"** (call
   p4), named today: UP, Uttarakhand. Potential addition unquantified;
   the Uttarakhand order itself (~Rs 47 Cr) is already inside the current
   SAM/SOM math above, not incremental to it.
4. **Exports** (Rs 5-7 Cr FY27 target, call p4): immaterial to the India
   SAM; kept out of scope per Section 1A.
5. **Managed laboratory / testing-service model** (call p5, "evaluating
   entry... requires significant funding"): would be a new revenue stream
   entirely outside the current SAM definition (a services TAM, not a
   product TAM) if funded — named as optionality, not sized.

### 5E Final output card

- TAM (conservative, 2026, national, product-filtered): **Rs 12,065 Cr**
- TAM (realistic, 2027): **Rs 12,848 Cr**
- SAM: **Rs 1,900 Cr** (10.2% of national all-product TAM Rs 18,624 Cr)
- SOM 3yr (FY29): **Rs 155 Cr** | SOM 5yr (FY31): **Rs 270 Cr** (range
  Rs 261-281 Cr)
- SOM-implied revenue CAGR: **43.4% (yr3) / 38.7% (yr5)**
- Current SAM share: **2.76%**
- Revenue headroom: **36.2x**
- Runway class: **STRONG** (conditional, see 5C)

**Valuation implication line:** At **38.7%** revenue CAGR implied by SOM
(5yr), with margin trajectory of **~28-30% EBITDA** (FY26 actual 28.45%,
management guidance is to at least maintain this while scaling, call p6),
the earnings growth embedded here is roughly **35-40% CAGR** (PAT
plausibly tracking revenue given the operating-leverage story, though
CFO/PAT conversion at 0.675x FY26 (B04 must_track_metrics) is a live
caveat on whether reported PAT growth converts to cash), which **supports**
the current valuation of **~21.4x P/E** (Rs 342.05 CMP, Rs 187.62 Cr
market cap / Rs 8.75 Cr FY26 PAT, screener Data_Sheet.csv, 2026-09-26) —
a PEG of roughly 0.55 on the SOM-implied case. **This supports the
valuation only on the SOM (evidence-based, aggressive-case share-gain)
trajectory, not on management's own FY27/FY28 guidance path, which this
stage's central finding (Section 3B) treats as likely order-driven rather
than durably repeatable.** Stage 11 should carry the SOM-implied CAGR as
the cross-check, per this stage's formal handoff, and treat the guidance
CAGR as management's own case requiring independent verification of
repeat order flow.

## SECTION 6: DOWNSTREAM SIGNAL CANDIDATES

| # | Candidate Signal | Entity Type | Why It Drives Demand | Likely Primary Source | Expected Cadence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Mindray territory/exclusivity decisions for North India | Counterparty | Determines whether Avience's geography filter can expand from its current sub-set toward the full 32% North India figure; also the sole source of 72% of FY26 revenue | Company Reg 30 filings (NSE); Mindray India distributor announcements | Event-driven |
| 2 | State government diagnostic-reagent tender awards (UP, Uttarakhand, and any new named states) | End-customer | Direct source of the order-driven growth this stage flags as distinct from durable share gain | State e-procurement portals (UP eProcurement, Uttarakhand eProcurement); GeM | Event-driven |
| 3 | CDSCO product-licence grants (toward the 175-product FY27 target) | Regulatory | Gates which manufactured SKUs Avience can sell; approval-rate slippage directly caps the manufacturing-mix-shift thesis | CDSCO SUGAM portal / CDSCO licence database | Quarterly |
| 4 | YEIDA plant commissioning and medical-device-park incentive notifications | Regulatory | Gates the Rs 250-265 Cr manufacturing capacity ceiling used in the 3C cross-check | Yamuna Expressway Industrial Development Authority notifications; UP government medical device park circulars | Event-driven |
| 5 | GST Council / CBIC notifications on medical-device and diagnostic-kit rates | Macro | GST 2.0's 5% rate (down from 12-18%) is a live pricing tailwind/margin variable for the entire product range | GST Council press releases; CBIC notifications | Event-driven |
| 6 | PLI Scheme for Pharmaceuticals (IVD category) disbursement/participation updates | Macro | Direct government incentive tied to Avience's own incremental-manufacturing-sales strategy | Department of Pharmaceuticals (Ministry of Chemicals & Fertilizers) scheme disclosures | Event-driven |

SHARED: row 1 (Mindray) is correlated with both the traded-goods revenue
stream and the reagent-rental placement stream (both sit inside the same
non-exclusive relationship) — FTTCP should count it once. Row 3 (CDSCO)
is correlated across all three manufactured lines (reagents, rapid cards,
instruments) — also count once.

## SECTION 2: MENTAL MODEL DECLARATION

**DRAFT - PENDING OPERATOR SIGN-OFF.** This section is a working thesis for
the operator to test on live web and sign in claude.ai. It is not signed.

### PART A — THE FROM STATE (the anchor, not the model)

**A1. ARCHETYPE.** Per business line (B04 business_type: "hybrid";
FLAG-ARCHETYPE):
- Traded reagents, instruments and consumables (72% of FY26 revenue, B04
  revenue_streams): nearest fit is **Outsourcing partner (CDMO/EMS/IT
  services)**, inverted — Avience is a regional channel partner DEPENDENT
  on Mindray as its principal/supplier, the reverse of the archetype's usual
  service-provider framing (B04 analyst_note explicitly names this as a
  partial fit, not a force-fit).
- Manufactured reagents, rapid cards and instruments (28% of FY26 revenue,
  B04 revenue_streams): nearest fit is **Licence/scarcity business** (88
  CDSCO product licences gate what it can sell) layered with elements of
  **Build-to-spec component maker** (design-in via the reagent-rental
  placement lock-in).
- B04 flags this combination (embedded razor-and-blade reagent-rental model
  nested inside a non-exclusive distribution/agency relationship) as a
  **genuine gap in the CLAUDE.md ARCHETYPE LIBRARY** (FLAG-ARCHETYPE), not
  something this dossier resolves by force-fitting.

**A2. THE SIMPLE ANALOGY.** Avience buys diagnostic machines and the
chemicals ("reagents") they run on from a Chinese maker, Mindray, and
resells them across Delhi NCR and eastern UP as Mindray's regional partner;
this trading business is 72% of what Avience sells (B04 revenue_streams).
Separately, Avience makes its own reagents, rapid test cards (for
pregnancy, dengue, HIV) and a few machines under its own CDSCO licences at
a Noida factory (B04, business-narrative.md). For most of its own
machines, Avience gives the device away free on a five-year contract and
earns money back only on the reagents that machine consumes each month, a
model it borrows conceptually from Mindray's own playbook (B04
moats_present, B04 unit_economics). Today, most of the money still comes
from reselling someone else's product, not from Avience's own factory.

### PART B — THE TRANSITION (the model)

**B1. FROM to TO.** One transition line dominates. FROM: a low-pricing-power
**distribution/resale business** riding a single non-exclusive supplier
relationship (nearest QUALITY LADDER rung: **R1 COMMODITY PRICE-TAKER**, on
the traded 72% of revenue — no pricing power, margin set by Mindray's
wholesale terms, B04 pricing_power: "weak"). TO: a **cost-advantaged,
partially spec'd-in manufacturer with a closed-loop reagent-rental
installed base** (claimed destination rung: between **R2 COST-ADVANTAGED
CONVERTER** and **R3 VALUE-ADDED / SPEC'D SUPPLIER** — the reagent-rental
lock-in gives partial switching-cost pricing power on the manufactured
line, bounded by the 5-year contract term, B04 moats_present). Management's
own stated vector is the manufacturing:trading mix moving from ~27:72 to
~50:50 (B00 LBF1; B05 guidance).

**B2. THE ENGINE.** Two things must physically change. (1) The YEIDA plant
must come onstream and scale utilisation from a guided 15-20% in FY27
toward its Rs 250-265 Cr peak manufacturing capacity (B05 guidance; B09
capacity_check), converting owned-manufacturing revenue from ~Rs 14.2 Cr
(FY26, 26.99% of Rs 52.51 Cr) toward the ~Rs 50 Cr a true 50:50 mix at
>Rs 100 Cr would require (B12b F-E findings, confidence.yaml). (2) The
installed base of free-placed instruments under 5-year reagent-rental
contracts must grow, since 80-90% of the closed-loop reagent business
depends on machines Avience itself placed (B04 business-narrative.md;
B07 active_categories B2).

**B3. THE PROOF GATE.** The hard binary observation, quarter by quarter:
the manufacturing-mix percentage of consolidated revenue, disclosed at each
half-yearly result, sustaining a rise past ~35% by FY27-end (B04
must_track_metrics: "red_flag: stuck near 27:72 past FY27"), read together
with the YEIDA CWIP moving to capitalised PPE and commercial production
starting by Oct-2026 per management's own guidance (B04 must_track_metrics;
B05 triggers priority 2). As of this run, CWIP stands at Rs 1,285.28 lakh
with zero capitalised to PPE (B02 top_findings rank 12), and the mix is
still 27:72 (B00 LBF1). The gate has NOT FIRED.

**B4. THE RECOGNITION GAP (open question, resolved at Stage 11).** Whether
the market's current pricing of AVIENCE already reflects the claimed TO
state (a partially manufacturing-led, closed-loop IVD business) or still
prices it as the traded-reseller FROM state is an open question this
dossier does not answer. Stage 11 resolves it via the PE gap against the
Section 1B destination multiple, itself contingent on an unresolved
sector-cap-row question the operator must rule on first (B00
sector_cap_row_evidence; B04 FLAG-VALUATION: Pharma/CDMO 38x versus a
possible Amendment 27.2 Distribution/trading 20x SOTP slice for the 72%
traded revenue). If the TO state is already priced, the re-rating channel
this thesis would otherwise rely on is closed and only earnings growth
would remain; this dossier states no number and no conclusion.

**B5. THE UGLINESS TEST.** Today's ugly optic is cash: consolidated CFO/PAT
below 0.70x for two straight years (0.478x FY25, 0.675x FY26), FCF negative
in all three years (B01 block_b_trend; B03 flags), and receivables aged
over 6 months rising from 10.2% to 38.9% of the standalone book with
provisioning coverage falling from 15.8% to 8.3% over the same period (B02
flags). Classification: **contested, not yet resolvable from corpus alone**.
An ARTIFACT-OF-CLIMB reading holds that this is the plant-build and
working-capital signature of a genuine capex-and-mix transition (YEIDA CWIP
funding, reagent-placement stocking ahead of revenue). A STRUCTURAL-FEATURE
reading holds that the aged-receivables deterioration and the 80.9%
finished-goods inventory build against only 16-22% revenue growth are
independent of the capex story and instead signal collection and
demand-quality problems (B02 red_flags; B03 flags). The gate-recommendation
file (final/gate-recommendation.md) records this same tension as
FLAG-CASH: INDETERMINATE, the single fact this dossier carries forward
unresolved rather than force-classified.

**B6. THE TRANSITION FALSIFIER.** The evidence that would kill the
transition thesis specifically (not the business): the manufacturing:trading
mix still sitting near 27:72 past FY27-end (B04 must_track_metrics), or the
YEIDA plant CWIP remaining uncapitalised and non-commercial past the
Dec-2026 half (B04 must_track_metrics; B05 triggers priority 2 kill_signal),
either of which would mean the reseller engine, not the maker engine, is
what actually grew.

### PART C — WHAT THE MODEL WATCHES

**C1. DOMINANT VARIABLES**, derived from the engine (B2) and proof gate (B3):
1. Manufacturing:trading revenue mix. Current state: ~27:72 as of FY26
   (B00 LBF1); no H1/FY27 print yet.
2. YEIDA plant CWIP-to-PPE capitalisation and commissioning date. Current
   state: Rs 1,285.28 lakh CWIP, zero capitalised, Oct-2026 target
   reaffirmed with an intermediate ~3-month construction slip already
   observed (B02 top_findings rank 12; B05 timeline_slippages).
2b. Reagent-rental installed-base scale (placed-instrument count and
   per-instrument reagent revenue). Current state: NOT FOUND anywhere in
   the corpus (B04 unit_economics; B07 input_gaps) — the single biggest
   disclosure blind spot behind the whole engine.
3. Consolidated CFO/PAT and the aged-receivables share. Current state:
   0.675x FY26 CFO/PAT (below the 0.70x bar); 38.9% standalone aged share,
   provisioning coverage 8.3% (B02 flags; B03 flags).
4. The ~Rs 47 Cr Uttarakhand order's conversion to filed, confirmed revenue.
   Current state: unfiled, unnamed customer, zero Reg 30 corroboration
   (B05 FLAG-UNVERIFIED-ORDER; B09 FLAG-GUIDANCE-VS-SOM).

**C2. WHAT THE MODEL REJECTS.** Total addressable market size is not the
binding constraint: B09's own market sizing puts headroom at ~36x current
revenue with a STRONG runway_class (B09 revenue_headroom_x, runway_class),
and the Rs 250-265 Cr manufacturing peak capacity is "sufficient" against
both SOM and management scenarios through year 5 (B09 capacity_check). The
model therefore rejects sizing questions (how big is the Indian IVD market,
is there room to grow) as noise; the binding constraints are the mix-shift
execution, the working-capital funding gap (Rs 35-40 Cr needed at Rs 100 Cr
revenue, not yet arranged per the call, B04 mgmt_questions), and the
single unverified order, none of which are market-size questions.

**C3. THE BUSINESS FALSIFIER**, distinct from B6: evidence that would force
re-declaring the FROM business itself (not just the transition) — Mindray
narrowing or reallocating the non-exclusive regional channel-partner
territory (B04 first_deterioration_signals; the traded line is 72% of
revenue and rests on one non-exclusive supplier relationship, B04
FLAG-CONCENTRATION), since that would remove the majority of current
revenue independent of whether the manufacturing transition itself is
real.

---

## SECTION 3: BUSINESS UNDERSTANDING NARRATIVE

*(Per prompts/13-synthesis-pipeline.md's shared five-question spec. This is
the Halt 1 draft from B01-B09; Stage 13's copy in
runs/avience-2026-09-26/outputs/final/business-narrative.md is the
authoritative, most current version, reproduced verbatim below since this
run already carries a completed Phase-1-lite synthesis pass.)*

Avience sells the chemicals, test kits and machines that hospitals and labs
use to test blood and other samples, a field called in vitro diagnostics.
Most of what it sells is not its own. In FY26, 60% of turnover came from
reagents and consumables bought from Mindray, a Chinese diagnostics maker,
and resold as Mindray's regional channel partner for Delhi NCR and eastern
UP. Another 13% came from Mindray instruments. Its own Noida factory makes
reagents and consumables (15% of turnover), rapid test cards for pregnancy,
dengue and HIV (9%), and a few analysers and oxygen concentrators (3%),
under 88 CDSCO product licences. A lab cannot run a blood analyser without
the matching reagents, and for 80 to 90% of the reagent business the
machine takes only the reagents it was placed with. So Avience gives away
80 to 85% of its instruments free on five year contracts and earns the
money back on the reagents each machine consumes.

The buyers are hospitals, private labs and diagnostic centres (45% of 10
month FY26 standalone sales), other businesses and distributors (45%),
government (8%) and export (2%). Named accounts include Max Healthcare,
Sarvodaya Hospital and Dr Lal PathLabs. The ten largest customers take more
than half of revenue and are not named. A lab that signs a placement
contract buys that machine's reagents at agreed prices for five years, so
switching is costly inside the term and open again at renewal. Government
buyers pay slowly, 90 to 120 days on large projects by management's own
account.

Present demand comes from a rising load of tests for diabetes, TB, heart
disease and cancer, wider health insurance, and free diagnostics under the
National Health Mission. Two signals govern it for Avience. Mindray
territory and exclusivity decisions for North India decide the traded 72%
of sales. State government diagnostic reagent tender awards in UP,
Uttarakhand and new states decide the order led growth, and the unfiled
Rs 47 Cr Uttarakhand order is the current test.

Forward growth has four external signals. CDSCO product licence grants
toward the 175 product FY27 target gate how many products the factory can
sell, and they show on the CDSCO SUGAM portal. YEIDA plant commissioning
and medical device park incentive notifications gate the new capacity from
October 2026. The PLI Scheme for Pharmaceuticals covers IVD devices, but
Avience is not among the five selected applicants. GST Council and CBIC
notifications on diagnostic kit rates set a live price variable for the
whole range. The conservative market sizing grows the Indian IVD market
about 6.5% a year.

The advantage differs by line. The placed instrument base with its locked
reagent stream carries a moderate switching cost, bounded by the five year
contract term. The 88 licences are a moderate barrier to a new entrant but
do not stop Roche, Abbott or Transasia, who clear the same process at far
larger scale. The traded Mindray line has no moat of its own: the
arrangement is non exclusive and Mindray can reallocate the territory.
Rapid cards have no moat, since many licensed makers sell the same tests.
The emerging moat scan finds no new moat forming, with a score of about 9
to 10 against a threshold of 12 for modest development (verifier-corrected;
B07's own stated 11.4 is superseded per confidence.yaml F-E2). Only three
categories reach moderate strength: backward integration, where China's
share of purchases fell from 31% to 3%; the incremental reagent rental
lock in from the new plant; and a regulatory tailwind that other medical
device parks share equally. The Family I items scored zero.

---

## SECTION 4: DOWNSTREAM DOSSIER

### 4a. Verticals framed

**Vertical 1 — Manufacturing:trading mix shift.** The corpus establishes the
FY26 starting point (26.99% manufacturing / 73.01% trading of Rs 52.51 Cr
revenue, B04 revenue_streams; confidence.yaml F-E1 note) and management's
guided destination (~50:50, B00 LBF1). It cannot establish whether the mix
shift is achievable on the disclosed capacity timeline, because a true 50:50
mix at >Rs 100 Cr needs ~Rs 50 Cr of manufactured revenue against a plant
starting at only 15-20% utilisation, and the flagship Rs 47 Cr order is
itself equipment-heavy and likely Mindray-sourced (B12b MAJOR finding).
Questions that decide it: (1) What share of the Uttarakhand order, if
confirmed, is Avience-manufactured versus Mindray-traded equipment? (2) What
utilisation trajectory does the YEIDA plant actually show in H1/H2 FY27
against the 15-20%-year-1 guide? (3) Does the mix disclosure in the next
half-yearly result move materially past 30%, or stay near 27%?

**Vertical 2 — YEIDA plant commissioning.** The corpus establishes the CWIP
balance (Rs 1,285.28 lakh, zero capitalised, B02 top_findings rank 12), the
RHP's original schedule (building complete Jun-2026, commercial production
Oct-2026, RHP pdf p.130-131), and one already-observed ~3-month slip on the
intermediate construction milestone (B05 timeline_slippages). It cannot
establish whether the Oct-2026 date will hold, since the only close peer
comparable (Tarsons) slipped its own new-plant commissioning date twice in
nine months on a plant that was already substantially built (B06
industry_cross_read, analyst_note). Questions: (1) Does CWIP move to PPE by
the H1/FY27 or H2/FY27 result? (2) Does production start show in a Reg 30
filing or only in prose commentary? (3) What utilisation does the first
disclosed quarter of production show against the 15-20% guide?

**Vertical 3 — Cash conversion and receivables quality.** The corpus
establishes a two-year run of CFO/PAT below 0.70x, FCF negative three years
running, and aged-receivables share nearly quadrupling with provisioning
coverage falling (B02 flags, B03 flags). It cannot establish whether this is
temporary (funding a real capex-and-mix build) or structural (collection
discipline deteriorating independent of the capex story), because no
customer-class split of the aged receivables book exists and no rating
agency working-capital view is available (gate-recommendation.md FLAG-CASH
section; B00 rating gap). Questions: (1) Does the H1/FY27 half-yearly cash
flow statement show CFO turning positive and >=0.70x? (2) Does the aged
share reverse toward FY25's 10-18% or continue past 40%? (3) Is a customer-
class split (government/private/related-party) of the aged book ever
disclosed?

**Vertical 4 — The unverified Uttarakhand order.** The corpus establishes
that management cited a ~Rs 47 Cr order "in hand" twice on the one call
(transcript p.4, p.5, B05 FLAG-UNVERIFIED-ORDER), with no Reg 30 filing,
customer name, or tender reference anywhere in the 18-filing post-listing
list. It cannot establish whether the order exists in the form described, is
smaller, is contingent, or has already lapsed. Questions: (1) Does any Reg
30 filing name the counterparty or tender reference? (2) Does the H1/FY27
revenue line show a Uttarakhand or government-linked component consistent
with the order's stated size? (3) Why did the CMD's own qualified remark
("once confirmed orders are in place, banks... can support," B12b finding)
sit alongside the "in hand" framing on the same call?

### 4b. Candidate signal table

*(Expanding B09's downstream_candidates; falsifiers and cadence are DRAFTS,
unverified, for Role 5.5 in claude.ai to confirm and write to the tracker.)*

| Candidate Signal | Draft Falsifier | Draft Cadence | Likely Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Mindray territory/exclusivity decisions for North India | Territory narrowed, reallocated, or made non-exclusive-in-fact via a competing regional partner named publicly | Event-Driven | Company Reg 30 filings (NSE); Mindray India distributor announcements (Downstream_Source_Discovery_Protocol_v1_0) |
| State government diagnostic-reagent tender awards (UP, Uttarakhand, new states) | No Uttarakhand/government-linked revenue line appears in H1/FY27 results | Event-Driven | State e-procurement portals (UP/Uttarakhand eProcurement); GeM |
| CDSCO product-licence grants toward the 175-product FY27 target | Licence count stalls materially below ~120 by FY27-end | Quarterly | CDSCO SUGAM portal / licence database |

| PLI Scheme for Pharmaceuticals (IVD category) disbursement/participation updates | Avience remains outside the five selected applicants through FY27 | Event-Driven | Department of Pharmaceuticals scheme disclosures |
| Reagent-rental installed-base scale (placed-instrument count, per-instrument reagent revenue) | No disclosure of placement count or per-unit reagent revenue appears in the next AR or deck, keeping the core engine unmeasurable | Event-Driven / Annual | Investor deck; AR; direct management question |
| Note 40 profit-split labelling correction | FY27 AR's equivalent note repeats an internal-consistency error against the parent's/subsidiary's own standalone P&Ls | Annual | Next AR Additional Information note |

### 4c. Fragility read

- **variable_count**: 6 — (1) manufacturing:trading mix shift, (2) YEIDA
  plant commissioning/utilisation, (3) reagent-rental installed-base scale
  (undisclosed), (4) the Uttarakhand order's conversion, (5) cash
  conversion/receivables quality, (6) Mindray channel-partner continuity.
- **verifiability_ratio**: "2 of 6 externally observable" — CDSCO licence
  grants and Reg 30/GeM tender filings are externally checkable; the mix
  shift, plant utilisation, installed-base scale, and cash-conversion trend
  are all company-narrated or company-filed-only, with no independent
  corroboration source identified in the corpus (B06's peer coverage
  reaches adjacent sector questions, never Avience's own figures directly).
- **single_point_failure**: "none - failure requires conjunction" for the
  overall transition thesis, BUT the FY27 guidance specifically has a named
  single point of failure: the ~Rs 47 Cr Uttarakhand order, since B05 states
  it is "the single largest quantified input behind the FY27 >=60%/>Rs 100
  Cr guidance" with "zero independent corroboration" (B05 FLAG-UNVERIFIED-
  ORDER; B09 FLAG-GUIDANCE-VS-SOM).
- **fragility_verdict**: **FRAGILE** — six variables, only two externally
  verifiable, and the near-term guidance print rests on one unverified,
  company-narrated order.

## SECTION 5: PLAIN-LANGUAGE SUMMARY

1. Avience makes and sells diagnostic reagents, test kits and machines used
   to test blood and other samples (B04).
2. Most of what it sells today is not its own: 72% of FY26 revenue is
   Mindray products bought and resold as Mindray's regional partner (B04).
3. Its own Noida factory makes reagents, rapid test cards, and a few
   machines under 88 government licences, the remaining 28% of revenue (B04).
4. Buyers are hospitals, labs and diagnostic centres (45%), other
   distributors (45%), government (8%) and export (2%), with the ten
   largest customers over half of revenue but unnamed (B04 business-
   narrative.md).
5. Demand grows because more people are tested for diabetes, TB, heart
   disease and cancer, and government free-diagnostics programmes are
   expanding (B04, B09).
6. Near-term growth also rides one specific, unverified order in
   Uttarakhand worth about Rs 47 Cr, which has no exchange filing behind it
   (B05, B09).
7. The moat sits in the reagent-rental lock-in: machines placed free run
   only on Avience's or Mindray's own reagents for five years (B04, B07).
8. The moat does not sit in the traded Mindray line: the partnership is
   non-exclusive and Mindray could reallocate the territory (B04).
9. The mental model is a transition bet: from a low-margin reseller toward
   a manufacturer with a locked-in reagent base, tested by whether the
   manufacturing share of revenue actually moves from 27% toward 50% (B00,
   B04, this dossier Section 2).
10. The fragility read on that bet is FRAGILE: six variables decide it, only
    two are checkable outside the company's own filings, and the near-term
    guidance leans on one unverified order (Section 4c).
11. The corpus could not establish how many instruments are currently
    placed under rental contracts, or how much reagent revenue each one
    generates (B04, B07).
12. The corpus could not establish why FY26 exports collapsed 90% from the
    prior year, since no analyst asked and no document explains it (B02,
    B05, B07).
13. Cash has not kept pace with profit: operating cash flow covered only
    48% to 68% of profit over two years, and free cash flow was negative
    all three years (B01, B03).
14. Receivables older than six months nearly quadrupled as a share of the
    book in one year, while the reserve set aside against them fell (B02).
15. The single biggest open question is whether the Rs 47 Cr order and the
    new factory's October 2026 start date are real on the stated timeline,
    since the nearest comparable peer slipped its own new-plant date twice
    in nine months (B05, B06).

---

## SECTION 6: STANDING EXTRACTION ANNEX

### 1. UNITS

No per-unit figure (revenue per placed instrument, reagent run-rate per
device, ARPU) is printed anywhere in the corpus. Quote (transcript,
paraphrase of management's own framing at the qualitative level, no rupee
figure given): management describes reagent-rental margins in a range
("40-60%" reagent-rental, "30-70%" manufacturing by product) without any
per-unit revenue or volume figure (transcript p.6-7, cited at B04
unit_economics). The business model report states directly: "Revenue per
unit | NOT FOUND at the per-instrument level (no average contract value or
reagent run-rate per placed unit disclosed anywhere in the corpus)" (04-
bizmodel.md line 184). Comment: no volume (placed-instrument count) or
per-unit revenue line exists from which one could be derived either; only
aggregate revenue-stream percentages (B04 revenue_streams) are available.
NOT DISCLOSED, reason: management gives only qualitative margin ranges,
never per-instrument economics, on the one call held to date.

### 2. SEGMENT CAPITAL AND DEBT

NOT DISCLOSED as a formal Ind AS 108 segment note. The AR's MD&A carries
"no segment analysis, no standalone-vs-subsidiary breakdown, no
manufacturing-vs-trading mix discussion" (03-ardeep.md line 183, describing
AR pdf p.63-64 Business Performance section). The closest analogue is Note
40 (consolidated, Additional Information, AR pdf p.143-144), which splits
FY26 consolidated PROFIT (not segment assets/liabilities/capital employed)
between "Holding Company" and "Subsidiary": as printed, Holding Company
24%/Rs.205.91 lakh and Subsidiary 76%/Rs.669.38 lakh; independently
re-verified by three stages (B02, B03, B08) as TRANSPOSED — the Holding
Company actually generated ~76-77% (Rs.673.33 lakh standalone PAT, Note 4
p.89) and the subsidiary ~23-24% (Rs.203.9966 lakh AOC-1 PAT, p.56). The
net-asset side of Note 40 shows the subsidiary's share shrinking from 28%
(FY25) to 21% (FY26), not part of the same labelling error (B02
analyst_note). Borrowings: SIDBI YEIDA loan balance grew Rs 352.50 lakh in
FY26 with no separate interest line in the Finance Cost note, implying full
capitalisation into CWIP, amount NOT DISCLOSED (B02 top_findings rank 7,
Note 28 standalone p.100 / Note 30 consolidated p.138). Total borrowings:
Rs 28.93 Cr (B00 LBF4, screener). Comment: capital employed and borrowings
are disclosed only at whole-company level, standalone and consolidated;
no line splits them by manufacturing versus trading or by legal entity
beyond the Note 40 profit table.

### 3. GUIDANCE VERSUS ASPIRATION

Classified per the H2/FY26 call (24-Jul-2026) and the FY26 AR, from B05's
own guidance table and 05-concall.md:
(a) GUIDANCE WITH A PERIOD: "FY27 revenue growth >=60%, may be higher"
(transcript p.3); "FY27 revenue absolute > Rs 100 Cr" (transcript p.5);
"YEIDA plant utilisation ~15-20%... FY27" (transcript p.6); "Manufacturing:
trading mix target ~50:50 (from ~27:72)... this year (FY27)" (transcript
p.6-7); "Exports ~Rs 5-7 Cr... FY27" (transcript p.4); "Product count ~175
products... end FY27" (transcript p.4-5).
(b) ASPIRATION WITHOUT A FIRM PERIOD (medium-term, not this-year committed):
"FY28 revenue aspiration ~Rs 160-165 Cr" (transcript p.5) — 05-concall.md
line 55 labels this explicitly "~₹160-165 Cr aspiration | FY28"; "Margin:
maintain FY27, improve FY28/FY29 via scale/operating leverage" (05-
concall.md line 43: "Committed (maintain) / aspirational (improve)").
(c) CAPACITY OR CAPABILITY ONLY: "YEIDA plant peak capacity ~Rs 250-265 Cr"
(transcript p.5-6), a ceiling, not a near-term target. Comment: the
flagship FY27 revenue guidance (>Rs 100 Cr) is stated with a period and
therefore counts as guidance, not aspiration, even though roughly half of
it (the Rs 47 Cr Uttarakhand order) is itself unverified (Section 4e Chain
1); the classification concerns the STATEMENT'S form, not its reliability.

### 4. CONCENTRATION

Product: reagents/consumables and instruments dominate; exact product-level

Run: avience-2026-09-26 | Verification date: 2026-09-26 | Model: claude-haiku-4-5

## VERIFICATION METHODOLOGY

Materiality hierarchy (order of priority):
1. Verdict-card figures (Gate 0 classification score, Block totals, final score) — CRITICAL severity any MISMATCH
2. Section 1B pillar inputs (ROCE, cash conversion basis, margin figures) — CRITICAL severity any MISMATCH
3. Scorecard input figures (Block A-F row inputs, table cells) — MAJOR severity any MISMATCH
4. Supporting footnote data, subsidiary figures, cross-checks — MINOR severity any MISMATCH

Coverage scope: Phase 1 reports are Gate 0 scoring (Block A-F inputs) and their source anchors (screener, revised results, AR notes). Later-stage reports (B02-B09) contain extensive narrative and detailed findings; numbers are secondary to qualitative analysis in those stages, so verification effort focuses on the Gate 0 scorecard's numerical foundation, which drives the downstream thesis.

---

# FILE: `runs/avience-2026-09-26/outputs/reports/12b-verifier-redflags.md`

Source: `run/avience-2026-09-26`

# VERIFIER B: CONCALL RED FLAGS, AVIENCE (phase 1, run 2026-09-26)

Model: claude-opus-5-5. Mode: NO-CONCALL MODE (manifest `concalls_available: false`).
Following the orchestrator's NO-CONCALL MODE section, the communication analysis is audited against the AR, the results filings, the RHP and the announcements, as well as the company's one transcript.

Sources read fresh for this audit (`[page N]` = PDF page):
- Company transcript: `inputs/concalls/Concall_Jul_2026_Transcript.txt` (H2/FY26 call, 24-Jul-2026, filed 28-Jul-2026). This was read in full.
- Results: original audited results OCR 27-Jul-2026; revised consolidated results 25-Aug-2026 (`inputs/results/`); 17-Jul-2026 cash-flow extract.
- AR FY2026: Board's Report (p.39-50), MD&A Annexure-4 (p.63-66), Notes 13/14, CARO Annexure 1.
- RHP Jun-2026: Objects of the Issue and Schedule of Implementation (p.125-131), state-wise revenue (p.160), SWOT (p.210).
- Announcements folder: all 10 files were scanned for any order or tender filing. None exists.
- Peer transcripts: QLINE Jun-2026 and TARSONS Feb/Aug-2026 were read at the passages that bear on the claims. MOLBIO Sep-2026 and TARSONS Nov-2025/Jun-2026 were checked only at the anchors B06 cites.
- Pipeline artifacts under audit: `outputs/reports/05-concall.md`, `outputs/blocks/B05-concall.yaml`, `outputs/reports/06-peers.md`.

Structural limit: there is only one company transcript. Tone shifts between quarters and repeated evasions (2+ quarters) cannot be observed, so a CRITICAL cannot come from the repeated-evasion test. Contradictions were tested across documents instead: RHP (Jun) vs call (Jul) vs revised results (Aug) vs AR (Sep).

---

## 1. INDEPENDENT RED-FLAG LIST (my read, before comparison)

| # | Item | Anchor | Severity |
|---|---|---|---|
| I1 | The CMD says twice that a ~Rs 47 Cr order is "in hand". He names no customer and no tender. No Reg 30 filing exists. The order is about 90% of FY26 revenue (Rs 52.51 Cr). | Transcript p.3, p.5 (Choudhary); announcements folder (no order filing) | MAJOR |
| I2 | The call contradicts itself on that order. It is "in hand" (p.3, p.5). Yet on working capital: "once confirmed orders are in place, banks and financial institutions can support" (p.6). The AR Board's Report sec.4, signed 07-Sep-2026, says "no material changes and commitments affecting the financial position" apart from the IPO. | Transcript p.6 (Choudhary to Manav Kothari); AR p.39-40 | MAJOR |
| I3 | The call was held for H2 FY26. That half grew revenue +5.0% YoY (Rs 2,568.65 vs Rs 2,447.04 lakh) and fell -4.2% against H1 (Rs 2,681.93 lakh). H2 PAT fell -1.3% YoY (Rs 421.50 vs Rs 426.89 lakh). Management cited full-year figures only, and the CFO called the year "consistent improvement". Full-year revenue growth (16.07%) was never stated as a percentage. No analyst asked. The >=60% FY27 guidance sits on this slowing base. | Revised consolidated results 25-Aug-2026 p.11 (Revenue line, PAT line); transcript p.4 (Saurabh Verma); AR p.39 (16.07%) | MAJOR |
| I4 | FY27 export guidance of Rs 5-7 Cr is framed without the FY26 base. That base fell from Rs 524.30 lakh to Rs 53.11 lakh (-89.9%). | Transcript p.3-4; AR Note 39(b) FOB table (line 4376-4377); AR Board's Report forex earnings (line 2169) | MAJOR |
| I5 | The RHP and the call disagree on working capital. The RHP working-capital plan (auditor-certified 28-May-2026) assumes FY27 receivable days fall to 80 from 128, a requirement of Rs 2,049.39 lakh, and IPO working capital of Rs 500 lakh. Eight weeks later the call cites 90-120 days on large government projects and does not dispute a Rs 35-40 Cr need. The CMD concedes "the entire requirement is not available today", and the bank lines are unsanctioned. [INFERENCE] The RHP receivables imply FY27 standalone revenue of ~Rs 60 Cr (1,317.50 x 365 / 80). That is below the call's trajectory. | RHP pdf p.125-126 (lines 6971-7008); transcript p.6 | MAJOR |
| I6 | Management says the equipment-heavy Rs 47-50 Cr order will carry a *higher* contribution margin than the reagent business (40-60%). Peer QLINE reports instrument-manufacturing gross margin of 15-20% and domestic traded-instrument margin of 10-15%, and says large instruments are placed at cost. Avience itself says 80-85% of its instruments earn no direct revenue. A peer statement directly contradicts the main company. | Transcript p.7 (Ashwani Agarwal Q, Choudhary A; Choudhary on reagent rental); QLINE 23-Jun-2026 p.12 (Meenal Gupta), p.17 (Ajay Mahanty), p.19 (Y.S. Prabhakara) | MAJOR |
| I7 | The FY27 guidance does not add up internally. A 50:50 mix at >Rs 100 Cr needs ~Rs 50 Cr of manufactured revenue. FY26 manufactured revenue was ~Rs 14.2 Cr (26.99% x Rs 52.51 Cr). The new plant starts only in Oct-2026 at 15-20% utilisation. The order that carries about half of FY27 is equipment-heavy, and high-end equipment is traded (Mindray). [INFERENCE] The mix lands nearer 33-40% unless the order is manufactured goods. Management was not asked to reconcile this. | Transcript p.4 (Mindray sourcing), p.6 (50:50 target; ~Rs 250 Cr capacity), p.7 (26.99%; 15-20%; equipment-heavy order) | MAJOR |
| I8 | Two headline numbers are never reconciled. >=60% growth means ~Rs 84 Cr. >Rs 100 Cr means ~+90%. Rs 52.51 Cr plus Rs 47 Cr is ~Rs 99.5 Cr, so the Rs 100 Cr figure needs a flat base plus the full order in-year. | Transcript p.3, p.5 | MINOR |
| I9 | Consolidated results were revised on 25-Aug-2026, a month after the call. Note 8 says Excel workbook links were removed, so inter-company balances of Rs 337.55 lakh were not eliminated. There is no P&L impact. The AR MD&A sec.6 then asserts that controls "ensure accuracy and reliability of financial reporting". Together with CARO ii(b) and Note 40, this makes three reporting-control errors in the first filing cycle. | Revised CFS p.1 item 3, Note 8 (lines 744-751), auditor para 3 (p.9-10); AR p.65 | MINOR |
| I10 | The Board's Report says the auditor's report has "no qualification, reservation, adverse remark". CARO Annexure 1 lists clause ii(b) against the Holding Company. The note calls the difference "not material". | AR p.50 (line 1494); CARO Annexure 1 (line 4881-4889); Note 47 | MINOR |
| I11 | The RHP set building completion for Jun-2026. The call says "balance work... by September". The Oct-2026 production date is unchanged. | RHP p.131 (line 7285-7289); transcript p.3 | MINOR |
| I12 | The AR MD&A, signed six weeks after the call, has no quantified outlook. It does not mention the order, the growth target or the YEIDA plant. The statutory document omits every number the call gave. | AR Annexure-4 p.63-66 | MINOR |
| I13 | The CMD volunteered the share price on a results call: issue Rs 208, traded ~Rs 414.95, "nearly double". | Transcript p.4 | MINOR |
| I14 | Three soft dodges. (a) The approval success rate was asked and not answered (Praneeth, p.5). (b) FY28 utilisation was asked and the FY27 figure was given (Manav Kothari, p.7). (c) The revenue breakup was deferred offline (p.5) and partly supplied later (p.7). | Transcript p.5, p.7 | MINOR |

### Pipeline red flags I did not raise independently
None. I found every B05 red flag (two MAJOR, one MINOR, one WATCH) on my own read. B06 raises no flags. Checks on the pipeline flags:
- FLAG-UNVERIFIED-ORDER: SUPPORTED. No order filing exists in the announcements folder, and the AR has no order reference.
- FLAG-SILENT-BASE: SUPPORTED. AR Note 39(b) shows Rs 53.11 vs Rs 524.30 lakh, and the transcript never mentions the base.
- FLAG-DISCLOSURE: SUPPORTED. The CARO Annexure 1 table lists ii(b) against the Holding Company. The MINOR grade is right because the differences are "not material" (Note 47).
- WATCH slippage: SUPPORTED. RHP line 7285 vs transcript p.3.

pipeline_flags_not_supported: none.

### Pipeline statements that overreach (not red flags, logged as MINOR)
- B05 2C says management "answered every question asked (no visible deflection)". NOT SUPPORTED; see I14.
- B05 2A row 2 calls FY26 CWIP additions (Rs 823.37 lakh) "directionally consistent" with the net-proceeds tranche (Rs 1,595.53 lakh, up to FY2026-27). The IPO closed in Jun-2026, which is FY27. FY26 spend was funded before the IPO and cannot evidence use of the proceeds. This is a period mismatch.
- The B06 Q2 table describes QLINE receivables as "~134 trending toward 90-120" in one cell and "120-150-day range" in another. The transcript supports both figures (134 for FY26 at p.12; "currently 120 to 150"; target "within 90 to 120" at p.13). The cells need reconciling. Cosmetic.

## 4. CREDIBILITY GRADE

B05 grade: C. **Concur.** NO-CONCALL MODE sets C as the default, and no guidance has reached a test date. Two items I found (I2, I3) push toward the low end of C, not toward a different grade. First, the call's own wording undercuts "order in hand". Second, management framed a half with +5.0% revenue and -1.3% PAT as "consistent improvement". Neither is a delivery failure yet. Both are framing failures, and the H1 FY27 print should be read with them in mind.
